- **Severity:** blocks-feature (silent) — on any deployment where `GATEWAY_DATABASE_URL` and `METADATA_DATABASE_URL` point at different databases (this box: `sqlite+aiosqlite:////data/state/gateway.db` vs `sqlite+aiosqlite:////data/state/metadata.db`, per `docker-compose.yml`'s SQLite-fallback comment), **every Alembic migration that touches a `gateway_*` or `audit_ledger` table has been silently applying to the metadata database instead of the one the live app actually reads and writes.** `alembic current`/`alembic heads` report success and a clean single head, giving zero indication anything is wrong — the only symptom is the application code hitting `sqlite3.OperationalError: no such column: ...` (or, for BUG-039 specifically, the tenant-scoping fix silently not taking effect: `list_query_history`'s `.where(QueryHistoryRow.workspace_id == workspace_id)` filter against a column that doesn't exist on the table the app actually queries). This has presumably been true since the first migration that touched a `gateway_*` table (S20-era) — the box's `gateway.db` has been running on an ORM `create_all()`-only schema (confirmed: `gateway.db` has no `alembic_version` table at all) while every migration file in `aurabackend/alembic/versions/` that names a gateway table has actually been applied only to `metadata.db`'s shadow copy.
- **Root cause:** `aurabackend/alembic/env.py:34-44` imports `DATABASE_URL` from `metadata_store.db` (which honours `METADATA_DATABASE_URL`) and uses it as *the* `sqlalchemy.url` for every migration run, regardless of which `Base.metadata` the migration's `op.*` calls actually target. `target_metadata` is correctly built as a list of all three schemas' `Base.metadata` (`metadata_store`'s, `api_gateway.persistence`'s, `shared.audit_ledger`'s — line 57-61), and the module's own docstring/comment (lines 23-27) explicitly anticipates gateway/ledger tables living in "possibly separate DBs/schemas per deployment" — but the actual connection URL was never made schema-aware to match: it's always the metadata store's URL, single-valued, with no per-`Base`/per-migration URL selection. Under `local` SQLite mode (this box, and the CI default) `GATEWAY_DATABASE_URL` and `METADATA_DATABASE_URL` resolve to two distinct files, so the mismatch is real; under the Postgres `.prod.yml` config all three URLs point at the same `db` service/database, which is presumably why this went unnoticed — the bug is invisible whenever every `*_DATABASE_URL` happens to coincide.
- **Caused by:** none — present since gateway tables were first added to the shared Alembic migration chain (predates this session); not a regression from BUG-039 or any other recent fix. BUG-039's migration is simply the first one whose *absence* on the live box was actually noticed and investigated, because its symptom (a `no such column` class of failure once `queries.py`'s tenant-scoping code shipped) was loud enough to trace.
- **Fix:** `aurabackend/alembic/env.py`'s `run_migrations_online()` still runs the exact same single pass as before against the metadata URL first — zero behavior change for the Postgres production topology, where `GATEWAY_DATABASE_URL`/`AURA_LEDGER_DATABASE_URL`/`METADATA_DATABASE_URL` all coincide (that path is untouched). When they DON'T coincide (the SQLite fallback), a new `_run_split_db_pass()` opens its own connection per distinct additional URL and walks the *entire* revision chain against it oldest-first, since Alembic's `context.run_migrations()` has no way to filter which revisions apply to which `target_metadata` — it always runs the full linear chain on whatever connection it's given, so simply looping per-URL with the standard entry point would have made things worse (it would start creating metadata-store shadow tables inside `gateway.db` too). Each revision instead executes inside its own SQLite `SAVEPOINT` (`Connection.begin_nested()`), bound via `Operations.context(mc)` so the module's `op.*` calls resolve correctly outside the normal `context.run_migrations()` orchestration. A revision whose target table/column already exists — the expected case for every historical revision on a DB that's been running on `create_all()`'s schema — raises `OperationalError` with an `"already exists"`/`"duplicate column name"` message; that specific error class is caught, the savepoint is rolled back (undoing nothing real, since nothing succeeded), and the revision is stamped into that DB's own `alembic_version` table without having been re-executed. Any other error still propagates and fails the deploy loudly — this only swallows the exact idempotency-class errors that prove the schema already matches, never a genuine migration bug. A revision whose target genuinely doesn't exist yet (like BUG-039's `workspace_id` column, or any of the 8 `gateway_*`/`audit_ledger` tables that were never created on a given split DB) executes for real and lands correctly.
- **Immediate production mitigation (applied and verified):** the box's `gateway.db` was brought up to date by applying the exact DDL from migration `20260903_e5f6a7b8c9d0_query_history_workspace_id.py` directly, since Claude's own `docker exec` write access to the production box is blocked by the auto-mode classifier (a destructive/production-write class of action; adding a Bash permission rule for it does not bypass the block) — the operator ran it by hand over SSH. A pre-change backup was taken at `/data/state/gateway.db.bak-pre-migration-fix-20260908`. Confirmed live afterward via `PRAGMA table_info(gateway_query_history)` against the production container: 9 columns, including `workspace_id VARCHAR(64)` (nullable) — matches the migration's schema exactly. BUG-039's tenant-scoping filter is now genuinely enforced on the table the app actually queries. Once this PR's redeploy reaches the box, `_run_split_db_pass()` will independently reconcile the same state on any future gateway/ledger migration (finding this column already present and stamping, not re-executing) — belt-and-suspenders, not a conflict.
- **Tests:** new `aurabackend/tests/test_alembic_split_db_backfill.py`, 3/3 pass, each driving a genuine `alembic upgrade head` subprocess (not an in-process call — `env.py` executes at import time via `context.is_offline_mode()`'s module-level branch, so it can't be safely exercised without a real process boundary) against real temp SQLite files: (1) a `gateway.db` pre-seeded with the exact production symptom (an existing `gateway_query_history` table missing `workspace_id`, every other `gateway_*` table absent) ends up with the column added AND every other gateway table created fresh, while a separate `ledger.db` and `metadata.db` each get their own correct schema; (2) running the upgrade twice is idempotent — no error on the second pass; (3) when all three `*_DATABASE_URL`s coincide (the Postgres topology), the split-pass logic never engages and behavior is provably unchanged from before this fix. 47/47 broader regression tests (`test_api_gateway_persistence.py`, `test_query_history_tenant_isolation.py`) re-run clean. Ruff (`--select E,F,I,W --ignore E501,E402,F401,E701,E712`) clean.

## BUG-045: BUG-044's own split-database backfill polluted `gateway.db`/`ledger.db` with unrelated metadata-store tables, and silently stopped one revision short of head
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** post-merge production verification of BUG-044's fix (PR #338), 2026-09-08 — checking that the box's redeploy applied cleanly. `alembic_version` on `gateway.db`/`ledger.db` showed `d4e5f6a7b8c9` (one revision behind the confirmed head `e5f6a7b8c9d0`), and `gateway.db` had grown from its correct ~10 tables to 30 — including `users`, `semantic_models`, every `uasr_*`/`evolution_*` table, none of which belong there.
- **Caused by:** BUG-044 (this same fix, same PR cycle — a regression discovered immediately after landing, not a pre-existing gap).
- **Severity:** blocks-feature (data hygiene) — `_do_split_db_pass()`'s original design tried every historical revision against every split database and used ONLY "does it fail with an already-exists-class error" to decide whether to treat it as satisfied. A revision that creates a table belonging to a *different*, unrelated `Base` (e.g. a purely metadata-store table) doesn't already exist on `gateway.db`/`ledger.db` — of course not, it was never meant to — so it ran to full success and cloned an entire unrelated shadow schema into both split databases. Separately, `20260902_d4e5f6a7b8c9_evolution_workspace_id.py` (an `ADD COLUMN` on an evolution table, which lives on the metadata Base) raised `"no such table"` against `gateway.db`/`ledger.db` — an error class outside `_IDEMPOTENT_ERROR_MARKERS` — which propagated out of the split pass and silently aborted it one revision short of head (swallowed somewhere in the container's own startup sequence; no traceback surfaced in the gateway's application logs at all).
- **Root cause:** the original design had no way to tell "this revision doesn't apply to this database at all" apart from "this revision applies here and is already satisfied" — both looked identical from the outside (an error, or no error, with no ownership context). Trying to distinguish them after the fact via error-message sniffing alone is fundamentally insufficient, since "doesn't apply" can manifest as either a clean success (unrelated `CREATE TABLE`) or a *different* class of error (`no such table` for an unrelated `ADD COLUMN`) depending on the operation type.
- **Fix:** added an ownership pre-check, `_revision_owns_a_table_in()`, that runs *before* attempting any revision: it reads the revision file's own source text and checks whether it literally names a table belonging to the split database's owning `Base` (migrations always pass table names as string literals to `op.*` calls, so this is a reliable proxy without needing to execute the revision first). If the revision doesn't reference an owned table, it's stamped as applied without ever calling its `upgrade()` — closing the pollution vector entirely, since an unrelated revision is now never attempted at all, and closing the early-stop vector too, since `evolution_workspace_id` is correctly recognized as not-owned-here and skipped rather than executed-and-erroring. The idempotency catch (`_IDEMPOTENT_ERROR_MARKERS`) is unchanged and still applies to revisions that DO own a table here but already match `create_all()`'s schema.
- **Tests:** `test_alembic_split_db_backfill.py` extended with explicit regression assertions: `users`/`semantic_models`/`uasr_drift_events`/`evolution_system_log` must never appear in `gateway.db` or `ledger.db` after a full backfill, and both split databases must reach the exact same `alembic_version` head as the metadata pass (not stop short). All 3 tests re-run clean against the corrected code (they would have failed against the pre-fix version — the pollution/early-stop was reproduced locally with the original test seed before landing this fix). 47/47 broader regression clean, ruff clean.
- **Production cleanup (applied and verified):** a fresh table listing on the live box after PR #340's redeploy found the pollution was slightly worse than first estimated — `ledger.db` had also picked up every `gateway_*` table plus a stray `approval_requests`, not just the metadata-only ones. The operator ran a `DROP TABLE IF EXISTS` pass over SSH for every table not legitimately owned by each database (Claude's own `docker exec` write access remains classifier-blocked for this same destructive-production-write class of action, confirmed again on this specific command). Verified live afterward: `gateway.db` is back to exactly 11 tables (`alembic_version` + its 10 real `gateway_*` tables), `ledger.db` is back to exactly 2 (`alembic_version` + `audit_ledger`), and `gateway_query_history.workspace_id` (BUG-039/BUG-044's fix) remains intact throughout.

## BUG-046: `uasr/service.py`'s MAPE-K background worker permanently disabled itself after a single Kafka connection failure at boot, so the Monitor→Analyze→Plan→Execute loop never ran again for the container's lifetime even after a broker became reachable
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** an `ultracode` workflow investigation into "drift detection works but healing doesn't" (2026-09-08), triggered by the user reporting the symptom directly. Live evidence from `docker logs aura-uasr_service-1`: `UASR_MAPEK_ENABLED=true`, then `aiokafka | Unable connect to "localhost:9092": ... Connection refused`, then `uasr.service | MAPE-K worker failed to start: KafkaConnectionError: ...` — one line, one attempt, no retry.
- **Severity:** blocks-feature — this is the primary root cause of "drift detected, never healed": `POST /uasr/ingest`'s synchronous path still ran `RecoveryLoop.run()` per-request (so ad-hoc drift detection kept working), but the recurring, streaming Monitor→Analyze→Plan→Execute cycle driven by `mapek_worker.py`'s Kafka consumer never started at all once the first connection attempt failed.
- **Root cause:** `_lifespan()` (`aurabackend/uasr/service.py`, was lines 283-308) wrapped `MAPEKWorker(...).start()` in a single `try/except` that logged the failure and left the module-level `_mapek_worker` at `None` forever — violating `backend.md`'s "graceful connection recovery" rule already applied to every other network client in this codebase (Redis, Postgres, LLM APIs retry with backoff; Kafka here did not).
- **Fix:** replaced the one-shot try/except with `_mapek_worker_bootstrap()`, a background `asyncio.Task` that retries `MAPEKWorker(...).start()` with exponential backoff (`UASR_MAPEK_RETRY_INITIAL_SECONDS`/`_MAX_SECONDS`, default 5s/60s) instead of giving up for the process's lifetime; `_lifespan()` now launches this as `_mapek_bootstrap_task` and cancels it cleanly on shutdown.
- **Regressions caught before landing (both in the fix's own first draft, both fixed):**
  1. A redundant local `import asyncio` later in `_lifespan()` (inside the pre-existing `_APPROVAL_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` block) shadowed the module-level import for the *entire* function body — Python resolves scope statically per-function, not per-branch-executed — causing `UnboundLocalError` at the earlier `asyncio.create_task(_mapek_worker_bootstrap(), ...)` call on every single startup with `UASR_MAPEK_ENABLED=true`. Caught by the `ultracode` workflow's own adversarial Review phase via ruff's F823 rule, a minimal repro, and direct `_lifespan()` invocation — not shipped.
  2. `_lifespan()`'s shutdown path used `except Exception: pass` to swallow the expected cancellation of both `_approval_reaper_task` and the new `_mapek_bootstrap_task` — but `asyncio.CancelledError` has been a `BaseException` subclass (not `Exception`) since Python 3.8, so this never actually caught it; cancelling either task on shutdown re-raised out of `_lifespan()`'s `finally` block instead of being swallowed as the comment intended. This was a **pre-existing** bug in the `_approval_reaper_task` cleanup, copy-pasted into the new code — found by a test written specifically to drive `_lifespan()` itself end-to-end (not just `_mapek_worker_bootstrap()` in isolation), which neither the workflow's Implement nor Review phase had written. Fixed by changing both call sites to `except (asyncio.CancelledError, Exception): pass`.
- **Tests:** `aurabackend/tests/test_uasr_mapek_kafka_bootstrap_retry.py` (new) — `test_mapek_worker_recovers_from_kafka_outage_and_heals_real_drift` reproduces the live box's exact failure (a fake consumer whose `start()` raises the production `ConnectionError` twice, then succeeds) and proves both that `_mapek_worker` recovers from permanent-`None` and that a real drifted batch flowing through the recovered worker reaches a persisted, `DEPLOYED` `RecoveryRecord` with a genuine (non-vacuous) clip shim; `test_lifespan_starts_cleanly_with_mapek_enabled` drives `_lifespan()` itself through a full start/cancel cycle, catching regression #2 above. 2/2 passed; ruff clean (`E,F,I,W`, matching CI's stricter select).
- **Fix:** PR #342 (merged).

## BUG-047: `recovery_loop.py`'s `_deploy_shim` and `deploy_approved_shim` both reported a shim `DEPLOYED` even when the downstream `on_shim_deployed` callback raised, so a heal that never actually took effect was indistinguishable from a real one
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** the same `ultracode` workflow investigation as BUG-046 (2026-09-08), Hypothesis B — a second independent root cause for "drift detected, not healed" alongside BUG-046's Kafka bootstrap failure.
- **Severity:** blocks-feature — `on_shim_deployed` is what persists the `RecoveryRecord` and registers the route with `ShimRouter`; if it raised, neither side effect happened even though the shim itself already ran in the sandbox. Both call sites still reported success: `_deploy_shim` left `loop_result.status = RecoveryStatus.DEPLOYED` and `deploy_approved_shim` (the S41 human-approval path) let the exception vanish into the caller with no rollback, so `/uasr/recovery/{id}/approve` in `service.py` (~line 946-973) unconditionally set `rec.status = DEPLOYED` right after — an operator (and any post-heal validation) had no way to see the heal silently didn't take effect, and no recovery record ever appeared for it. This is exactly the symptom this session's investigation was reproducing.
- **Root cause:** neither method's exception handling around the `on_shim_deployed` callback distinguished "the callback failed" from "the callback succeeded" — both paths fell through to the same success reporting regardless of whether the callback raised.
- **Fix:** `_deploy_shim`'s callback failure path now rolls back the `_deployed_shims` registry entry, clears `_post_deploy_watch` for that source, sets `shim.deployed = False`, and reports `RecoveryStatus.FAILED` instead of `DEPLOYED`. `deploy_approved_shim`'s failure path rolls back its `_deployed_shims` entry and re-raises, so `/approve` never reaches its unconditional `DEPLOYED` assignment for a heal that didn't take effect. Checked whether `deploy_approved_shim` was also missing a `_post_deploy_watch` cleanup symmetric to `_deploy_shim`'s: confirmed via `grep -n "_post_deploy_watch" recovery_loop.py` that `deploy_approved_shim` never reads or writes `_post_deploy_watch` in either its success or failure path (only `_deploy_shim` populates/clears it, at lines 613/642) — no asymmetry, no additional bug there.
- **Operator visibility:** `mapek_worker.py` (~lines 442-447, 489-494) already fires a `recovery_failed` event for any non-`DEPLOYED` status, so no separate event-wiring change was needed — fixing the status value alone restores operator visibility into a failed heal.
- **Tests:** `aurabackend/tests/test_recovery_loop_deploy_callback_failure.py` (new) — `test_real_drift_to_real_heal_reports_failed_when_downstream_effect_fails` drives real drift through the full loop with a failing `on_shim_deployed` and asserts `FAILED`, `shim.deployed is False`, `_deployed_shims` empty, and `_post_deploy_watch` cleaned up; `test_successful_callback_still_reports_deployed` is the happy-path control; `test_deploy_approved_shim_reraises_on_callback_failure` confirms the S41 approval path re-raises and rolls back. 3/3 passed standalone and as part of the broader `recovery_loop` suite (8/8 total); ruff clean (`E,F,I,W`).
- **Fix:** PR #343.

## BUG-048: `MAPEKWorker.start()` leaked an `AIOKafkaConsumer` object on every failed Kafka bootstrap attempt, logging "Unclosed AIOKafkaConsumer" forever once BUG-046's retry-with-backoff made those attempts indefinite
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** live verification of BUG-046/BUG-047 on production (2026-09-09, post-deploy of commit `631a0f8`) — `docker compose logs uasr_service` showed, every 60s: `ERROR | asyncio | Unclosed AIOKafkaConsumer` immediately before each `KafkaConnectionError` retry line, repeating without end (this box's free-tier profile has no Kafka broker at all, so every attempt fails — see BUG-046).
- **Severity:** cosmetic today (log noise), but a genuine resource leak (sockets / internal aiokafka background state) on an indefinite retry loop, on a 1GB box where that accumulation matters over long uptime. Only surfaced *because* BUG-046's fix made retries permanent — the pre-BUG-046 code leaked identically on its single attempt but nobody would've noticed one leaked object at process start.
- **Root cause:** `MAPEKWorker.start()` (`aurabackend/uasr/mapek_worker.py`) assigned `self._consumer = AIOKafkaConsumer(...)` and then called `await self._consumer.start()` as a separate statement. `AIOKafkaConsumer.start()` can allocate internal sockets/background tasks before the bootstrap handshake itself fails, so when it raised, the (already-assigned, half-initialized) consumer object was simply dropped when the exception propagated to `service._mapek_worker_bootstrap()`'s retry loop — nothing ever called `.stop()` on it, so aiokafka's own finalizer logs the "Unclosed" warning when it's eventually garbage-collected.
- **Fix:** construct the consumer into a local variable, wrap `.start()` in a `try/except` that calls `await consumer.stop()` (swallowing any error from `stop()` itself, since the consumer never fully started) before re-raising, and only assign `self._consumer = consumer` after a successful `.start()` — so a failed attempt is always cleaned up and never left half-assigned on the instance.
- **Tests:** `aurabackend/tests/test_mapek_worker_consumer_cleanup.py` (new) — `test_failed_consumer_start_is_cleaned_up_via_stop` asserts a consumer whose `start()` raises gets `.stop()`'d and that `self._consumer` stays `None`; `test_worker_can_still_start_after_a_failed_attempt` confirms a fresh worker still starts normally once the consumer is willing to connect. Confirmed non-vacuous: stashed the fix and re-ran against pre-fix code — the cleanup assertion failed as expected (`stop_called` was `False`); restored the fix, both pass. 2/2 passed; ruff clean.
- **Fix:** PR #344.

## BUG-049: `verify_live_deployment.py`'s `check_uasr_self_heal` asserts drift auto-deploys, but DSR-015 (this session, `main`) intentionally flipped `UASR_RISK_TIERED`'s default to `true` — auto-heal now requires human approval by default, so the live check fails against correct, intended behavior
- **Status:** false-positive
- **Found by:** ironclad-hardening loop, iteration 1, 2026-09-14 — fresh `verify_live_deployment.py` baseline run (`live_verify_20260914_013031.md`), 13 passed / 1 failed.
- **Severity:** cosmetic (test-only) — no product code is wrong; the live box is behaving exactly as DSR-015 intended (default-safe: a validated shim is held `pending_approval` rather than auto-deployed).
- **Root cause:** `uasr/service.py:117` — `_RISK_TIERED = os.getenv("UASR_RISK_TIERED", "true")` — default flipped from `false`→`true` earlier this session (DSR-015, `docs/DATA_SUITE_ROADMAP.md`), so a fresh schema-drift batch now returns `status="pending_approval"` instead of `status="deployed"`. `check_uasr_self_heal` (`scripts/verify_live_deployment.py:356`) still hard-asserts `status == "deployed"`, which was correct only under the pre-DSR-015 default. Confirmed directly: `POST /uasr/ingest` on the live box returned exactly `pending_approval` with a real gate reason (`similarity -0.0010 < threshold 0.7500`), not an error — the feature works, the test's expectation is stale.
- **Caused by:** none against product code (DSR-015 was an intentional, already-shipped decision, not a regression) — but the *test's* assumption needed updating as a direct, foreseeable consequence of that decision. Tracked here rather than silently patched because the discrepancy was only caught by running the live suite.
- **Fix:** `scripts/verify_live_deployment.py`'s `check_uasr_self_heal` extended to drive the full path instead of accepting the old shortcut: on `pending_approval`, calls `POST /uasr/recovery/{id}/approve` and asserts the returned recovery's status is `deployed`. Re-run live after the fix: 14/14 passed (`live_verify_20260914_013324.md`), `uasr_self_heal` now exercises detection + the S41 approval gate + deployment end-to-end, a stronger check than the original. Tooling-only change, PR to follow.

## BUG-050: Postgres connection-pool cache key excludes the password — any caller who knows a tenant's host/port/database/username (but not the password) is handed the same already-authenticated pool
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ironclad-hardening loop, iteration 1 — ultracode audit of `aurabackend/api_gateway/routers/` (review + adversarial verify, `wf_4c39bf2d-fe8`), 2026-09-14.
- **Severity:** blocks-feature (critical) — full authentication bypass and cross-tenant SQL execution.
- **Root cause:** `queries.py:87-106` (`_pg_pool_key`) hashes only `host`/`port`/`database`/`username`, explicitly excluding `password`. `queries.py:109-137` (`_get_or_create_pg_pool`): once a pool exists under that key, it's returned immediately with no re-check of the caller's supplied password against the pool's actual credentials — password is only used at initial pool creation (`asyncpg.create_pool`). `execute_query_with_insights` (`queries.py:362-411`) unconditionally does `connector.pool = await _get_or_create_pg_pool(connector_config)` then executes the query — no auth of the presented password before reuse. Once one caller creates a pool with real credentials, any later request with the same host/port/database/username and ANY password (including wrong/blank) reuses the identical live pool, persisting until process restart.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** First attempt folded the password into a keyed HMAC-SHA256 pool-cache key — CodeQL still flagged it (its dataflow follows the password into `hashlib.sha256` even through `hmac.new`, PR #383's CI). Corrected approach: `_pg_pool_key` stays coordinates-only (no password ever flows into the hash, so no CodeQL flag), and `_get_or_create_pg_pool`'s registry now maps each coordinates key to a list of `(password, pool)` entries; reuse requires a `hmac.compare_digest` constant-time match against a stored password, not a hash-key collision. A wrong/blank password never matches any stored entry, so `asyncpg.create_pool` runs for real and fails naturally against the actual server. `close_all_pg_pools` updated to flatten the new per-coordinate list structure. New tests: `test_key_excludes_password_by_design` (documents the key is intentionally coordinates-only), `test_wrong_password_never_reuses_the_real_pool` (asserts a second call with a wrong password creates a genuinely separate pool and that `asyncpg.create_pool` was actually invoked with the wrong password). Confirmed non-vacuous against true `main`: stashed the fix, the reuse test failed exactly as expected (same pool handed back); restored, 9/9 pass. Ruff clean (`E,F,I,W`, CI's actual ignore list).
- **Fix:** PR #383 (amended).

## BUG-051: GET /pipeline/download/{filename} has no tenant/ownership check; pipeline output files land in one shared directory under a caller-controlled filename
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** same ultracode audit as BUG-050.
- **Severity:** blocks-feature (critical) — cross-tenant IDOR, same class as BUG-016/018/019.
- **Root cause:** `pipelines.py:340-357` (`pipeline_download`) takes only `filename: str`, no `Request`/tenant param, no ownership check — only path-traversal sanitization. `pipeline/engine.py:707-713`'s `_write_file_sink` uses `sink.file_name` (caller-supplied via the pipeline definition) verbatim as the output filename under one shared `data/processed` directory with no per-tenant namespacing. A caller who creates a pipeline with a predictable `file_name` (e.g. `export.csv`) and then hits `GET /pipeline/download/export.csv` receives whichever tenant's pipeline last wrote that name. Untested (`aurabackend/tests` has zero references to `pipeline_download`/`pipeline/download`).
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** `pipeline/engine.py`'s `_write_file_sink` now writes under `OUTPUT_DIR/<tenant_slug>/` (`shared.storage.base.tenant_slug`, the same helper already used for uploaded-source isolation), with `tenant` threaded through `execute()` → `_write_sink()` → `_write_file_sink()`. `pipeline_download` now takes `request: Request` and only ever resolves inside the REQUESTING caller's own `tenant_slug(_request_tenant(request))` subdirectory — it can never reach another tenant's file regardless of filename collisions, no client-facing API change needed since the tenant is derived from the caller's own verified identity, not a URL parameter. New tests: `test_file_sink_output_is_namespaced_per_tenant` (two tenants writing the same `file_name` land in separate subdirectories with the right content in each), and a new `test_pipeline_download_tenant_isolation.py` (tenant can download its own output; a different tenant requesting the identical filename gets 404, not the first tenant's data). **Known side effect:** pipeline outputs written before this fix live in the old flat directory and become unreachable through the fixed endpoint — acceptable for a security fix, no migration script written (out of scope). Confirmed non-vacuous: stashed the fix, all 3 new/changed tests failed exactly as expected (missing `request` param, no per-tenant subdirectory); restored, 16/16 pass (49/49 across the broader pipeline test files). Ruff clean (`E,F,I,W`, CI's actual ignore list).
- **Fix:** PR to follow.

## BUG-052: /etl/execute writes output to one shared, non-tenant-scoped local directory (bypassing the S45 StorageBackend used for reads), and /etl/download/{filename} has no ownership check
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** same ultracode audit as BUG-050.
- **Severity:** blocks-feature (critical) — cross-tenant IDOR + silently drops the S3-backed persistence guarantee under `AURA_STORAGE_BACKEND=s3`.
- **Root cause:** `etl.py:327-329` sets `output_dir` to a fixed local path unconditionally, never calling `get_storage_backend()` for the write side (only for reads, per the BUG-035 fix's scope). `destination_filename` defaults (`etl.py:377`) to `{table_name}_transformed` derived only from the uploaded source file's basename — no tenant/workspace id or random component. `etl_download` (`etl.py:452`) takes only `filename: str`, no `Request` param, only path-traversal checks — no ownership check exists to bypass, it's simply absent. Two tenants uploading similarly-named source files collide on the same output filename in the same shared directory; any authenticated caller can then read another tenant's ETL output.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing; BUG-035 fixed the read side only, this is the write/download side never covered.
- **Fix:** `etl_execute`'s `output_dir` now namespaced under `tenant_slug(tenant)` (same helper the source-read side and pipelines.py's BUG-051 fix use), computed after `tenant = _request_tenant(request)` is resolved (was computed before, unconditionally, so had to be reordered). `etl_download` now takes `request` and only resolves inside the requesting caller's own `tenant_slug(...)` subdirectory. **Not fixed here (separate, larger scope):** writes still go directly to local disk via DuckDB `COPY`, not routed through the `StorageBackend` abstraction the way reads are — under `AURA_STORAGE_BACKEND=s3` the destination still silently stays on local disk rather than S3. Closing the tenant-isolation IDOR (the security-critical part) didn't require that larger rework; noted here rather than silently left unaddressed. New tests (`test_etl_download_tenant_isolation.py`): two tenants colliding on the identical `destination_filename` land in separate outputs with the right content in each; an unrelated tenant guessing a filename it never produced gets 404. Confirmed non-vacuous: stashed the fix, both tests reproduced the exact IDOR (tenant A's download returned tenant B's `SECRET` row; the unrelated tenant got 200 instead of 404); restored, 9/9 pass across the three ETL test files. Ruff clean (`E,F,I,W`, CI's actual ignore list).
- **Fix:** PR to follow.

## BUG-053: ETL's `custom_sql` transform step splices caller-supplied SQL into DuckDB verbatim, and the shared DuckDB connection factory applies no filesystem/network access restriction — bypasses the per-tenant sandboxing enforced for the initial source file
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** same ultracode audit as BUG-050.
- **Severity:** blocks-feature (critical) — arbitrary local file read (other tenants' uploads, host files) or, with httpfs/S3 configured, network exfiltration.
- **Root cause:** `etl.py:232-238` splices the `custom_sql` config string verbatim into a CTE with only an optional `{{input}}` substitution — no sanitization or path check. The connection comes from `shared/duckdb_factory.py:16-19`, which calls `get_storage_backend().configure_duckdb(con)`; both the base (`storage/base.py:63-68`, no-op) and S3 (`storage/s3.py:110-121`, installs httpfs only) implementations leave DuckDB's default filesystem/network access fully enabled. The `safe_object_name()`/`StorageBackend` sandboxing applied to `pipeline.source_file` at load time (`etl.py:267-274/331-333`) is never applied to the `custom_sql` step's SQL text. A transform like `{"type":"custom_sql","config":{"sql":"SELECT * FROM read_csv_auto('<path outside tenant>')"}}` executes with full DuckDB privileges.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** the shared connection can't simply be locked down (`enable_external_access=false`) without breaking the legitimate source-load and sink-write uses on the same connection, so instead `_validate_custom_sql` blocks the concrete DuckDB table-functions/statements a transform never legitimately needs: `read_csv`/`read_csv_auto`/`read_parquet`/`read_json(_auto)`/`read_ndjson`/`read_text`/`read_blob`/`glob`/`attach`/`detach`/`copy`/`pragma`/`install`/`load`/`httpfs`, plus any `://` URI. A transform only ever needs to compute over the table it receives via `{{input}}`. New file `test_etl_custom_sql_sandbox.py`: 9 parametrized malicious `custom_sql` payloads rejected (local file read, S3, cloud metadata via `read_json_auto`, `ATTACH`, `COPY` exfiltration, `PRAGMA`, `INSTALL`/`LOAD` httpfs, a bare URI), 2 legitimate computation-only queries still allowed, plus one test proving `_build_transform_sql` itself (not just the validator function in isolation) rejects a malicious step. **Residual risk, documented not silently accepted:** this is a keyword blocklist, not a sandboxed connection — a sufficiently creative DuckDB syntax variant not covered by the pattern could in principle slip through; closing this with certainty would need a genuinely separate, externally-access-disabled connection for the transform step, out of scope here. Confirmed non-vacuous: stashed the fix, the new test file failed to even import (`_validate_custom_sql` didn't exist); restored, 21/21 pass across the four ETL test files. Ruff clean (`E,F,I,W`, CI's actual ignore list).
- **Fix:** PR to follow.

## BUG-054: POST /webhooks accepts any http(s) URL with no SSRF filtering; the dispatcher fires real outbound requests including an on-demand test endpoint that returns the response as an oracle
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** same ultracode audit as BUG-050.
- **Severity:** blocks-feature (critical) — SSRF against internal infrastructure / cloud metadata endpoints (e.g. `169.254.169.254`), with a same-request scan oracle via `/webhooks/{id}/test`.
- **Root cause:** `webhooks.py:87` validates only `req.url.startswith(("http://","https://"))` — no denylist/allowlist of loopback, link-local, or private-CIDR hosts, no DNS-rebind protection. `WebhookDispatcher._deliver` (`shared/webhook_dispatcher.py:316-343`) does a bare `httpx.AsyncClient.post(sub.url, ...)` from the trusted backend's network position. `fire_test` (`webhook_dispatcher.py:386-401`) performs the request synchronously and returns `http_status`/`error` in the JSON response (`webhooks.py:149-154`), giving a caller a same-request oracle to scan/fingerprint internal hosts. Tenant scoping of the webhook row itself is correct and irrelevant to this — the outbound request always originates from the backend regardless of which tenant registered it.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** new `_is_ssrf_safe_url` (`webhooks.py`) parses the URL, resolves the hostname (or parses it as a literal IP directly, skipping DNS), and rejects it if any candidate address is private/loopback/link-local/reserved/multicast/unspecified (Python's `ipaddress` module). Applied to both `POST /webhooks` (replacing the old scheme-only check) and `PATCH /webhooks/{id}` (which previously had no URL validation at all — see BUG-062, closed as a side effect of this fix rather than a separate PR, since properly closing BUG-054 requires validating both paths). The DNS lookup runs via `asyncio.to_thread` (blocking `socket.getaddrinfo`, backend.md). New tests: 5 parametrized loopback/link-local/private/IPv6-loopback/metadata URLs rejected on create, one accepted (public IP), one on update. **Residual gap, documented not silently accepted:** this is registration-time validation only — no DNS-rebind protection (a hostname resolving safely at registration time could re-resolve to a private address before actual delivery). Closing that fully would require re-validating at dispatch time inside `shared/webhook_dispatcher.py`, out of scope for this fix. Confirmed non-vacuous: stashed the fix, all 6 new rejection tests failed exactly as expected (dispatcher was called); restored, 57/57 pass in the two webhook test files. Ruff clean (`E,F,I,W`, CI's actual ignore list).
- **Fix:** PR to follow.

## BUG-055: POST /upload writes the uploaded file to storage synchronously inside the async handler, with no asyncio.to_thread offload
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** same ultracode audit as BUG-050.
- **Severity:** blocks-feature (availability) — single-uvicorn-worker deployment; blocks every tenant's concurrent request for the write's duration.
- **Root cause:** `files.py:163` calls `get_storage_backend().write(tenant, safe_name, data)` directly (no `asyncio.to_thread`) inside `async def upload_file`. `LocalBackend.write` (`shared/storage/local.py:34-37`) is a synchronous `p.write_bytes(data)`. The same file already offloads a comparable call correctly (`files.py:252`, `get_file_schema` via `asyncio.to_thread`, with an explanatory comment) — the storage write, the endpoint's primary I/O, was missed.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** `obj_info = await asyncio.to_thread(get_storage_backend().write, tenant, safe_name, data)`, matching the file's own `get_file_schema` offload pattern. New test `test_upload_write_offloaded_to_thread` (spies on `asyncio.to_thread` the same way `test_pipeline_execution.py`'s offload tests do) asserts `write` appears in the offloaded-call list. Confirmed non-vacuous: stashed the fix, the new test failed exactly as expected; restored, 8/8 pass. Ruff clean (`E,F,I,W`, CI's actual ignore list).
- **Fix:** PR to follow.

## BUG-056: POST /upload buffers the entire request body into an unbounded in-memory BytesIO with no server-side size cap
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** same ultracode audit as BUG-050.
- **Severity:** degrades-accuracy → availability (OOM risk) — the advertised `max_file_size: 25MB` (`files.py:56`) is documentation only; `shared/file_service.py`'s `self.max_file_size` attribute is set but never read/compared anywhere.
- **Root cause:** `upload_universal`'s read loop (`files.py:143-160`) accumulates chunks into `io.BytesIO()` with no comparison against any maximum. No middleware (CORS/RateLimit/JWTAuth/APIKeyAuth/RequestID/RequestLogging/AuditLog/SecurityHeaders, per `shared/service_factory.py`) inspects `Content-Length` or caps body size.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** the read loop now checks `bytes_written` against `file_service.max_file_size` (25MB) after every chunk and raises `HTTPException(413)` the moment it's exceeded — aborts mid-stream rather than after buffering the whole body. Added `except HTTPException: raise` before the router's existing broad `except Exception` (which would otherwise flatten a deliberate 413 into a 500), matching the pattern already used elsewhere in this same file (`get_file_profile`/`delete_file`). New tests: an upload exceeding a (monkeypatched, small) limit gets 413 and is never written to storage; one under the limit still succeeds. Confirmed non-vacuous: stashed the fix, the oversized-upload test got 200 instead of 413, exactly as expected; restored, 10/10 pass, no regressions in the other four upload-related test files (8/8). Ruff clean (`E,F,I,W`, CI's actual ignore list).
- **Fix:** PR to follow.

## BUG-057: /workspaces CRUD (list/create/update/delete) has no tenant scoping at all — one global workspace registry shared by every tenant
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** same ultracode audit as BUG-050.
- **Severity:** blocks-feature (IDOR) — cross-tenant read of all workspace names/descriptions; brute-forceable `ws_<timestamp_ms>` ids let a caller rename or delete another tenant's workspace record.
- **Root cause:** `workspaces.py`'s `list_workspaces`/`create_workspace`/`update_workspace`/`delete_workspace` (lines 198/206/224/241) all operate on the module-level `_workspaces_store` keyed only by `id`, never calling `_request_tenant()`/`current_workspace_id()` — the two tenant-scoping helpers defined in the same file for other routers to use. `delete_workspace`'s only guard is against the literal id `"default"`, not ownership.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** every record now carries a `tenant_id` stamped at creation (`_request_tenant(request)`); `list_workspaces` filters to records the caller owns, `update_workspace`/`delete_workspace` 404 (not just "not found by id") when the id exists but belongs to a different tenant. `DEFAULT_WORKSPACE_ID` keeps `tenant_id=None` and stays globally visible/undeletable for every caller — a shared system placeholder, not tenant data, so its existing behavior is preserved exactly. New file `test_workspaces_crud_tenant_isolation.py`: cross-tenant list leakage blocked, rename/delete of another tenant's workspace 404s even knowing the real id (and doesn't actually mutate it), the owner can still update/delete their own. Confirmed non-vacuous: stashed the fix, all 5 new tests failed with `TypeError` (the old functions didn't even accept a `request` param — structurally incapable of a tenant check); restored, 23/23 pass across the five tenant-isolation-related test files. Ruff clean (`E,F,I,W`, CI's actual ignore list).
- **Fix:** PR to follow.

## BUG-058: POST /synthetic/generate's output_uri is a fully caller-controlled write destination (file://, s3://, gs://, abfs://) with no tenant-path confinement
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** same ultracode audit as BUG-050.
- **Severity:** blocks-feature (arbitrary write / credential-scoped SSRF).
- **Root cause:** `GenerateRequest.output_uri` (`synthetic.py:84`) is a plain `str` with no validator/allow-list, passed unmodified to `SyntheticDatasetWriter.generate` (`synthetic.py:128-132`), which resolves it directly via `pafs.FileSystem.from_uri(output_uri)` (`synthetic/writer.py:80-91`) using the process's own filesystem/cloud credentials — no scheme allow-list, no tenant-derived path prefix.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** new `_confine_output_uri` rejects unsupported schemes and, for a local path/`file://` URI, rewrites it to `<synthetic-output-root>/<tenant_slug>/<job_id>/<requested-basename>` before it ever reaches `SyntheticDatasetWriter` — the caller can request a name, never a location. `job_id` (already generated per request) keeps each call's output unique so repeated/concurrent calls with the same requested basename never collide or silently reuse a previous run's files (caught by an initial version of this fix breaking `test_generate_job_runs_to_completion` — n_files came back 0 because a second confined path collided with a first run's leftover state; adding `job_id` to the confined path fixed it). **Deliberately NOT restricted: `s3://`/`gs://`/`abfs://` URIs pass through unchanged** — this is an enterprise bring-your-own-cloud feature where the caller names their own cloud destination by design, not this server's storage; only the local-filesystem case (unambiguously this server's own disk/credentials) is confined. New file `test_synthetic_output_uri_confinement.py` (9 tests: absolute path/file:// URI/Windows drive path/traversal all confined; different tenants get different roots; cloud URIs pass through unchanged; unsupported schemes rejected) plus `test_generate_job_runs_to_completion` updated to read the real on-disk location back from the job record instead of asserting on the caller's original (now-bypassed) path. Confirmed non-vacuous: stashed the fix, the new test file failed to import (`_confine_output_uri` didn't exist); restored, 19/19 pass across the three synthetic test files. Ruff clean (`E,F,I,W`, CI's actual ignore list). PR #391.

## BUG-059: Password-mode login (POST /auth/token) leaks whether an email is registered via a timing side channel
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** same ultracode audit as BUG-050.
- **Severity:** degrades-accuracy (user enumeration) — no direct credential compromise.
- **Root cause:** `auth.py:113` (`_issue_token_password`): the "user not found"/no-password-hash path returns immediately after a bare SELECT; the "wrong password" path always falls through to `verify_password` → `bcrypt.checkpw` (tens-to-hundreds of ms). Both raise the identical `AuthenticationError("Invalid credentials")`, but response latency distinguishes them. No dummy-hash constant-time comparison exists for the not-found path.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** a module-level `_DUMMY_PASSWORD_HASH` (a real bcrypt hash of a random, unknown value, computed once at import time) is now compared against on the not-found path, so it runs exactly one bcrypt comparison — the same cost as a real user's wrong-password rejection. New test `test_nonexistent_user_still_runs_a_bcrypt_comparison` proves this structurally (spies on `shared.password.verify_password`, asserts exactly one call on both the not-found and wrong-password paths) rather than via a flaky wall-clock timing assertion. Confirmed non-vacuous: stashed the fix, the new test failed with `AssertionError` (0 calls on the not-found path, as before); restored, 14/14 pass. Ruff clean (`E,F,I,W`, CI's actual ignore list). PR #392.

## BUG-060: GET /databases/test/{db_type} relays the downstream service's JSON body as an HTTP 200 regardless of its actual upstream status code, and never forwards the caller's Authorization header
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** same ultracode audit as BUG-050.
- **Severity:** degrades-accuracy — violates backend.md's explicit proxy rule (preserve upstream status code, forward Authorization); silently downgrades a real upstream error into an apparent success for automated callers.
- **Root cause:** `connections.py:351-362` (`test_database_connection`) does `response = await client.get(...)` and returns `response.json()` directly with no `raise_for_status()`/status inspection, and doesn't accept a `Request` param so it can't forward headers.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** `test_database_connection` now takes a `Request` param, forwards the caller's `Authorization` header to the downstream database service, and returns a `JSONResponse` carrying the upstream's real `status_code` instead of always 200 (same pattern as the UASR/causal_service proxies in `pipelines.py`). An unreachable/malformed-JSON upstream now reports 503 instead of masquerading as a 200. `httpx` moved to a module-level import so it's patchable in tests, matching `pipelines.py`. Regression tests in `tests/test_connections_database_test_proxy.py` (Authorization forwarding, upstream-401-preserved, unreachable-service-503) confirmed failing against the pre-fix code via `git stash` before the fix landed. PR #393.

## BUG-061: /chat/stream's commander loop runs on the process's shared default ThreadPoolExecutor via run_in_executor(None, ...), and cannot be cancelled once started — risks starving every other blocking call in the single-worker gateway
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** same ultracode audit as BUG-050.
- **Severity:** blocks-feature (availability) — requires several concurrent/abandoned streams to trigger, but the mechanism is real and unguarded.
- **Root cause:** `chat.py:690` calls `loop.run_in_executor(None, _worker)`, selecting the loop's shared default `ThreadPoolExecutor` (`min(32, cpu_count()+4)` workers) — the same pool every `asyncio.to_thread` call in the gateway depends on (no custom executor is configured anywhere). `_worker` runs `run_commander` (`agents/commander.py:121`), up to 8 iterations of a synchronous LLM call plus a DuckDB tool execution — genuinely long-running. `_sse()`'s `finally: worker.cancel()` (`chat.py:700`) is a no-op once the underlying thread has started (per `concurrent.futures.Future.cancel()`'s documented behavior), so an abandoned/disconnected stream's thread runs to completion regardless, still holding its pool slot. No semaphore/concurrency limiter exists on this route.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** `chat.py` now builds a dedicated `ThreadPoolExecutor` (`_COMMANDER_EXECUTOR`, `AURA_COMMANDER_MAX_WORKERS` env var, default 8 workers) at module load and passes it to `loop.run_in_executor(_COMMANDER_EXECUTOR, _worker)` instead of the shared default (`None`). True cancellation of an already-started `concurrent.futures` thread still isn't possible (documented `Future.cancel()` behavior, unchanged by this fix), so the fix is isolation, not cancellation: an abandoned/long commander stream can now only exhaust its own bounded pool, never the pool every other `asyncio.to_thread` call in the gateway depends on. Regression test `test_stream_worker_runs_on_dedicated_executor_not_the_shared_default` in `tests/test_commander_endpoint.py` spies on `BaseEventLoop.run_in_executor` and asserts the executor passed is `chat._COMMANDER_EXECUTOR`, not `None`; confirmed failing against the pre-fix code via `git stash` before the fix landed. PR #394.

## BUG-062: PATCH /webhooks/{id} can overwrite `url` with any string, skipping the http(s)-scheme validation POST /webhooks enforces
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** same ultracode audit as BUG-050.
- **Severity:** cosmetic (validation inconsistency) — practical impact limited by httpx rejecting unsupported schemes at delivery time.
- **Root cause:** `webhooks.py:131-139` (`update_webhook`) passes `req.model_dump(exclude_none=True)` straight into `webhook_dispatcher.update()` with no equivalent check to POST's `req.url.startswith(("http://","https://"))` (`webhooks.py:87-88`). `WebhookUpdateRequest.url` is a plain `Optional[str]` with no validator.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** closed as a side effect of BUG-054's fix, not a separate PR — `update_webhook` now runs the same `_is_ssrf_safe_url` check (which also rejects non-http(s) schemes) whenever `req.url` is set. Covered by BUG-054's `test_update_webhook_rejects_internal_url`.

## BUG-063: Concurrent first-touch of a new UASR source_id loses one side's baseline/schema write (lost update)
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/uasr/` (`core-service` + related groups), 2026-09-14.
- **Severity:** blocks-feature — silently defeats drift detection for the affected source.
- **Root cause:** `state_store.py:167` (`InMemoryStateStore.load`) returns a brand-new, un-inserted `SourceState()` for any `source_id` not yet present in `self._data` — insertion only happens in `save()`. `service.py`'s HTTP handlers (`ingest_batch` line 596, `heal_batch` line 717, `register_baseline` lines 819/834, `declare_schema_intent` line 877) all offload the load-mutate-save sequence via `asyncio.to_thread`, which runs on real OS threads from the default `ThreadPoolExecutor` — genuinely concurrent, not serialized by the event loop. Two near-simultaneous first-touches of the same new source (e.g. a pipeline's first ingest racing an operator's `POST /uasr/baseline`) each mutate their own private `SourceState` object; whichever `save()` runs last silently overwrites the other, permanently losing that call's baseline/schema write with no error surfaced.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing; the `asyncio.to_thread` offload (added to satisfy the single-worker non-blocking rule) is what turns this from a hypothetical race into a real cross-thread one.
- **Fix:** fixed together with BUG-064 (same root gap — no lock across any load-mutate-save round-trip). `DriftDetector` now owns a per-source `threading.Lock` registry (`_lock_for`, guarded dict access for creating a new source's lock), and every load-mutate-save call site (`register_baseline`, `register_reference_embedding`, `declare_schema_intent`, `detect`) runs inside `with self._lock_for(source_id):`. `threading.Lock`, not `asyncio.Lock`, because these calls run on real worker threads via `asyncio.to_thread`, not coroutines. See BUG-064 for test evidence (both fixed in the same PR/commit).

## BUG-064: Unsynchronized load-mutate-save on an existing source's drift state allows a lost update under concurrent detect() calls
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/uasr/` (`detection-drift` group), 2026-09-14.
- **Severity:** degrades-accuracy — silently drops KL-history samples, skewing the adaptive threshold.
- **Root cause:** `drift_detector.py:302` (`detect`) loads `SourceState`, mutates `kl_history`/schema in place, then saves — no lock across the read-modify-write. Neither `InMemoryStateStore.save` (plain dict assignment) nor `RedisStateStore.save` (unconditional SET, no CAS/version check) guards against a second concurrent `detect()` for the *same, already-existing* `source_id` (e.g. an HTTP request racing the `mapek_worker` Kafka consumption loop, or duplicate-delivery retries), both dispatched via `asyncio.to_thread`. The same unguarded-mutation pattern applies to `martingale.py:419`'s `self._martingale[source_id][column] += increment` and `conformal_martingale.py`'s `ConformalDriftMartingale.update`.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing; related to BUG-063 (same missing per-source lock across load/mutate/save), but distinct in that this fires on sources that already exist, not just on first-touch.
- **Fix:** `detect()`'s entire load→check→save body now runs inside `with self._lock_for(batch.source_id):` (same per-source `threading.Lock` registry added for BUG-063), so a concurrently-arriving batch for the same source can't interleave its own load-mutate-save with this one. Deliberately scoped to `DriftDetector`'s own state (the confirmed root-cause location); `martingale.py`/`conformal_martingale.py`'s separate in-process dicts share the same class of gap but are a distinct class/state and out of this diff — not filed as a new bug since it's already flagged in this entry's root cause for a future pass. New `TestPerSourceLocking` class in `tests/test_uasr_drift_detector.py`: two tests directly prove the locking mechanism (same source_id strictly serialized across 6 threads — `max_concurrent == 1`; different source_ids are NOT serialized against each other — `max_concurrent > 1`, ruling out an accidental single global lock), plus an identity test (`_lock_for` returns the same object for the same source, different objects for different sources) and an end-to-end concurrent-writes test. Confirmed non-vacuous: stashed the fix, `_lock_for` didn't exist (`AttributeError`) so 4/5 new tests failed immediately; restored, 5/5 pass. Full `aurabackend/uasr/` + `recovery`/`mapek` test sweep: 130 passed, 1 skipped, no regressions. Ruff clean (`E,F,I,W`, CI's actual ignore list). PR pending merge.

## BUG-065: Cross-source-heal re-baseline path calls the DriftDetector synchronously, blocking the event loop under RedisStateStore
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/uasr/` (`mapek-loop` + `state-config-metrics` groups, found independently by both — same bug), 2026-09-14.
- **Severity:** blocks-feature (availability) — stalls the whole single-worker UASR process for every tenant.
- **Root cause:** `mapek_worker.py:554-556`, in the cross-source auto-heal branch of the Kafka consume loop, calls `self._detector._compute_distributions(batch)` and `self._detector.register_baseline(batch.source_id, dists)` directly — no `asyncio.to_thread`. `register_baseline` (`drift_detector.py:148-179`) performs a `StateStore.load()`/`save()` round trip, which under `RedisStateStore` (a real, selectable backend per `runtime_config.py`) is a synchronous, blocking `redis.Redis` client call. Every other call to `detect`/`register_baseline`/`baseline_distance`/`has_baseline` in this file and in `service.py`'s HTTP handlers is wrapped in `asyncio.to_thread` (one with an explicit comment citing exactly this Redis-blocking risk) — this call site is not.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** fixed together with BUG-066 (same duplicated pattern, two call sites). New `MAPEKWorker._reregister_detector_baseline` helper wraps both `_compute_distributions` and `register_baseline` in `asyncio.to_thread`; both call sites (this one and `_knowledge_update`'s) now `await` it instead of calling the detector directly. See BUG-066 for test evidence (both fixed in the same PR/commit).

## BUG-066: _knowledge_update's post-heal re-baseline calls DriftDetector.register_baseline without asyncio.to_thread
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/uasr/` (`mapek-loop` + `state-config-metrics` groups, found independently by both — same bug), 2026-09-14.
- **Severity:** blocks-feature (availability) — the more commonly hit of the two register_baseline blocking-call sites (fires on every DEPLOYED+batch_healed recovery, not just cross-source heals).
- **Root cause:** `mapek_worker.py:1073` (`_knowledge_update`, an `async def` awaited directly from `_run_forever`) calls `self._detector._compute_distributions(batch)` and `self._detector.register_baseline(batch.source_id, dists)` with no `asyncio.to_thread` wrapper. Same blocking-Redis hazard as BUG-065 — under `UASR_STATE_BACKEND=redis`, this stalls the sole event loop (and every other tenant's concurrent request) for the duration of the synchronous Redis `SET`.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing; same bug class as BUG-065, different call site.
- **Fix:** both this call site and BUG-065's now `await self._reregister_detector_baseline(batch)`, a new helper that wraps `_compute_distributions` and `register_baseline` in `asyncio.to_thread`. Two new tests in `tests/test_mapek_baseline_poisoning.py`: `test_knowledge_update_rebaseline_offloads_to_a_thread` spies on `asyncio.to_thread` and asserts both functions were dispatched through it; `test_knowledge_update_rebaseline_does_not_block_the_event_loop` injects a 0.3s delay into `register_baseline` and asserts a concurrently-scheduled ticker coroutine never sees a gap >0.15s. Confirmed non-vacuous: stashed the fix, the offload-spy test failed (`assert _compute_distributions in []`) against the pre-fix direct calls; restored, 6/6 pass. Broader `mapek`/`cross_source_heal`/`martingale` sweep: 30 passed. Ruff clean (`E,F,I,W`, CI's actual ignore list). PR pending merge.

## BUG-067: Kafka consumer (and DuckDB connection) leak if MAPEKWorker.start() fails after consumer.start() succeeds
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/uasr/` (`mapek-loop` group), 2026-09-14.
- **Severity:** degrades-accuracy (resource leak, availability risk under sustained retry) — leaks one Kafka consumer connection per retry on a persistent failure.
- **Root cause:** `mapek_worker.py:300-336` (`start`) guards `consumer.start()` itself with a try/except that calls `consumer.stop()` on failure (lines 314-327), but the two statements immediately after — `self._open_duckdb()` (line 330) and `Path(parquet_dir).mkdir()` (line 331) — have no equivalent guard. If either raises (bad `duckdb_path`/permissions, read-only or full `parquet_dir`), `self._running` is never set `True`, so a later `stop()` call is a no-op (`if not self._running: return`, line 339) and the already-started `AIOKafkaConsumer` is never closed. `service._mapek_worker_bootstrap` retries this construction on an infinite backoff loop, so a persistent failure at this step leaks one Kafka consumer (background tasks/sockets) per retry indefinitely.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** the `consumer.start()` try/except now wraps the whole setup sequence (`consumer.start()` → `_open_duckdb()` → `mkdir()`), and `self._consumer`/`self._duckdb_con`/`self._running` are only assigned once every step has succeeded. On any failure, whatever got created (a started consumer, an opened duckdb connection) is closed/stopped before re-raising — mirroring the existing BUG-046 cleanup pattern this file already had for `consumer.start()` alone, extended to cover the two steps after it. Two new tests in `tests/test_mapek_worker_consumer_cleanup.py`: `test_consumer_is_stopped_if_duckdb_open_fails_after_consumer_starts` and `test_duckdb_connection_is_closed_if_mkdir_fails_after_duckdb_opens`. Confirmed non-vacuous: stashed the fix, both new tests failed (`assert False` on `stop_called`/`closed["value"]`); restored, 4/4 pass. Broader `mapek`/kafka-bootstrap sweep: 6 passed. Ruff clean (`E,F,I,W`, CI's actual ignore list). PR pending merge.

## BUG-068: UASR DuckDB table name f-string-interpolated into DDL/DML instead of using shared/sql_identifiers.py's quote_identifier
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/uasr/` (`mapek-loop` group), 2026-09-14.
- **Severity:** cosmetic today, real risk once `table_name` becomes configurable — no live exploit (hardcoded default), but the escaping is genuinely wrong.
- **Root cause:** `mapek_worker.py:851` (`_write_duckdb_atomic`) splices `self._cfg.table_name` directly into `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS "{tbl}" ...` and `INSERT INTO "{tbl}" BY NAME ...` via an f-string with hand-rolled double-quotes, instead of `shared/sql_identifiers.py`'s `quote_identifier` — the one shared quoter `backend.md`/`security.md` mandate for every identifier spliced into SQL. `table_name` only ever takes the hardcoded default `"uasr_events"` today, so there is no live exploit, but a value containing a double-quote would break out of the identifier the moment this becomes configurable (env var, per-tenant table).
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** `_write_duckdb_atomic` now calls `quote_identifier(self._cfg.table_name)` once and splices the already-quoted result directly (no extra manual quotes) into both statements. New test `test_table_name_with_embedded_quote_is_safely_escaped` in `tests/test_mapek_concurrent_sink.py` uses a table name containing `" ; DROP TABLE secrets; --` and asserts the write still succeeds and the row lands in the correctly-escaped table. Confirmed non-vacuous: stashed the fix, the same test raised `duckdb.ParserException: syntax error at or near ";"` on the pre-fix code — the injection actually broke the SQL parser; restored, passes. Full concurrent-sink suite: 6/6 pass (no regression to the existing multi-writer/catalog-race behavior). Ruff clean (`E,F,I,W`, CI's actual ignore list). PR pending merge.

## BUG-069: Semantic-drift embedding uses Python's randomized built-in hash() — breaks across process restarts and RedisStateStore replicas
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/uasr/` (`detection-drift` group), 2026-09-14.
- **Severity:** blocks-feature — silently defeats the semantic-drift channel's cross-replica design, with no error/log signal.
- **Root cause:** `drift_detector.py:902-904` (`_compute_batch_embedding`) feature-hashes categorical values with Python's built-in `hash(str)`, whose output is randomized per-process (PEP 456 / `PYTHONHASHSEED`) unless explicitly pinned — `idx = abs(h) % dim` places the feature in a different embedding-vector dimension in every process. `register_reference_embedding()` persists this vector via `StateStore`, including `RedisStateStore` (whose own docstring states it is "shared across worker replicas ... resolves the cross-replica cold-miss"), and `_check_semantic_drift()` later compares it via cosine distance. A baseline registered by one process and compared against a batch embedded by a different process (a second replica, or the same replica after a restart) shares almost no dimensions by construction — cosine distance is effectively random regardless of actual drift, either firing spurious CRITICAL alarms constantly or masking genuine semantic drift inside the noise.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** `hash(token)` replaced with `zlib.crc32(token.encode("utf-8"))`, deterministic across processes/restarts. `crc32` is unsigned (no sign bit like Python's `hash()`), so the random-projection sign bit is now derived from a low bit of the same hash (`h & 1`) and the dimension index from the remaining bits (`(h >> 1) % dim`) so the two don't reuse the same bit. New `TestSemanticEmbeddingCrossProcessStability` in `tests/test_uasr_drift_detector.py` spawns two real subprocesses with different explicit `PYTHONHASHSEED` values, computes the embedding for an identical batch in each, and asserts the two embeddings are bit-for-bit identical — the only way to genuinely reproduce the per-process hash randomization this bug depended on. Confirmed non-vacuous: stashed the fix, the test failed (`AssertionError`, embeddings differed at index 74) against the pre-fix `hash()`-based code; restored, passes. Full drift-detector suite: 50/50 pass, no regression to the existing categorical-drift/numeric-false-alarm behavior. Ruff clean (`E,F,I,W`, CI's actual ignore list). PR pending merge.

## BUG-070: CausalRLEvaluator.select_winner runs CPU-bound shim evaluation and sandbox code execution synchronously on the event loop
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/uasr/` (`detection-drift` group), 2026-09-14. Adversarial verify: 2/3 confirmed, 1/3 refuted.
- **Severity:** blocks-feature (availability) — only when `use_causal_rl_evaluator` is enabled, but blocks the whole worker for every tenant when it triggers.
- **Root cause:** `causal_rl_evaluator.py:173-183` (`select_winner`, an `async def`) calls `cand.transform(...)` (executes LLM-generated shim code via `recovery_loop.py`'s `_sandbox_execute`) and `drift_score_fn(...)` (wraps `DriftDetector.detect`) directly, with no `asyncio.to_thread` anywhere in the call chain — unlike `service.py`'s three HTTP handlers, which explicitly wrap the identical `DriftDetector.detect` call in `asyncio.to_thread` with a comment noting it "can hit a blocking Redis round-trip" and is CPU-heavy. Whenever the causal-RL evaluator path is enabled and a drift event collects multiple validated shim candidates, every candidate runs sandbox execution plus two full `detect()` passes synchronously on the single uvicorn worker's event loop.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** `drift_score_fn` calls (`drift_before` and each candidate's `drift_after`) now go through `asyncio.to_thread`. `cand.transform` is branched on `asyncio.iscoroutinefunction`: an async transform is awaited directly (already event-loop-safe by the existing contract), a sync transform is dispatched via `asyncio.to_thread` — calling a sync callable directly first (the prior code's approach) would already have executed all its blocking work before the code could know whether to await it, so the branch has to happen before the call, not after. The prior "sync callable that itself returns an awaitable" edge case is preserved: after the offloaded call returns, the result is awaited if it has `__await__`. Three new tests in `tests/test_uasr_causal_rl.py`: `test_select_winner_offloads_drift_score_fn_and_sync_transform` (spies on `asyncio.to_thread`), `test_select_winner_does_not_block_the_event_loop` (0.3s synthetic delay, concurrent ticker), `test_select_winner_still_awaits_async_transform_directly` (regression guard that async transforms still work correctly, not accidentally broken by the to_thread branch). Confirmed non-vacuous: stashed the fix, the offload-spy test failed against the pre-fix direct calls; restored, 3/3 pass. Full causal-RL suite: 32/32 pass. Ruff clean (`E,F,I,W`, CI's actual ignore list). PR pending merge.

## BUG-071: Adaptive ζ floor is half the documented default, doubling detector sensitivity on low-variance sources versus the documented design
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/uasr/` (`detection-drift` group), 2026-09-14.
- **Severity:** cosmetic (documentation/behavior mismatch, produces extra false-positive drift alerts on stable sources) — not a correctness-critical path.
- **Root cause:** `drift_detector.py`'s `_dynamic_threshold` docstring (line 749) states "ζ = mean(D_KL) + 2·std(D_KL), floored at the default", but the implementation (line 759) floors it at `self._default_zeta * 0.5` — half the stated value — once 5+ KL samples exist. For a source whose real KL noise floor is very low, the effective ζ can settle as low as `default_zeta/2` instead of never dropping below `default_zeta`, making the detector up to 2x more sensitive than documented once past cold-start.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** documentation-only fix, not a behavior change — git history shows the `* 0.5` floor has been the actual implementation since `_dynamic_threshold`'s introduction (no drift/regression at any point), so this is corrected as a stale docstring rather than risking a live change to production detection sensitivity that nothing asked for. The docstring now states the true floor (`default_zeta / 2`) and explains why. New `TestDynamicThresholdFloor` class in `tests/test_uasr_drift_detector.py` (4 tests) pins the exact numeric behavior — including a test that explicitly asserts the floor is `default_zeta / 2`, NOT the full `default_zeta` — so doc and code can never silently drift apart again undetected. Since this is doc-only, there is no pre-fix/post-fix behavior difference to confirm via stash; the new tests pass identically against both, and exist to lock the (unchanged) behavior going forward. Full drift-detector suite: 54/54 pass. Ruff clean (`E,F,I,W`, CI's actual ignore list). PR pending merge.

## BUG-072: Cross-source shim borrowing has no tenant/org scoping — one tenant's shim can be executed against another tenant's data
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/uasr/` (`actuation-routing` group), 2026-09-14.
- **Severity:** blocks-feature (cross-tenant data/logic leakage) — an unattended background MAPE-K action, no caller/auth context involved at all.
- **Root cause:** `cross_source_heal.py:62` (`attempt_cross_source_heal`) borrows a "sibling" source's DEPLOYED shim purely by `drift_type` + recency, via `HealingMetricTracker.find_recent_deployed_shim()` (`metrics.py:280-309`), which filters only on `drift_type`, `exclude_source_id`, and a time window — there is no `tenant_id`/`org_id` anywhere in `RecoveryEvent`, `BatchPayload`, or `DriftDetectionResult` (`models.py`). Nothing stops a shim generated from Tenant A's source/schema being selected as a candidate and executed (via `run_with_candidate_shim`) against Tenant B's batch the moment both tenants' sources drift with the same `drift_type` within `correlation_window_seconds` of each other. This is distinct from the already-documented "KNOWN GAP" comment in `service.py` (which is about an authenticated caller's HTTP request not being checked against the source they own) — this is a background action with no caller/auth context at all.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** pending a product/architecture decision, not a one-file patch. Investigated 2026-09-14: `source_id` carries NO tenant namespace anywhere in the UASR ingest path — `connections.py:435` builds it as `req.source_id or f"{connector_type}:{table_name}"` with no tenant prefix, and `BatchPayload`/`DriftDetectionResult`/`RecoveryEvent` (`models.py`) have no `tenant_id`/`org_id` field to check against. This is the SAME whole-subsystem gap the `service.py:856` "KNOWN GAP" comment already documents and deliberately defers ("do not add ad hoc tenant checks here without doing the same for the other two endpoints, which would just fragment the (still missing) scoping model") — fixing cross_source_heal alone, without a real tenant field threaded through the ingest→detect→recover pipeline, would be exactly that anti-pattern. Mitigating factor: `attempt_cross_source_heal` only runs when `UASR_CORRELATION_AUTO_HEAL` is explicitly set (opt-in, default off per `runtime_config.py`'s `correlation_flags`), so this is not live-exploitable on a default deployment. Left open pending an explicit decision on whether/how to add tenant scoping to UASR's data model, rather than resolved unilaterally.
- **Decision (2026-09-26, user):** document the risk rather than add tenant scoping to UASR's data model now. Documented in the `correlation_flags()` docstring (`aurabackend/uasr/runtime_config.py`) with an explicit do-not-enable-on-multi-tenant warning. Operating rule: leave `UASR_CORRELATION_AUTO_HEAL` unset (its default) on any multi-tenant deployment; it is safe only where every source belongs to one tenant. Not verified: whether the live box has the flag set -- it is off by default and nothing in this repo sets it, but the box's environment was not inspected. Revisit if a real multi-tenant UASR deployment needs auto-heal.
- **Decision (2026-10-02, user):** reversed -- scope UASR by tenant. `HealingMetricTracker.find_recent_deployed_shim` now only returns a shim from a source in the same tenant (`uasr/tenancy.same_tenant`); un-namespaced sources form one group, so single-tenant deployments behave as before. Regression: `test_a_shim_is_never_borrowed_from_another_tenant` in `tests/test_uasr_tenant_scoping.py` (borrows from the other tenant on the old code). Fixed together with BUG-262, BUG-263, BUG-264 and BUG-270.

## BUG-073: Unescaped column names spliced into generated Python shim source — code injection via drift_vector
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/uasr/` (`actuation-routing` group), 2026-09-14.
- **Severity:** blocks-feature (code injection) — an upstream/attacker-influenced schema field can inject arbitrary Python into a shim before it ever reaches sandbox validation.
- **Root cause:** every template-shim generator in `actuator_agent.py:152` (`_schema_shim` type_change/removed/added/rename, `_statistical_shim` rescale/clip) splices `col` — a column name taken directly from `drift_vector`, itself derived from an upstream (potentially external/influenced) data source's schema — into the generated Python source via bare f-string interpolation like `f'if "{col}" in row:'`, with no quoting/escaping. Unlike `shared/sql_identifiers.py`'s `quote_identifier` used for the equivalent SQL-splicing risk elsewhere in the codebase, there is no equivalent guard here. A column name containing a `"` or backslash breaks out of the Python string literal in the generated `def transform(rows):` source, letting arbitrary Python statements be injected before the "sandbox" ever validates them — i.e. the sandbox validates attacker-chosen code, not just a data transform.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** every manual `f'"{col}"'`-style quoting across all six generators (`_schema_shim`'s type_change/removed/added/rename branches, `_statistical_shim`'s rescale/clip/monitor branches) replaced with Python's own `{col!r}` (repr), which correctly escapes embedded quotes/backslashes the same way `quote_identifier` does for SQL identifiers. New tests in `tests/test_uasr_actuator_shims.py` probe every generator with a column name containing `"] = __import__('os').system(...); row["` and assert the generated shim still `ast.parse`s to valid Python and treats the value as inert data. Confirmed non-vacuous: stashed the fix, all 7 new tests failed with `SyntaxError` (the injected code broke the generated source, exactly the vulnerability); restored, 20/20 pass. Ruff clean (`E,F,I,W`, CI's actual ignore list). PR pending merge.

## BUG-074: distributed_repair's _prune_expired() races when called outside the admission lock, corrupting the per-source active-lease counter
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/uasr/` (`actuation-routing` group), 2026-09-14.
- **Severity:** degrades-accuracy — silently defeats `max_per_source` fleet-wide fairness; the corruption persists and never self-corrects.
- **Root cause:** `distributed_repair.py:184-196` (`_prune_expired`) reads the expired-token set, decrements the per-source `hincrby` counter for each, then removes them from the sorted set — three separate non-atomic Redis round-trips. It is called both under the admission lock inside `_try_admit()` (line 239) and, critically, unlocked directly from `active_count()`, which any caller (a health/status endpoint, a monitoring loop) can invoke concurrently with an in-flight `_try_admit()` or another `active_count()` call. Two concurrent callers can both read the same expired token before either removes it, and both `hincrby` the same source's counter down — double-decrementing (or driving negative) a value `_try_admit()`'s per-source cap check depends on, silently defeating `max_per_source` for that source with no self-correction.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** split `_prune_expired` into a lock-acquiring public wrapper and `_prune_expired_locked` (the actual read/decrement/remove body). The wrapper acquires the same short-lived admit lock (`SET NX PX`) already used by `_try_admit`, so any two callers — `active_count()`, `queue_depth()`, and `_try_admit()`'s own prune — now serialize through one critical section instead of racing; if the lock is already held, the wrapper skips this round rather than blocking (pruning is opportunistic, and `_try_admit()` always prunes under lock immediately before admitting, so nothing is missed). `_try_admit()` itself now calls `_prune_expired_locked()` directly since it already holds the lock, avoiding a self-deadlock. `aurabackend/uasr/distributed_repair.py`. Regression test `test_concurrent_prune_does_not_double_decrement_per_source_counter` (`tests/test_uasr_distributed_repair.py`) seeds one expired lease, slows `zrangebyscore` to widen the read/remove gap, runs two concurrent `_prune_expired()` calls, and asserts the per-source counter lands at exactly 0 — confirmed non-vacuous via `git stash` (fails with `-1` on the old unlocked code, passes on the fix). PR: pending.

## BUG-075: js-yaml CVE-2026-84375 (CPU-DoS via YAML merge keys) — transitive dev dependency below the already-patched override floor
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** GitHub Dependabot alert #74, 2026-09-14 (flagged on every backend PR push throughout this session's `remote:` output).
- **Severity:** cosmetic (dev-only dependency, not shipped to production; requires attacker-controlled YAML fed into build-time tooling — eslint config parsing or `openapi-typescript`'s spec parsing — to trigger the CPU exhaustion, not a runtime attack surface of the deployed app).
