Confirmed non-vacuous by stashing the fix — exactly the 4 new cases fail with "DID NOT RAISE", every pre-existing case still passes — then restoring it and confirming all 17 tests in the file pass, the sibling BUG-082/083 pipeline tests (`test_pipeline_execution.py`) show no regression, and the audit's exact PoC string now raises. PR: pending.

## BUG-115: PII redaction is exact-key-name matching only — trivially bypassed by unlisted keys or PII embedded in free-text values
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/shared/` (`error-handling-pii-ratelimit` group), 2026-09-18.
- **Severity:** high — `PIIMaskingMiddleware` calls this on every inbound JSON body as the documented "Perimeter Defense" before data reaches internal Kafka streams, but the guarantee doesn't actually hold for any key spelled differently than the fixed list, or for PII embedded inside an unrelated field's text value.
- **Root cause:** `aurabackend/shared/pii_masking.py:12`'s `PII_KEYS = {"ssn", "social_security_number", "employee_name", "first_name", "last_name", "phone", "email"}` is a small fixed set. `redact_pii`/`tokenize_pii` only redact a field when its lowercased key is an *exact* match — no substring/regex matching on key names (`"customer_ssn"`, `"billing_email"`, `"dob"`, `"credit_card_number"`, `"address"` all pass through untouched) and no scanning of string values at all (an SSN or email embedded in a free-text `"notes"` field is never touched, since the terminal `else: return data` branch never inspects string content).
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** replaced the exact-match `PII_KEYS` set with `PII_KEYWORDS` (a keyword list) matched as a *substring* of the lowercased key — deliberately erring toward over-redaction (a benign field like `"phone_type"` gets redacted too) since that's the safe direction for a perimeter-defense control. Also added regex-based scanning of string *values* for email, SSN (`\d{3}-\d{2}-\d{4}`), and credit-card-like (13-19 digit) patterns, applied in both `redact_pii` (fixed placeholder) and `tokenize_pii` (also a fixed placeholder for value-embedded matches, since there's no clean `(context, field)` boundary to key an HMAC token to the way a real PII field has — still upholds "raw PII never enters the stream," just not correlatable for embedded matches). Test: extended `tests/test_pii_tokenization.py` with 7 key-variant cases (`customer_ssn`, `billing_email`, `dob`, etc.) and 2 value-scanning cases (one each for `redact_pii`/`tokenize_pii`). Confirmed non-vacuous by stashing the fix — all 9 new cases fail against pre-fix code, the 7 pre-existing cases unaffected — then restoring it and confirming the full `test_pii_tokenization.py` suite (16 tests) plus both production callers' test suites (`test_exception_queue.py`, `test_counterfactual_sprint23.py`, 50 tests) pass with no regressions. PR: pending.

## BUG-116: RateLimitMiddleware trusts the left-most X-Forwarded-For entry, letting any client bypass IP-based throttling (including the auth brute-force bucket)
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/shared/` (`error-handling-pii-ratelimit` group), 2026-09-18.
- **Severity:** high — defeats both the general per-IP rate-limit window and the stricter `auth:<ip>` bucket that exists specifically to throttle password-guessing against `/api/v1/auth/token`; confirmed reachable — `RateLimitMiddleware` is wired into every service via `service_factory.py`, gated only by `settings.rate_limit_enabled` (default true).
- **Root cause:** `aurabackend/shared/middleware.py:348`'s `_client_ip` does `forwarded.split(",")[0].strip()` when `AURA_TRUST_FORWARDED_FOR` is enabled (the documented production configuration behind a reverse proxy) — it trusts the *first* entry of `X-Forwarded-For` as the real client IP. Standard reverse-proxy behavior (e.g. nginx's common `proxy_set_header X-Forwarded-For $proxy_add_x_forwarded_for`) *appends* the proxy's address rather than overwriting the header, so the left-most entry remains exactly what the client sent. A client can set an arbitrary/rotating value as the first entry on every request and get a fresh rate-limit bucket key each time.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** changed `_client_ip` to take the *right-most* entry (`forwarded.split(",")[-1].strip()`) instead of the left-most. This matches the single-reverse-proxy topology this repo actually deploys (one proxy directly in front of one uvicorn worker, per `deploy/`): for that topology the right-most entry is the one the trusted proxy itself appended, and is the only entry not attacker-controlled. There was an **existing test that explicitly asserted the vulnerable left-most behavior as correct** (`test_x_forwarded_for_first_token_used_when_chain_present`, with a comment claiming "the leftmost token is the original client, which is what we want") — renamed and rewrote it to assert the corrected right-most extraction, and added `test_x_forwarded_for_client_cannot_rotate_bucket_via_leftmost_spoofing` reproducing the exact rotation exploit the finding describes (two requests with different attacker-controlled leftmost entries but the same trusted-proxy-appended rightmost entry must resolve to the same rate-limit key). Confirmed non-vacuous by stashing the fix — both new/rewritten cases fail, the rotation test showing two different "client IPs" (`1.2.3.4`, `9.9.9.9`) for what should be the same bucket — then restoring it and confirming the full `test_rate_limit_forwarded_for.py` and `test_rate_limit.py` suites (25 tests) pass, no regressions. PR: #451.

## BUG-117: require_role() is an async factory returning an unusable FastAPI dependency
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/shared/` (`auth-identity-secrets` group), 2026-09-18.
- **Severity:** medium — currently no router calls `require_role()`, so the gap is latent, but it means the only role-based-access-control primitive `auth.py` advertises is non-functional the moment anyone wires it in exactly as documented.
- **Root cause:** `aurabackend/shared/auth.py:126`'s `require_role` is declared `async def require_role(*roles): ... return _check`, making it a coroutine function. Calling `require_role("admin")` (as the module's own usage pattern and `api_gateway/routers/auth.py:152`'s docstring both prescribe, e.g. `Depends(require_role("admin"))`) does not run the factory body synchronously — it returns an un-awaited coroutine object, not the `_check` callable FastAPI's dependency resolver expects. Dependency resolution breaks rather than ever running `_check`, so the role check never executes.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** dropped `async` from the outer `require_role` factory — it does no awaiting itself, only its returned `_check` closure needs to stay `async def` (FastAPI awaits the resolved dependency, not the factory that produces it). Calling `require_role("admin")` now runs immediately and returns the usable `_check` callable. Added `TestRequireRole` to `tests/test_auth.py`: asserts `require_role` itself is not a coroutine function, that calling it returns an awaitable dependency which resolves an allowed role, and that it raises `ForbiddenError` for a disallowed role. Confirmed non-vacuous by stashing the fix — all 3 new tests fail against the pre-fix `async def` (two `assert` failures plus a `TypeError: 'coroutine' object is not callable` on the disallowed-role case) — then restored and confirmed the full `test_auth.py` (17 tests) and `test_tenant_identity.py` (4 tests) pass, no regressions. PR: #453.

## BUG-118: A failed audit-log write is silently dropped — the hash chain continues cleanly, making a lost compliance record undetectable
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/shared/` (`error-handling-pii-ratelimit` group), 2026-09-18.
- **Severity:** medium — defeats the tamper-evidence goal specifically for transient write failures (disk pressure, momentary I/O error); `verify_chain` reports `ok: True` with zero failures even when a real request's compliance record never reached disk.
- **Root cause:** `aurabackend/shared/audit_log.py:132`'s `_AuditWriter.append` catches `OSError` around the file write and only logs it ("audit-write failure must NOT crash the request path"); `self._prev_hash` is left unchanged and the request proceeds normally. Because the JSONL format carries no independent monotonic sequence counter (unlike `audit_ledger.py`'s tenant/seq UNIQUE constraint with bounded retry that ultimately raises on persistent failure), a dropped record leaves the on-disk chain internally consistent — the next record's `prev_hash` correctly points to the last record that *was* written — so there is no way, after the fact, to know a gap exists at all.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** `_AuditWriter` now tracks `_dropped_since_last_success`, incremented on each caught `OSError` (still never raised — the "must not crash the request path" contract is preserved). The next successful write stamps its stable fields with `gap_before: <count>` (only when non-zero, so a clean run's hash format is byte-for-byte unchanged from before) and resets the counter. `verify_chain` now includes the optional `gap_before` field when present in its stable-field hash recomputation, and returns a new `gaps` list (`{line, dropped_records}`) separate from `failures` — a reported gap is honest and hash-chain-consistent, not tampering, so it does not flip `ok` to `False`. Added `tests/test_audit_log_gap_detection.py` (5 tests): a dropped write never reaches disk, the next successful record carries the correct `gap_before`, the counter resets after being reported (two drops → one `gap_before: 2`, not two separate markers), a clean run has no `gap_before` and an unchanged hash format, and `verify_chain` surfaces the gap in `gaps` while keeping `ok: True`/`failures: []`. Confirmed non-vacuous by stashing the fix — 4 of 5 new tests fail with `KeyError: 'gap_before'`/`KeyError: 'gaps'` against pre-fix code (the 5th, proving the drop itself never reaches disk, already held) — then restored and confirmed all 5 plus the full `test_audit_merkle.py` (18 tests) pass, no regressions. PR: #454.

## BUG-119: vault_client.py splices the LIMIT value into SQL via f-string instead of a bound parameter
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/shared/` (`auth-identity-secrets` group), 2026-09-18.
- **Severity:** low — no current caller passes a request-derived `limit` (only fixed literals/tests today), so not yet reachable from an HTTP request, but the method itself has no runtime guard and would become exploitable the moment a route exposes `limit` without its own strict Pydantic/int coercion.
- **Root cause:** `aurabackend/shared/vault_client.py:61`'s `get_top_customers()` and `get_user_vr_path()` build SQL with `f"LIMIT {limit}"`, splicing the `limit` parameter directly into the query string instead of binding it, contrary to `security.md`'s strict-parameter-binding rule. `limit: int` is only a type *hint* — Python does not enforce it at runtime, so a non-integer value forwarded unchecked would concatenate straight into the SQL text.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** both call sites now bind `limit` as a positional `$N` parameter, matching the placeholder style the rest of the file already uses for other values (e.g. `user_id = $1`). `get_top_customers()`: `"...ORDER BY total_spend DESC LIMIT $1"` with `params=[limit]`. `get_user_vr_path()`: `"...LIMIT $2"` with `params=[user_id, limit]`. Added two regression tests to `tests/test_vault_client.py` (`test_get_top_customers_binds_limit_not_splices_it`, `test_get_user_vr_path_binds_limit_not_splices_it`) that inspect the actual SQL string + params tuple passed to the mocked adapter and assert the literal limit value never appears in the query text. Confirmed non-vacuous by stashing the fix — both fail against pre-fix code, one literally showing `LIMIT 42` spliced into the recovered query string — then restored and confirmed the full `test_vault_client.py` (27 tests) passes, no regressions. `ruff --fix` also removed pre-existing unused imports (`os`, `uuid` in `vault_client.py`; several unused test imports) while the files were open, unrelated to this fix. Also folds in the BUG-118 PR-link doc update that was pending after that fix merged. PR: #455.

## BUG-120: ETL `add_column`/`filter` transform steps bypass the SQL-expression guard entirely — arbitrary file read / SSRF via `POST /etl/execute`
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/api_gateway/routers/` (rotation re-run, diffed forward from `c00cbd6`), 2026-09-19. 3 review lenses converged on this independently; adversarial-verify: 8/8 skeptics did not refute (one skeptic's report referenced a wrong/stale checkout path and is disregarded — the other 8, plus my own direct read, confirm the code as described).
- **Severity:** critical — arbitrary local file read (including other tenants' uploaded files on shared storage) and SSRF, reachable by any authenticated caller.
- **Root cause:** `aurabackend/api_gateway/routers/etl.py:103`'s `_build_transform_sql` calls `_validate_custom_sql` (aliased from `shared.sql_expression_guard.validate_sql_expression`, the BUG-053/082/083/114 guard) only in the `custom_sql` branch (line 252). Two sibling branches splice caller-controlled SQL text with **no guard call at all**: the `filter` step's `condition` (line 122, spliced into `WHERE {condition}`) and the `add_column` step's `expression` (line 147, spliced into `({expr}) AS "{col_name}"`). The DuckDB connection used here has no `enable_external_access` restriction, so an unguarded expression can use `read_csv_auto`/`read_parquet`/`ATTACH`/`httpfs`/a bare-string replacement-scan to read arbitrary local files or make outbound network calls. `pipeline/engine.py`'s sibling ADD_COLUMN/CUSTOM_SQL implementation *does* call the guard for both step types (confirming add_column was meant to be guarded here too), but this router's own `add_column`/`filter` branches were simply never wired to it — predating and postdating BUG-053/082/083/114 unguarded. Reachable directly via `POST /etl/execute` (`etl_router` is included in `main.py`; `pipeline.transforms` is an unvalidated caller-supplied list) and indirectly via `POST /etl/natural-language`-generated transforms fed back into the same endpoint.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing; distinct from and not covered by BUG-053 (etl.py `custom_sql` only), BUG-082/083 (a different file, `pipeline/engine.py`), or BUG-114 (hardened the guard's own regex, touched no call site).
- **Fix:** added `_validate_custom_sql(condition)` to the `filter` branch and `_validate_custom_sql(expr)` to the `add_column` branch, both called before the value is spliced into the CTE SQL — the exact same guard `custom_sql` already used, applied consistently across all three branches that accept caller-supplied SQL text. Extended `tests/test_etl_custom_sql_sandbox.py` (now 21 tests) with `test_build_transform_sql_rejects_malicious_add_column_step` and `test_build_transform_sql_rejects_malicious_filter_step` (each reproduces the audit's exact exploit shape end-to-end through `_build_transform_sql`, the function `/etl/execute` actually calls) plus `test_build_transform_sql_allows_benign_add_column_step`/`_filter_step` guarding against over-blocking ordinary conditions/expressions. Confirmed non-vacuous by stashing the fix — both malicious-payload tests fail with "DID NOT RAISE" against pre-fix code, proving the exploit reached `_build_transform_sql` untouched — then restored and confirmed the full sandbox suite (21 tests) plus adjacent `test_etl_download_tenant_isolation.py`/`test_etl_preview_source_traversal.py`/`test_etl_storage_backend.py`/`test_pipeline_execution.py` (48 tests total) pass, no regressions. PR: pending.

## BUG-121: ETL transform column names spliced as raw f-strings instead of through the shared identifier quoter — identifier-injection
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/api_gateway/routers/` (rotation re-run), 2026-09-19. Adversarial-verify: 3/3 skeptics did not refute.
- **Severity:** high — reachable via the same `POST /etl/execute` endpoint as BUG-120; requires a caller-controlled string containing an embedded double-quote to reach `_build_transform_sql`, which every affected branch permits.
- **Root cause:** `aurabackend/api_gateway/routers/etl.py:103`'s `_build_transform_sql` uses `_q()` (`shared.sql_identifiers.quote_identifier`) only for table/CTE references (`_q(prev)`, `_q(table)`). Every column-name string drawn from the transform step's `config` dict is instead spliced via raw f-string double-quoting instead of `_q()`: `rename`'s mappings (line 130), `drop_columns`' columns (line 138), `add_column`'s `name` (line 147), `sort`'s column (line 157), `aggregate`'s group_by/alias (lines 166-168), `deduplicate`'s columns (line 173), `cast_type`'s column (line 187), and `fill_missing`'s column/generated replace expressions (lines 210-237). `shared/sql_identifiers.py`'s own docstring exists precisely to prevent this: "Splicing them raw lets a crafted header like `amt\" ; ATTACH ...` ... break out of the identifier and inject SQL." A column name containing an embedded `"` (attacker-controlled directly via the `ETLTransformStep` payload, or indirectly via a column name from an uploaded CSV that an LLM-generated transform then references) breaks out of the quoted identifier and injects arbitrary SQL into the CTE chain.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing; distinct from BUG-053 (custom_sql expression guard, not identifier quoting) and the repo-wide table-name quoting sweep (BUG-020, 7 other call sites, none in this file).
- **Fix:** replaced every raw `f'"{col}"'`-style splice with `_q(col)` (`quote_identifier`) across all eight affected branches: `rename`'s old/new mappings, `drop_columns`' EXCLUDE list, `add_column`'s output name, `sort`'s ORDER BY column, `aggregate`'s group_by/column/alias, `deduplicate`'s PARTITION BY columns, `cast_type`'s column, and `fill_missing`'s both the explicit-column and `*`-schema-driven (DESCRIBE-derived) branches. Added `tests/test_etl_transform_identifier_injection.py` (9 tests) that drive `_build_transform_sql` end-to-end against a **real DuckDB connection** (not just string-shape assertions) with a quote-breaking column name (`evil" ; SELECT 1337 AS pwned; --`) through every affected step type, proving the malicious name round-trips as a literal, safely-quoted identifier rather than breaking out. Confirmed non-vacuous by stashing the fix — 6 of 9 tests fail against pre-fix code with `duckdb.ParserException: syntax error at or near ";"` (the exact signature of the identifier breaking out and the trailing text becoming bare, unparseable SQL — the other 3 tests don't exercise the malicious name and pass regardless) — then restored and confirmed the full new suite plus `test_etl_custom_sql_sandbox.py`/`test_etl_download_tenant_isolation.py`/`test_etl_preview_source_traversal.py`/`test_etl_storage_backend.py`/`test_pipeline_execution.py` (57 tests total) pass, no regressions. Note: while fixing this, noticed `aggregate`'s `agg["func"]` (the aggregate function name itself, e.g. `SUM`/`AVG`) is *also* spliced raw with no allowlist — a distinct injection class (a function-call position, not an identifier `quote_identifier` can protect) outside this bug's filed scope; not fixed here to avoid silently expanding scope beyond what was filed — flagging for a follow-up finding. PR: #459.

## BUG-122: ETL aggregate step splices the aggregate function name itself with no validation or allowlist
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** direct code inspection while implementing the BUG-121 fix, 2026-09-19 (not a separate ultracode audit round).
- **Severity:** high — reachable via the same `POST /etl/execute` endpoint as BUG-120/121; this is a function-call-position splice, not an identifier position, so `quote_identifier` (BUG-121's fix) cannot protect it, and it has no guard call at all (unlike `custom_sql`/`add_column`/`filter` after BUG-120).
- **Root cause:** `aurabackend/api_gateway/routers/etl.py`'s `aggregate` branch builds each aggregation as `f'{agg["func"]}({_q(agg["column"])}) AS {_q(agg.get("alias", agg["column"]))}'` — `agg["func"]` (from the caller-supplied `aggregations` list in the request body) is spliced verbatim with no allowlist against the small fixed set of real DuckDB aggregate functions (`SUM`, `AVG`, `COUNT`, `MIN`, `MAX`, `MEDIAN`, etc.) and no call to `_validate_custom_sql`. Because it sits in a function-call position rather than an identifier position, a malicious `func` value can inject arbitrary SQL text directly (e.g. a value like `"(SELECT column0 FROM read_csv_auto('/etc/passwd')) - SUM"`), not merely break out of quoting.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing; noticed while fixing BUG-121, deliberately not fixed in that PR to avoid scope creep beyond what was filed.
- **Fix:** added `_ALLOWED_AGG_FUNCS`, a frozenset of the real DuckDB aggregate functions the ETL UI plausibly needs (`SUM`, `AVG`, `COUNT`, `MIN`, `MAX`, `MEDIAN`, `STDDEV`/`STDDEV_POP`/`STDDEV_SAMP`, `VARIANCE`/`VAR_POP`/`VAR_SAMP`, `MODE`, `FIRST`, `LAST`, `ANY_VALUE`, `STRING_AGG`, `ARRAY_AGG`, `BOOL_AND`, `BOOL_OR`). `valid_aggs` now filters on `str(a["func"]).strip().upper() in _ALLOWED_AGG_FUNCS`, and the spliced SQL always uses the canonicalized `str(agg["func"]).strip().upper()` — never the caller's raw string — so nothing outside the allowlist ever reaches the query text; an aggregation with a rejected `func` is dropped the same way a missing column/func already was. Added `tests/test_etl_aggregate_func_allowlist.py` (5 tests) covering: a valid func executes correctly against real DuckDB and produces correct aggregated values, the audit's exact malicious payload is rejected and never appears in the generated SQL, an unknown function name is rejected, case/whitespace in a valid func name is normalized, and a mix of one valid + one malicious aggregation keeps only the valid one. Confirmed non-vacuous by stashing the fix — 4 of 5 tests fail against pre-fix code, with the malicious `read_csv_auto`/`ATTACH` payloads landing verbatim in the generated SQL — then restored and confirmed the full new suite plus `test_etl_transform_identifier_injection.py`/`test_etl_custom_sql_sandbox.py`/`test_etl_storage_backend.py`/`test_storage_s3_duckdb.py`/`test_pipeline_execution.py` (58 tests total) pass, no regressions. This closes the last open finding from this rotation's `api_gateway/routers/` re-audit (BUG-120..122). PR: #461.

## BUG-123: start_pipeline's mid-STARTING race is unguarded on delete/stop/pause/resume — orphans an unstoppable background task
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/pipeline/streaming/` (rotation re-run, diffed forward from `95e236d`), 2026-09-19. Adversarial-verify: 3/3 skeptics did not refute.
- **Severity:** high — reachable via the live `POST/DELETE /pipelines/{id}` endpoints; a leaked engine holds live source/sink connections (DB, httpx, file handles) forever with no reference anywhere that could stop it.
- **Root cause:** `aurabackend/pipeline/streaming/streaming_api.py`'s `start_pipeline` holds `_start_lock_for(pipeline_id)` (the BUG-089 fix) across constructing the engine, storing it in `_engines[pipeline_id]`, and `await engine.start()` — but `StreamingEngine.start()` sets `status = STARTING` and then hits real `await` points (source/sink `.start()`, the checkpoint-load thread offload) before ever reaching `RUNNING` and spawning its background tasks. `delete_pipeline`/`stop_pipeline`/`pause_pipeline`/`resume_pipeline` never acquire `_start_lock_for`, and `delete_pipeline`'s only guard (`status == RUNNING`) does not cover `STARTING`. A `DELETE` landing during that window passes the guard, pops `_pipelines`/`_engines` for that id, and returns 200 — but the suspended `start_pipeline` call still holds its own local `engine`/`pipe` references, resumes, flips to `RUNNING`, and spawns `_ingest_task`/`_run_loop` as detached `asyncio.create_task()`s with zero reachable reference in `_engines` anymore. This is the exact "leaked unstoppable background task" class BUG-089 fixed for concurrent duplicate starts, reopened through the four endpoints the new lock never covers.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing; BUG-089's fix was scoped only to `start_pipeline`'s own check-construct-start sequence.
- **Fix:** `delete_pipeline`/`stop_pipeline`/`pause_pipeline`/`resume_pipeline` all now acquire `_start_lock_for(pipeline_id)` (the same lock `start_pipeline` holds) around their status check + `_engines` read/mutation, so none of them can act on a pipeline while a start is still suspended mid-`STARTING` — they simply queue behind the in-flight start and see the real, final state once it completes. Added `TestStreamingAPIStartDeleteRace` to `tests/test_streaming.py` (2 tests): `test_delete_cannot_race_a_mid_start_engine` monkeypatches `SimulatedSource.start` to suspend on a controlled `asyncio.Event`, confirms a concurrent `delete_pipeline` call does *not* complete while the start is suspended and does not remove `_pipelines`/`_engines` entries prematurely, then releases the start and confirms delete correctly 409s (proving it queued behind the lock rather than racing past a stale check) with the started engine still intact and stoppable; `test_stop_cannot_race_a_mid_start_engine` proves the analogous case for `stop_pipeline`, confirming it succeeds against the real post-start engine once queued rather than 404ing on a stale/absent lookup. Confirmed non-vacuous by stashing the fix — both new tests fail (`delete_pipeline`/`stop_pipeline` complete immediately instead of blocking) against pre-fix code — then restored and confirmed the full `test_streaming.py` suite (73 tests) passes, no regressions. PR: #463.

## BUG-124: FileWatcherSource's directory scan still runs synchronously on the event loop every poll cycle
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/pipeline/streaming/` (rotation re-run), 2026-09-19. Adversarial-verify: 3/3 skeptics did not refute.
- **Severity:** high — this deployment runs a single uvicorn worker; a blocking call here freezes every concurrent tenant's request for its duration, on every poll cycle (default 2s) for every running file-watcher pipeline, worsening as the watch directory grows.
- **Root cause:** BUG-087 wrapped only `_parse_file` in `asyncio.to_thread` inside `aurabackend/pipeline/streaming/sources/file_watcher.py`'s `read_batch()`. The directory listing that precedes it — `watch_path.exists()` and `for f in watch_path.glob(self.pattern)` — remains a plain synchronous filesystem call executed directly on the event loop, both in `read_batch()` (lines 60-70) and in `start()`'s initial seen-files scan (lines 41-44). BUG-087's own fix text confirms the scope was intentionally narrow ("no change needed" beyond the parse call) — the scan that gates it was never covered.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing; BUG-087's fix only wrapped the parse step.
- **Fix:** extracted the directory listing into `_scan_dir()` and wrapped every call site in `asyncio.to_thread`, matching BUG-087's own pattern for the parse step: `start()`'s initial seen-files scan and `read_batch()`'s per-cycle scan both now offload. Simplified `read_batch()` along the way — since `_scan_dir()` already returns `[]` when the directory doesn't exist, the old separate `if not watch_path.exists(): sleep; return []` early-return was redundant with the existing "no pending events" sleep-and-return at the bottom, so it's gone (pure refactor, verified behavior-identical via the new `test_seen_files_scan_result_is_unaffected_by_offloading` test). Extended `tests/test_streaming.py::TestFileWatcherSource`: updated the existing BUG-087 offload-spy test to expect `[_scan_dir, _parse_file]` instead of `[_parse_file]` alone, and added `test_directory_scan_is_offloaded_to_a_thread` (spies on `asyncio.to_thread`, confirms `_scan_dir` is dispatched through it exactly twice — once in `start()`, once in `read_batch()` — even with zero files present) plus the behavior-parity test above. Confirmed non-vacuous by stashing the fix — both new/updated assertions fail against pre-fix code (`AttributeError: no attribute '_scan_dir'` and a count mismatch) — then restored and confirmed the full `test_streaming.py` suite (75 tests) passes, no regressions. PR: #464.

## BUG-125: Late-event window merge (BUG-092 fix) only covers TUMBLING windows — SLIDING and SESSION still double-fire
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/pipeline/streaming/` (rotation re-run), 2026-09-19. Adversarial-verify: 3/3 skeptics did not refute.
- **Severity:** high — a legitimate, documented pipeline config (`streaming_api.py` ships a built-in template using `{"type": "session", "late_data_policy": "update"}`) reaches this exact gap with real user input, causing every append-only sink (database/file/webhook/alert) to record the same window twice with disjoint partial aggregates.
- **Root cause:** `aurabackend/pipeline/streaming/window_processor.py`'s `_assign_tumbling()` checks `self._closed_by_key` and reopens/merges into the original `WindowState` when a late `update`-policy event targets an already-fired window (the BUG-092 fix). `_assign_sliding()` and `_assign_session()` do not perform this check — both unconditionally create a brand-new, empty `WindowState` via `if win_key not in self._windows: self._windows[win_key] = WindowState(...)`. `_closed_by_key` is populated generically in `_fire_ready_windows` for any fired window type, but the sliding/session assignment functions were simply never wired to check it — worse for session windows, whose non-SESSION branch of `_fire_ready_windows` never even populates `_closed_by_key` for them in the first place. BUG-092's own registry text explicitly scopes its fix to `_assign_tumbling` only.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing; BUG-092's fix covered only one of the three window types that can reopen via late `update` events.
- **Fix:** ported BUG-092's reopen-and-merge check to `_assign_sliding()` (checks `self._closed_by_key` before creating a fresh `WindowState`, identical pattern to `_assign_tumbling`) and to `_assign_session()` (extended its existing session-lookup loop with a second pass over `_closed_by_key` for sessions matching the same key whose gap window still covers the late event, reopening and extending them). Also fixed the deeper session-specific gap: `_fire_ready_windows`'s `SESSION` branch never populated `_closed_by_key` at all (only the tumbling/sliding branch did) — added `self._closed_by_key[ws.window_key] = ws` there too, since without it even a correct reopen check in `_assign_session` would have found nothing to reopen. Added `test_late_data_update_sliding` (uses `slide_seconds == size_seconds` to isolate the fix from multi-window overlap) and `test_late_data_update_session` to `tests/test_streaming.py::TestWindowProcessor`, both mirroring the existing `test_late_data_update` (tumbling) pattern. Confirmed non-vacuous by stashing the fix — both fail against pre-fix code (sliding: `event_count` stays `1` instead of merging to `2`; session: the late event's fired window has a different `window_key` than the original, proving it started a disjoint new session instead of merging) — then restored and confirmed the full `test_streaming.py` suite (77 tests) passes, no regressions. PR: #465.

## BUG-126: Failed pipeline start leaks already-opened sink resources with no cleanup path, and a retry loses the reference
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/pipeline/streaming/` (rotation re-run), 2026-09-19. Adversarial-verify: 3/3 skeptics did not refute.
- **Severity:** high — each failed-start-then-retry cycle permanently leaks one more open connection (httpx client, DB connection) with no code path that can ever close it.
- **Root cause:** `aurabackend/pipeline/streaming/streaming_api.py`'s `start_pipeline` registers the new engine in `_engines[pipeline_id]` before awaiting `engine.start()`, and `StreamingEngine.start()` has no cleanup on partial failure: if one sink's `start()` succeeds (e.g. `WebhookSink` opening an `httpx.AsyncClient`, or `DatabaseSink` opening a DB connection) but a later step throws, the `except` block only sets `status = FAILED` and re-raises — it never calls `stop()` or closes the sinks that already started (and `stop()` itself is gated to only act on `RUNNING`/`PAUSED`, so even calling it afterward would be a no-op). The exception propagates unhandled out of the route with no surrounding `try`/`except`. Because status is now `FAILED` (not `RUNNING`), a retry of `POST /pipelines/{id}/start` is legal and constructs a brand-new engine, overwriting `_engines[pipeline_id]` and permanently losing the only reference to the first engine's open resources.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing; distinct from BUG-089 (a concurrent-race fix, not partial-failure cleanup).
- **Fix:** `StreamingEngine.start()`'s except block now closes whatever already started (every constructed sink's `.stop()`, then the source's `.stop()` if it was created) before flipping `status = FAILED` and re-raising — mirroring `stop()`'s own close loop. Each sink type's `stop()` was verified safe to call even on a sink whose `start()` was never reached (all guard on `if self._conn`/`if self._client`/an empty buffer, or are unconditionally trivial), so calling it on the full `self._sinks` list — including sinks constructed but not yet started when an earlier one failed — is safe. Added `test_failed_start_closes_already_opened_sinks` to `tests/test_streaming.py::TestStreamingEngine`: a two-sink pipeline (`WebhookSink` then a `ConsoleSink` whose `start()` is monkeypatched to raise) confirms the already-started `WebhookSink`'s `httpx.AsyncClient` is closed (`._client is None`) after the failed `start()` call, not left open. Confirmed non-vacuous by stashing the fix — the test fails against pre-fix code with the real `httpx.AsyncClient` object still present instead of `None` — then restored and confirmed the full `test_streaming.py` suite (78 tests) passes, no regressions. Also records BUG-125's PR link (#465). PR: #466.

## BUG-127: Raw exception text stored in StreamMetrics.errors is returned unsanitized to API callers
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/pipeline/streaming/` (rotation re-run), 2026-09-19. Adversarial-verify: 3/3 skeptics did not refute.
- **Severity:** high — CWE-209 internal-detail leak (e.g. DB DSNs/paths from connection failures, missing-key errors) through a normal polling endpoint reachable without authentication in dev/open mode, not merely an error path.
- **Root cause:** `aurabackend/pipeline/streaming/streaming_engine.py`'s `start()` and `_run_loop()` except blocks both do `self._metrics.errors.append(str(e))`, storing the raw exception string on the shared `StreamMetrics` object. `aurabackend/pipeline/streaming/streaming_api.py` then serializes that object verbatim via `GET /pipelines` (list), `GET /pipelines/{id}`, and `GET /pipelines/{id}/metrics` — none of which route through `shared/error_handler.py::sanitize_error`, contrary to `security.md`'s "never proxy a raw upstream/engine error string to a client" rule.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing; BUG-080's tenant-ownership fix for these same endpoints enforces *access* to the pipeline, not sanitization of its metrics payload.
- **Fix:** both `except` blocks in `streaming_engine.py` now call `sanitize_error(e, context=...)` instead of `str(e)` before appending to `self._metrics.errors`; sanitize_error already logs the full traceback server-side, so no diagnostic detail is lost. PR: #467.

## BUG-128: FileSink.start() calls os.makedirs() synchronously in an async function
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/pipeline/streaming/` (rotation re-run), 2026-09-19. Adversarial-verify: 3/3 skeptics did not refute.
- **Severity:** medium — lower-frequency than BUG-085/086's per-write offloads (only runs once per pipeline start), but the same blocking-call-on-the-shared-event-loop class, worse on a slow or network-mounted output directory.
- **Root cause:** `aurabackend/pipeline/streaming/sinks/file_sink.py`'s `start()` calls `os.makedirs(self._output_dir, exist_ok=True)` directly with no `asyncio.to_thread` wrapper, even though the sibling write path in `_flush()` was correctly offloaded under BUG-086. BUG-086's fix text explicitly scopes itself to `_flush()` only.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing; BUG-086's fix didn't cover this call site.
- **Fix:** `start()` now awaits `asyncio.to_thread(os.makedirs, ...)`. PR: #468.

## BUG-129: DatabaseSink docstring/label claims "upsert" but emit_window() only ever INSERTs — duplicate rows for a re-fired window
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/pipeline/streaming/` (rotation re-run), 2026-09-19. Adversarial-verify: 3/3 skeptics did not refute.
- **Severity:** medium — combines with any legitimate window re-fire (including BUG-092's own now-fixed tumbling reopen path, or BUG-125 above) to silently double-count aggregations for any consumer summing/joining on `window_key`.
- **Root cause:** `aurabackend/pipeline/streaming/sinks/database_sink.py`'s module docstring ("Upsert window results into a relational table") implies idempotent writes keyed by window, but `emit_window()` always executes a plain `INSERT` (both the DuckDB f-string branch and the `_PG_INSERT` Postgres branch) with no `ON CONFLICT`/dedup key, and neither backend's `CREATE TABLE` DDL even defines a unique constraint on `window_key` to make an upsert enforceable — violating `backend.md`'s "Idempotent data inputs" rule.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** added a `UNIQUE (pipeline_id, window_key)` constraint to both backends' `CREATE TABLE`, and both `INSERT`s now carry `ON CONFLICT (pipeline_id, window_key) DO UPDATE SET ...` so a window re-fire replaces its row instead of duplicating it. PR: #469.

## BUG-130: FileWatcherSource._seen_files grows without bound for the life of the pipeline
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/pipeline/streaming/` (rotation re-run), 2026-09-19. Adversarial-verify: 3/3 skeptics did not refute.
- **Severity:** medium — same unbounded-growth class BUG-091 fixed for `AlertSink._fired`, but left uncovered here; also bloats the on-disk checkpoint file over the pipeline's lifetime since the full set is serialized into every periodic checkpoint.
- **Root cause:** `aurabackend/pipeline/streaming/sources/file_watcher.py`'s `self._seen_files: Set[str] = set()` accumulates every filename ever observed by the poll-cycle glob scan and is never pruned or capped, unlike `alert_sink.py`'s `_fired` deque (BUG-091's fix, bounded via `maxlen`). `get_offsets()` serializes the full, ever-growing set into every periodic checkpoint write.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing; BUG-091's fix was scoped to `alert_sink.py` only.
- **Fix:** added a `_mark_seen()` helper backed by the set (for O(1) membership) plus an insertion-order `deque` — once `max_seen_files` (config option, default 10000) is reached, the oldest filename is evicted from both before the new one is added, mirroring `AlertSink._fired`'s bound. `get_offsets()`'s serialized payload is now capped by construction. PR: #470.

## BUG-131: _start_locks dict grows unbounded — one asyncio.Lock leaked per pipeline_id ever started, never pruned on delete
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/pipeline/streaming/` (rotation re-run), 2026-09-19. Adversarial-verify: 3/3 skeptics did not refute.
- **Severity:** low — a bare `asyncio.Lock` is a small object; only matters under long-running single-process deployments with heavy pipeline create/delete churn (demos, CI, tenant turnover).
- **Root cause:** `aurabackend/pipeline/streaming/streaming_api.py`'s `_start_lock_for()` lazily creates and caches an `asyncio.Lock` per `pipeline_id` in the module-level `_start_locks` dict, introduced by BUG-089's fix — but nothing ever removes an entry, not even `delete_pipeline`, which does clean up `_pipelines` and `_engines` for the same id.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing; BUG-089's fix added the lock registry but no corresponding eviction.
- **Fix:** `delete_pipeline` now pops `_start_locks[pipeline_id]` right after releasing the lock it held across the delete. Safe because any coroutine already waiting on that specific `Lock` object still holds its own reference and completes normally — only a later `_start_lock_for()` call for the (now-deleted) id gets a fresh `Lock`. PR: #471.

## BUG-132: test_audit_wiring_creates_job_and_stores_result races its own background job against a per-request TestClient portal teardown
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** observed as a real (non-reaper) failure on a full-suite pre-push run, `AssertionError: None` — passed in isolation, failed under the full suite, the classic order/timing-dependence symptom `testing.md` calls out.
- **Severity:** medium — test-only defect (no production code path involved), but a structural race, not a load-proportional flake: it fails whenever the background job doesn't finish inside the one HTTP request's ASGI cycle, and passing "most of the time" was luck, not correctness.
- **Root cause:** `tests/test_audit_endpoint.py`'s `_client()` helper builds `TestClient(app, headers=...)` but never enters it as a context manager (`with TestClient(...) as c:`). Starlette's `TestClient._portal_factory()` (`.venv/Lib/site-packages/starlette/testclient.py:414-419`) only reuses one persistent portal/event loop across calls when `self.portal` was set by `__enter__` — otherwise every single request gets a brand-new portal. The endpoint's `POST /counterfactual/audit` schedules its job via a bare `asyncio.create_task(...)` (not awaited before the response returns), so that task lives on the POST request's own portal-loop; the instant that request's ASGI cycle finishes, its portal (and the task riding on it) is torn down. Whether the job actually finishes writing `_jobs[job_id]["state"] = "succeeded"` into the shared module-level dict before that teardown is a genuine race — not proportional to system load in the way a timeout-based flake is, just increasingly likely to lose as anything (executor thread scheduling, GIL contention from a large concurrent suite) slows the job down even slightly. Every later polling `GET` runs in its OWN fresh portal too, so once the original task is gone, no amount of extra polling time can recover it — the test's up-to-10s retry loop cannot mask this class of loss, only shrink the window in which it's exposed.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing test-harness bug; unrelated to any of this session's `pipeline/streaming` fixes.
- **Fix:** the test now does `with TestClient(m.app, headers=_auth()) as c:` around both the POST and the polling loop, so one persistent portal/event loop backs every call — exactly matching how a real long-lived uvicorn worker keeps one event loop across requests, letting the background task actually run to completion regardless of how slow any individual call is. Confirmed against the actual observed pre-fix full-suite failure (this exact `AssertionError: None`); re-ran the fixed test 3x locally plus the full `test_audit_endpoint.py` file (8/8 pass). PR: #472.

## BUG-133: Default landing chat panel never falls back off Commander when it's disabled — every query on the app's first screen renders a static "offline" message
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode completeness/UX audit (product-completeness slot, added 2026-09-21 to the standing loop rotation, see `## Audit coverage`). Adversarial-verify: 3/3 skeptics did not refute.
- **Severity:** critical — this is the app's default landing view; in the default/live config (no env overrides) no user can get a real answer from the primary chat surface at all.
- **Root cause:** `frontend/src/workbench/Workbench.tsx` defaults `nav` to `'Cockpit'` and unconditionally renders `AskAuraChat`, which is wired exclusively to `chatService.streamMessage` (`POST /chat/stream`, the Commander SSE endpoint). `AURA_COMMANDER_ENABLED` defaults `False` (`aurabackend/shared/config.py:288`), so the backend 404s (`aurabackend/api_gateway/routers/chat.py:681-682`) and `streamMessage` throws `Error('commander_disabled')` — its own docstring says this exists "so callers can fall back to sendMessage," but `AskAuraChat.ask()`'s catch block only ever called `describeChatError(err)`, which has no branch for `'commander_disabled'` and falls through to a generic "Commander offline" string. No SQL, no execution, no chart, no narration — ever, for any question, on the app's first screen, in the default configuration. A prior fix to this same catch block (BUG-028) differentiated 401/403/5xx/abort message text but explicitly left the 404/disabled case as the same static offline message, never adding the fallback the API layer's own docstring promised.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing; BUG-028's fix improved message differentiation but didn't add the fallback call.
- **Fix:** `AskAuraChat.ask()`'s catch block now special-cases `err.message === 'commander_disabled'`: it calls `chatService.sendMessage(q)` (the always-live `POST /chat` path) and maps the response into the same `sql`/`critic`/`columns`/`rows`/`answer` fields the streaming path populates, mirroring `AskAuraPanel.tsx`'s existing `sendMessage`-consuming pattern. All other error branches (401/403, 5xx, network failure, abort) are unchanged. New tests in `frontend/src/workbench/cockpit/__tests__/AskAuraChat.test.tsx` cover: a successful fallback showing a real answer/SQL, a fallback query that itself fails showing the real server error (not a fake offline message), and a genuine non-commander error still showing the offline message. Confirmed non-vacuous via `git stash` (2/3 new tests fail against pre-fix code, showing the static "Commander offline" text instead of a real answer/error). Full `frontend/src/workbench` suite (76 tests, including the pre-existing BUG-028 tests) passes; `tsc -b` and `eslint --max-warnings 0` clean. PR: #475.

## BUG-134: Backend produces a real chart on every successful query, but no live chat surface ever renders it
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode completeness/UX audit (product-completeness slot, added 2026-09-21 to the standing loop rotation). Adversarial-verify: 3/3 skeptics did not refute.
- **Severity:** high — the "NL → SQL → chart → narrated answer" pipeline advertised throughout the product is real end-to-end on the backend and completely invisible to every user, in every live configuration.
- **Root cause:** `aurabackend/agents/langgraph_orchestrator.py`'s `visualization_node` genuinely builds a `ChartSpec` (type/x/y/title), and `aurabackend/api_gateway/routers/chat.py` attaches it to every successful response as `execution_result.chart_spec`. `frontend/src/components/RechartsVisualization.tsx` is the only component in the repo built to render one — and it was imported by nothing outside its own test file. None of the three live chat/query surfaces (`AskAuraPanel.tsx`, `AskAuraChat.tsx`, `QueryPanel.tsx`) ever read `chart_spec`/`chartSpec` off the response; each only rendered SQL text and a raw row table.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing; the component and the backend field both shipped, but nothing was ever wired between them.
- **Fix:** wired `RechartsVisualization` into all three live consumers — `AskAuraPanel.tsx` (new `chartSpec` field on its `Msg` type, populated from `er.chart_spec`), `AskAuraChat.tsx` (extended `cockpit/types.ts`'s shared `Msg` type with `data`/`chartSpec`, populated on the `sendMessage` fallback path added by BUG-133 — Commander's own SSE protocol carries no chart data), and `QueryPanel.tsx` (reads `execution_result.chart_spec`/`.data` directly). Each render is gated on `chartSpec.type !== 'table'` (the component's own no-op case) and a non-empty `data` array. New tests: `AskAuraPanel.test.tsx`, `AskAuraChat.test.tsx`, and `QueryPanel.test.tsx` each gained a case asserting the chart's title actually renders from a real `chart_spec`. Confirmed non-vacuous via `git stash` (all 3 new tests fail against pre-fix code; 8 pre-existing tests in the same files still pass). `tsc -b` and `eslint --max-warnings 0` clean on all seven touched files; full `frontend/src/workbench` + `src/terminal` + `src/components` suite (206 tests) passes except one pre-existing, unrelated order-dependent flake in `CertificatesPanel.test.tsx` (filed separately as BUG-135). PR: #476.

## BUG-135: CertificatesPanel.test.tsx's error-state test is order-dependent — passes alone, fails in the full frontend suite
- **Status:** fixed (hardened; not reproduced)
- **Found by:** observed while verifying BUG-134's fix — `npx vitest run src/workbench src/terminal src/components` failed 2 tests in `CertificatesPanel.test.tsx`, both passing when the file is run in isolation (`npx vitest run src/workbench/panels/__tests__/CertificatesPanel.test.tsx`, 4/4 pass).
- **Severity:** medium — test-only defect (no evidence yet of a production code path involved), but per `testing.md`'s own rule a test that passes alone and fails in the suite is consuming state some earlier test left behind, not a quirk to shrug off.
- **Root cause:** most likely a test race (not reproduced, so not proven): `CertificatesPanel.verify` sets `status='error'` and `error=<message>` as two separate state updates, and the test's `waitFor` asserted only the title, then asserted the detail text synchronously. If the title renders a commit before the detail, the second assertion fails exactly as reported (title found, detail missing) -- a load-dependent window, which fits 'passes alone, fails among ~200 tests'. Original notes:  `it('shows an honest error state...')` gets as far as `screen.getByText(/verification failed/i)` (the `waitFor` succeeds) but then `screen.getByText(/HTTP 404: unknown record/)` fails to find the mocked error detail text — suggests either a leaked mock/fetch state from an earlier test file in the same run, or a timing race specific to running alongside ~200 other tests. Not yet isolated to a specific earlier test or shared module-level state.
- **Caused by:** none identified yet — pre-existing, unrelated to any of this session's chat/chart fixes (confirmed via `git diff --stat` showing zero changes to `CertificatesPanel.tsx`/`.test.tsx`).
- **Fix:** both assertions now sit inside the same `waitFor`. Verified only weakly: the broad suite (`src/workbench src/terminal src/components`, 245 tests) passed twice here before the change, so the failure did not reproduce; this hardens the most plausible cause rather than proving a cure. If it recurs, merge status+error into one state in the panel. PR #511.


## BUG-136: A query that legitimately returns zero rows is displayed to the user as a failure
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode completeness/UX audit (product-completeness slot, added 2026-09-21 to the standing loop rotation). Adversarial-verify: 3/3 skeptics did not refute.
- **Severity:** high — any question whose correct answer is "no matching rows" (a filter with no matches, a date range with no activity) is surfaced to the user as an error even though the query ran perfectly and correctly found nothing.
- **Root cause:** `aurabackend/api_gateway/routers/chat.py`'s `chat_endpoint` only set `execution_result.success = True` inside `if state.execution and state.execution.row_count > 0:` — a genuinely successful zero-row execution left `success` at its default `False` with no `error` set. `visualization_node`/`analysis_node` both correctly skip on `row_count == 0` (no chart/conclusion needed for an empty result), which is fine on its own, but the frontend's `ok = resp.status !== 'Error' && er.success !== false` computation (in `AskAuraPanel.tsx`, and mirrored in `AskAuraChat.tsx`'s fallback path added by BUG-133) reads `success` directly, so it fell back to the hardcoded literal `'Query failed.'` string for a query that never actually failed.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** the gate is now just `if state.execution:` — `execution_node` (`aurabackend/agents/langgraph_orchestrator.py`) only ever sets `state.execution` when the SQL ran without error (a failure returns via `_err()` and leaves it `None`), so presence alone is the correct success signal, and `columns` come from the cursor description (populated regardless of row count) so they still round-trip on an empty result. No frontend change needed — both `AskAuraPanel.tsx` and `AskAuraChat.tsx`'s `ok` computations were already correct given a truthful `success` flag. New test `test_chat_zero_row_result_is_a_success_not_a_failure` in `tests/test_e2e_chat.py` monkeypatches `run_orchestrator` to return a real, successfully-executed zero-row `OrchestratorState` (driving a real SQL generation to a guaranteed-empty result isn't deterministic through the mock LLM's rule matching) and asserts `execution_result.success is True`, `row_count == 0`, columns survive, and no error is set. Confirmed non-vacuous via `git stash` (fails with the exact pre-fix symptom against the stashed code). Full `test_e2e_chat.py` + `test_chat_pipeline.py` + `test_chat_resilience.py` (35 tests) pass; `ruff check --select E,F,I,W` clean. PR: #477.

## BUG-137: DPC (dual-paradigm cross-check) verdict computed but never surfaced to the user
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode completeness/UX audit (product-completeness slot, added 2026-09-21 to the standing loop rotation). Adversarial-verify: 3/3 skeptics did not refute.
- **Severity:** medium — the backend's independent pandas cross-check can flag a generated SQL answer as a "mismatch" against an independent computation, but a user has no way to know an answer failed its own verification; the product's own copy ("DPC cross-check", "generated, checked, and signed") advertises this safety net exists.
- **Root cause:** DPC verification runs by default on every chat query (`AURA_DPC_ENABLED`/`AURA_DPC_CHAT_ENABLED` both default on) and can return `status='mismatch'`. `verify_node` (`aurabackend/agents/langgraph_orchestrator.py:282-284`) only `logger.warning(...)`s a mismatch — no other effect. `chat_endpoint` (`aurabackend/api_gateway/routers/chat.py`) already attached the raw verdict unconditionally at `execution_result.verification`, but nothing on the frontend read it: `frontend/src/services/api.ts`'s `ExecutionResult` interface had no `verification` field at all, and none of the three live chat/query surfaces (`AskAuraPanel.tsx`, `AskAuraChat.tsx`, `QueryPanel.tsx`) ever rendered one — an answer that failed its own cross-check was displayed identically to a verified one.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing; the backend computed and even transmitted the verdict, nothing on the frontend ever consumed it.
- **Fix:** added a `VerificationResult` type to `api.ts` and wired a `status === 'mismatch'` warning banner into all three surfaces — `AskAuraPanel.tsx` (amber `border-warn`/`text-warn` banner, matching its existing error-banner pattern), `AskAuraChat.tsx` (amber `--warn`-token banner on both the Commander and `sendMessage`-fallback paths, extending the shared `cockpit/types.ts` `Msg` type), and `QueryPanel.tsx` (new `.panel-warn-inline` CSS class in `terminal.css`, mirroring the existing `.panel-error-inline` rule but amber instead of red — a mismatch is a caveat on the answer, not a failed request). New tests in all three surfaces' test files assert the mismatch warning renders with the DPC verdict's `reason` text, and (for `AskAuraPanel.tsx`) that no warning renders when `status === 'verified'`. Confirmed non-vacuous via `git stash` (all 3 new tests fail against pre-fix code; 12 pre-existing tests in the same files still pass). `tsc -b` and `eslint --max-warnings 0` clean on all 9 touched files. PR: #478.

## BUG-138: Dashboards panel — dead click affordance
- **Status:** fixed (partial)
- **Found by:** ultracode completeness/UX audit (added 2026-09-21 to the standing loop rotation). Adversarial-verify: 3/3 skeptics did not refute.
- **Severity:** high — Dashboard cards are styled as clickable (cursor-pointer, hover border highlight) but have no onClick handler, no keyboard handler, and aren't a real button/link — clicking or tabbing to a dashboard card does nothing.
- **Root cause:** A user opens Dashboards, sees saved dashboard cards with a hover highlight that reads as 'click to open', clicks one, and nothing happens — no navigation, no detail view, no error. In a demo this looks like a broken feature.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** nothing in the app opens a dashboard (no detail view or route exists), so the honest fix is to stop promising an action: the tile no longer has `cursor-pointer` or the hover-border highlight. New `DashboardsPanel.test.tsx` fails on the old markup and passes now; eslint and build pass. NOT done: an actual dashboard detail/open view — that is a product feature, not a defect fix, and remains a gap. PR #516.


## BUG-139: Ask AURA — two incompatible chat UIs for the same feature
- **Status:** open
- **Found by:** ultracode completeness/UX audit (added 2026-09-21 to the standing loop rotation). Adversarial-verify: 3/3 skeptics did not refute.
- **Severity:** high — There are two separate 'Ask AURA' implementations with different visual language and different accessibility: the Cockpit home screen renders the legacy AskAuraChat (rounded message bubbles, raw CSS-var inline styles, query results rendered as stacked non-semantic <div> rows) while the 'Ask AURA' nav destination renders AskAuraPanel (sharp corners per the house style, ui-kit EmptyState, and a real semantic <table> for results).
- **Root cause:** A user chats on the Cockpit landing page (rounded bubbles, div-grid 'table' with no <table>/<th> so a screen reader announces no column headers), then clicks 'Ask AURA' in the left nav and sees a visually different chat with a different input, different empty state, and an actually-accessible results table for the same underlying feature — a jarring inconsistency that also regresses accessibility on the first screen every user sees.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** pending. Citations: frontend/src/workbench/Workbench.tsx:440 mounts `<AskAuraChat .../>` for the default Cockpit nav; frontend/src/workbench/viewRegistry.ts:18,34 maps the 'Ask AURA' nav item to AskAuraPanel instead. Compare frontend/src/workbench/cockpit/AskAuraChat.tsx:87-94 (rounded `rounded-[10px_10px_3px_10px]` bubble, div-grid pseudo-table at lines 90-95) with frontend/src/workbench/panels/AskAuraPanel.tsx:93-126 (sharp-cornered bubbles, real `<table>` with `<th>`/`<td>`).

## BUG-140: Cockpit chat input — no accessible name
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode completeness/UX audit (added 2026-09-21 to the standing loop rotation). Adversarial-verify: 3/3 skeptics did not refute.
- **Severity:** high — The main chat input on the Cockpit landing screen has a placeholder but no <label> and no aria-label, so it has no accessible name for assistive technology, contradicting the project's own stated forms rule ('every input gets a real label... never a placeholder standing in as the label').
- **Root cause:** A screen-reader user tabs to the primary input on the app's default landing screen and hears only 'edit text' with no indication of what it's for, because the placeholder text is not exposed as an accessible name.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** the chat `<input>` now carries `aria-label="Ask AURA"` (matching AskAuraPanel). Test: `getByRole('textbox', {name:'Ask AURA'})`. Both tests confirmed failing without the change (stash) and passing with it; fixed together with the sibling cockpit-chat bug in one PR since they are the same two lines of one component. Not checked with a real screen reader. PR #512.


## BUG-141: Audit wizard Map step — validation errors not exposed to assistive tech
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode completeness/UX audit (added 2026-09-21 to the standing loop rotation). Adversarial-verify: 3/3 skeptics did not refute.
- **Severity:** medium — The Map-columns step shows field-level validation errors as plain sibling <span> text but never sets aria-invalid or aria-describedby on the corresponding <select>, and the confounder multi-select toggle buttons carry no aria-pressed — all state is color/visual-only.
- **Root cause:** A screen-reader user fills in the audit mapping step, leaves 'Treatment' unmapped, and hears nothing indicating the field is invalid when the error appears — only sighted users relying on the adjacent red text know why 'Next' is disabled. Similarly, which confounder columns are currently selected is only conveyed by color, not by ARIA toggle state.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** `MapStep` selects now carry `aria-invalid` and `aria-describedby` pointing at the visible error (else note); error spans have ids and `role="alert"`; the confounder buttons have `aria-pressed` and sit in a `role="group"` labelled 'Confounders' and described by its error. New `audit/__tests__/MapStep.a11y.test.tsx` (3 tests) fails on the old component and passes now; audit suite (159), eslint and `npm run build` pass. Not checked with a real screen reader. PR #513.


## BUG-142: Design-system split: Cockpit (first screen) is the least-migrated surface
- **Status:** open
- **Found by:** ultracode completeness/UX audit (added 2026-09-21 to the standing loop rotation). Adversarial-verify: 3/3 skeptics did not refute.
- **Severity:** medium — Every component under workbench/cockpit/ (the default landing view, shown first on login) is still built with raw inline style={{}} objects, hardcoded CSS custom properties, and rounded corners, while the deeper native panels under workbench/panels/ (Connectors, Files & Data, Dashboards) are fully migrated to the ui-kit + Tailwind token system the project mandates ('no inline styles', 'sharp corners, rounded-none is the house style').
- **Root cause:** A first-time user's very first impression of the product (the Cockpit dashboard) is visually inconsistent with the polished, sharp-cornered, token-driven panels they reach two clicks later — rounded chat bubbles and ad hoc pixel values on the landing screen next to a design system elsewhere that explicitly forbids both.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** pending. Citations: style={{...}} inline styles found in frontend/src/workbench/cockpit/HealingQueueApprovals.tsx, SessionEventsFeed.tsx, LineageSummaryCard.tsx, LiveRadarPanel.tsx, PipelinesStreamingPanel.tsx, CockpitStats.tsx, ForensicAuditPanel.tsx, AskAuraChat.tsx, and frontend/src/workbench/Workbench.tsx:420 (`style={{ fontWeight: 600, color: statusColor, background: statusBg, ... }}`); contrast with the migrated frontend/src/workbench/panels/ConnectorsPanel.tsx and DashboardsPanel.tsx which use only Tailwind token utilities. Rule stated in frontend/CLAUDE.md: 'do not hand-roll inline style={{…}} objects' and .claude/rules/frontend.md 'Anti-slop layout discipline'.

## BUG-143: Healing queue approvals — irreversible action with no confirmation
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode completeness/UX audit (added 2026-09-21 to the standing loop rotation). Adversarial-verify: 3/3 skeptics did not refute.
- **Severity:** medium — 'Approve & deploy' and 'Reject' on a pending self-healing recovery fire immediately on a single click with no confirmation dialog, even though the panel's own copy states every decision is 'a signed override in the WORM audit log' (i.e. permanent).
- **Root cause:** In a dense list of small (11px), closely-spaced buttons, a misclick instantly approves a production shim deploy or permanently rejects a recovery, with no undo and no 'are you sure' — a real operational risk being handled with the interaction weight of a toast dismiss.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** `HealingQueueApprovals` now needs two clicks: Approve & deploy / Reject only arms the row (inline, no modal, per the frontend rules), which swaps in a description of what will happen plus `Confirm approve` / `Confirm reject` (autofocused) and `Cancel`. Only the confirm click calls `decideHeal`. Tests: the two old click tests now assert nothing is called until confirm, plus a Cancel test; `Workbench.test.tsx`'s two approve tests confirm too. workbench suite (103), eslint and build pass. Not run: the old-behaviour tests against the old component (the change is a strict additional step, so the new 'not called after first click' assertions cannot pass on it). Not checked in a browser. PR #514.


## BUG-144: Cockpit chat — missing busy state on submit control
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode completeness/UX audit (added 2026-09-21 to the standing loop rotation). Adversarial-verify: 3/3 skeptics did not refute.
- **Severity:** low — The Cockpit chat's 'Ask' button has no disabled/aria-busy state while a request is in flight, so it remains fully clickable during a pending request; the equivalent AskAuraPanel button correctly disables itself.
- **Root cause:** A user double-clicks 'Ask' while the first query is still streaming; nothing visually indicates the button shouldn't be pressed again, unlike the nav-accessible Ask AURA panel which greys out its button and swaps its label to a busy indicator.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** the Ask button is `disabled` and `aria-busy` while `thinking` is set (with a disabled style), re-enabled on completion. Test holds `streamMessage` open and asserts disabled+aria-busy then re-enabled. Both tests confirmed failing without the change (stash) and passing with it; fixed together with the sibling cockpit-chat bug in one PR since they are the same two lines of one component. Not checked with a real screen reader. PR #512.


## BUG-145: backend: cross-tenant dataset-profile leak (metadata_store DatasetProfile / semantic model auto-generation)
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode completeness/UX audit (added 2026-09-21 to the standing loop rotation). Adversarial-verify: 3/3 skeptics did not refute.
- **Severity:** critical — DatasetProfile rows are keyed only by the bare uploaded filename (no tenant/org column), so any tenant that uploads a file with a common name ("data.csv", "export.csv", "Sheet1.xlsx", etc.) overwrites another tenant's stored profile — and one route reads that shared table with no ownership check at all.
- **Root cause:** Tenant A uploads sales.csv; the background hook in api_gateway/routers/files.py writes a DatasetProfile row with id=file_id='sales.csv' containing A's real column names and sample_data rows. Tenant B later uploads their own, unrelated sales.csv — the same upsert_dataset_profile() call (repository.py get(DatasetProfile, 'sales.csv')) finds A's existing row and overwrites it with B's data (or vice versa, depending on upload order). From that point, GET /files/sales.csv/profile for whichever tenant currently owns a local file named sales.csv returns the OTHER tenant's column names and real sample values, because the route's only gate is 'does a file with this name exist in MY tenant's own upload dir' (files.py's ownership check), not 'does this profile belong to me' — the table has no tenant column to check. Separately, POST /semantic/models/from-file/{file_id} (pipelines.py) calls repo.get_dataset_profile(file_id) with ZERO ownership/tenant check — the route's own comment admits this ('get_dataset_profile is still unscoped, because dataset_profiles has no tenant column yet') — so any authenticated user of any tenant can simply guess a common filename and pull another org's column names/dataset name into their own permanently-stored semantic model via a 200 'success' response.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** added a nullable, indexed `workspace_id` column to `DatasetProfile` (migration `f6a7b8c9d0e1`) and re-minted the upsert key as `id = f"{workspace_id or 'default'}::{file_id}"`, mirroring the precedent set for `SemanticModel.workspace_id`. Scoped by `_request_tenant(request)` (matching the file's own storage boundary) at all three call sites: files.py's upload hook, its `GET /files/{id}/profile` read route, and pipelines.py's `auto_generate_model_from_file` (closing the self-documented "still unscoped" gap). New cross-tenant collision test confirmed failing pre-fix, passing post-fix; full backend pre-push suite green (2605 passed). PR #483.

## BUG-146: backend+frontend: Excel/.xlsx/.xls (and .txt) uploads advertised as supported but never enter the profiling/chat schema context
- **Status:** fixed (partial)
- **Found by:** ultracode completeness/UX audit (added 2026-09-21 to the standing loop rotation). Adversarial-verify: 3/3 skeptics did not refute.
- **Severity:** high — The upload endpoint and the frontend both advertise Excel (and plain text) as a supported, immediately-queryable format, but the DuckDB-backed loader used for profiling has no Excel branch (it silently falls back to CSV parsing on binary data) and the schema-context builder used by chat/query/dashboards explicitly skips any extension other than .csv/.parquet/.json — so an uploaded .xlsx file is stored successfully but is never visible to chat and its profile generation fails.
- **Root cause:** A user uploads report.xlsx. POST /upload accepts it, stores it, and returns {"status":"success"} (files.py). The FilesAndDataPanel UI shows it in the dataset list with copy reading 'Upload a CSV, Excel, JSON, or Parquet file — it becomes queryable in Ask AURA immediately.' In the background, the BUG-030 profile hook calls PipelineGenerator.get_file_schema -> shared.data_utils.smart_load_file, whose ext dispatch only special-cases '.csv'; for '.xlsx' it falls into the else branch and defaults read_fn to 'read_csv_auto', i.e. it runs `SELECT * FROM read_csv_auto('report.xlsx')` against a binary OOXML file — this raises a DuckDB parse error that is only logged server-side (fire_and_forget swallows it into a log line, shared/tasks.py `_on_task_done`), so GET /files/report.xlsx/profile 404s forever and the user is never told. Independently, shared/data_utils.py::build_schema_context (used by chat.py, queries.py, and dashboards.py via build_schema_context_cached) has `if ext not in ('.csv', '.parquet', '.json'): continue`, so report.xlsx's table is never added to the schema context handed to the LLM — the user can open Ask AURA and ask a question about the file they just 'successfully' uploaded and the assistant has no knowledge the table exists. pipeline/generator.py's own _discover_files docstring acknowledges the same gap for .xlsx/.tsv discovery.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** `.xlsx` now loads for real: `shared/data_utils.py` gains `_load_excel_table` (pandas + openpyxl, first sheet, column names stringified, registered into DuckDB), used by `smart_load_file`, the `build_schema_context` allowlist, and the schema-cache replay recipe (`EXCEL_READ_FN` sentinel, since DuckDB has no built-in Excel reader). Remote (`s3://`) Excel raises a clear error instead of being parsed as CSV. `openpyxl>=3.1,<4.0` declared in requirements.txt (it was only transitive; constraints.txt already pins 3.1.5). The advertised formats were made honest: `.xls` and `.txt` removed from `/files/supported-formats` and the frontend `accept`, and the Excel note says first sheet only. `tests/test_excel_loading.py` (4 tests, real xlsx + real DuckDB, no mocks) passes with 81 related tests; the file fails at import without the change. NOT done: legacy `.xls` (would need xlrd), multi-sheet workbooks, Excel on S3 storage, and no live upload of a real workbook through the deployed gateway yet. PR #515.


## BUG-147: frontend: no UI surface for the dataset-profile/schema read path at all
- **Status:** open
- **Found by:** ultracode completeness/UX audit (added 2026-09-21 to the standing loop rotation). Adversarial-verify: 3/3 skeptics did not refute.
- **Severity:** medium — GET /files/{file_id}/profile — the endpoint the BUG-030 fix built specifically so a plain upload has a real, queryable profile — has zero frontend call sites; no panel ever fetches or displays a file's inferred columns, row count, or sample data after upload, so a user has no way to see the profile the product claims to generate, and no way to notice when profiling silently failed (as it does for the Excel case above).
- **Root cause:** A user uploads a file via the Files & Data panel. The panel (FilesAndDataPanel.tsx) refreshes the /files listing (filename, size, modified date, extension tag) but never calls GET /files/{file_id}/profile, GET /pipeline/schema/{fileName}, or renders columns/row-count anywhere in that flow; DatasetsPanel.tsx and MetadataStorePanel.tsx likewise never call the per-file profile endpoint (grep across frontend/src turns up zero matches for the profile route). The only way to discover whether profiling actually succeeded is to go ask the chat assistant a question and see if it knows the table exists — there is no direct 'here is what we understood about your data' confirmation screen the product's other panels' own descriptions imply exists.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** pending. Citations: frontend/src/workbench/panels/FilesAndDataPanel.tsx:82-153 (upload+list flow with no profile fetch); frontend/src/workbench/panels/MetadataStorePanel.tsx:4-61 (comment describes profile-driven semantic models but the panel never reads a profile directly); aurabackend/api_gateway/routers/files.py:345-388 (the unconsumed profile endpoint).

## BUG-148: Toast / notification system
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode completeness/UX audit (added 2026-09-21 to the standing loop rotation). Adversarial-verify: 3/3 skeptics did not refute.
- **Severity:** critical — Three independent, mutually-unaware toast implementations are mounted and firing simultaneously in the same app, with materially different behavior (single vs. queued, different positions, different variant sets), and one of them renders error messages with a permanent green success checkmark.
- **Root cause:** A user is inside the Workbench cockpit (which mounts `contexts/ToastContext.tsx` + `components/ui/Toast.tsx` globally via `workbench/views.tsx`) and rejects a healing proposal or hits a failed decision call. `Workbench.tsx` calls its own local `showToast()` (backed by `workbench/Toast.tsx`), which for `Decision failed: ...` (Workbench.tsx:287) still renders through the same bottom-center `Toast` component that unconditionally shows a green `✓` icon (workbench/Toast.tsx:7) regardless of the message being an error. Meanwhile `pages/PipelinesPanel.tsx` implements a THIRD, completely separate single-slot toast (state + JSX at lines 209-214 and 552-571) with its own bottom-right position, its own 4000ms timer, and only success/error variants (no warning/info, no aria-live). None of the three call the shared `useToast()`/`ToastContext` that the app already provides and that IS mounted at the root. Users see three different notification idioms depending on which panel triggered the toast, and one of them visually lies about failure states.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** converged all toast usage onto the existing `ToastContext`/`components/ui/Toast.tsx` queue. Moved the single `ToastProvider`+`ToastContainer` up to wrap the whole `Workbench` shell (split into a thin `Workbench` wrapper + `WorkbenchInner` so the provider sits above its own consumer), removed the redundant per-view provider `views.tsx` used to mount on every `ViewHost` render, switched `Workbench.tsx`'s decision-outcome calls to `toast.success`/`toast.warning`/`toast.error` (so a failed decision now renders the real error variant instead of a hardcoded green `✓`), switched `PipelinesPanel.tsx`'s local toast state to the same `useToast()`, and deleted the dead `workbench/Toast.tsx`. New regression test asserts a failed healing decision renders `role="alert"` with the danger variant, not the old success-only markup — confirmed failing pre-fix, passing post-fix. Full frontend suite green (348 tests). PR #485.

## BUG-149: Button / form primitive drift within the same feature
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode completeness/UX audit (added 2026-09-21 to the standing loop rotation). Adversarial-verify: 3/3 skeptics did not refute.
- **Severity:** high — Two structurally different Button implementations (a legacy CSS-class button and the shadcn/cva ui-kit button) are both actively used inside the same 'audit' feature area, producing different sizing scales, disabled/hover treatment, and focus-ring behavior for what is nominally one product flow — and the legacy one is the exact system the project's own frontend/CLAUDE.md says must not be used.
- **Root cause:** A user starts the audit flow at `AuditFrontDoor.tsx`, which renders its 'Retry' button via `@/components/ui-kit/button` (AuditFrontDoor.tsx:6, 52-54) — cva variants, `focus-visible:ring-[3px] ring-ring/50`, xs/sm/default/lg/icon sizing. They proceed into `AuditWizard.tsx`, whose Back/Next/Run buttons import the legacy `../ui/Button` (AuditWizard.tsx:12, 104-108) — a different component backed by `ui/primitives.css`'s hand-written `.ui-btn`/`.ui-btn--primary` classes (ui/primitives.css:3-35) with its own sm/md/lg scale, its own disabled/hover colors, and a plain `outline: 2px solid` focus style instead of the ui-kit focus-ring convention frontend.md mandates verbatim. The two buttons look and behave subtly differently one screen apart in the same task.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** switched `AuditWizard.tsx`'s Back/Next/Run buttons from the legacy `../ui/Button` to `@/components/ui-kit/button`, matching `AuditFrontDoor.tsx`'s existing usage (`variant="secondary"` → `variant="outline"`, the closest ui-kit equivalent; the default/primary variant needed no mapping). The legacy `ui/Button.tsx` and `ui/primitives.css`'s `.ui-btn` rules are left in place — `AuditProgress.tsx` and `Certificate.tsx` still use `.ui-btn` classes directly (not via the `Button` component) and are outside this report's scope. New regression test asserts the wizard's Next button carries `data-slot="button"`, a marker only the ui-kit component sets — confirmed failing pre-fix, passing post-fix. Full frontend suite green (349 tests). PR #489.

## BUG-150: Card/result component bypassing the design system entirely
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode completeness/UX audit (added 2026-09-21 to the standing loop rotation). Adversarial-verify: 3/3 skeptics did not refute.
- **Severity:** high — `CounterfactualCard.tsx`, the primary result card on the Counterfactual results page, is built entirely from ad-hoc inline `style={{}}` objects with raw hex colors and rounded corners, rather than composing `ui-kit`'s `Card`/`Panel`/`Button` — directly violating the project's own mandatory 'never inline styles or raw colors' rule and its 'sharp corners, no rounded cards' house style, and producing a visibly different look from every other panel/card in the app.
