- **Root cause:** A user runs a counterfactual audit and views the result card rendered by `pages/Counterfactual.tsx` via `CounterfactualCard`. The outer card uses `borderRadius: 8` (CounterfactualCard.tsx:489) — rounded corners — while every ui-kit `Card` in the rest of the app is deliberately `rounded-none` (components/ui-kit/card.tsx:1-7,17) per the locked 'Terminal-Authority' visual direction. All text/border colors are raw hex literals (`#f1f5f9`, `#38bdf8`, `#1e293b`, `#64748b`, `rgba(220,38,38,0.18)`, etc. — CounterfactualCard.tsx:76-91,487-491,584) instead of `tokens.css` utilities, so this card will not repaint correctly on a theme change the way every `bg-surface`/`text-text-primary` component does. The inline 'See the debate' toggle button (CounterfactualCard.tsx:577-593) is a raw `<button>` with no focus-visible ring, unlike every ui-kit `Button`.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** rebuilt `CounterfactualCard.tsx` on the ui-kit: the outer wrapper is now `Card` (sharp corners, token surface), the debate toggle is `Button variant="link"` (gets the shared focus-visible ring), and every raw-hex/`rgba` color map became token utilities (`danger`/`warn`/`signal` tones at `/15` fill and `/50` border, `raised`/`text-tertiary` for neutral). The only remaining inline `style` props are data-driven percent offsets for the propensity/sensitivity/CATE glyphs, which no static utility can express. Sensitivity dots and CATE bars lost their `borderRadius` (square markers, matching the house style). All existing `data-testid`s preserved (20 prior tests unchanged and green); 3 new regression tests assert the Card/Button `data-slot` markers, `rounded-none`, and no inline `style`/token badge color — confirmed failing pre-fix, passing post-fix. Verified every new utility compiles into the built CSS. PR #491.
- **Citations:** frontend/src/components/CounterfactualCard.tsx:75-116,480-493,577-593; frontend/src/components/ui-kit/card.tsx:1-23; frontend/CLAUDE.md rule 2 ('never inline styles or raw colors'); frontend.md 'Anti-slop layout discipline' (sharp corners, no rounded cards)

## BUG-151: Color-token vocabulary fragmentation
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode completeness/UX audit (added 2026-09-21 to the standing loop rotation). Adversarial-verify: 3/3 skeptics did not refute.
- **Severity:** medium — The same semantic colors (secondary text, hairline border, accent) are driven by at least three unrelated CSS-variable naming schemes across different pages, so a single theme/rebrand change would silently miss whole feature areas.
- **Root cause:** `workbench/cockpit/LineageSummaryCard.tsx` uses `var(--text2)`, `var(--text3)`, `var(--accent-bd)` (LineageSummaryCard.tsx:13,17-18) — workbench.css's own token set. `ui/primitives.css` (backing the legacy `Button`/`Card` used in the audit wizard) uses a different set: `var(--text-primary)`, `var(--accent)`, `var(--border-strong)` (ui/primitives.css:19-30). `CounterfactualCard.tsx` uses yet another pattern, inline fallback hex chained onto legacy-namespaced vars: `var(--text-primary, #f1f5f9)`, `var(--border, #1e293b)` (CounterfactualCard.tsx:487,495). Meanwhile the mandated system (frontend/CLAUDE.md) is Tailwind utilities bound to `tokens.css` (`text-text-secondary`, `border-border-hairline`). None of these three legacy/ad-hoc vocabularies is guaranteed to update together, so a brand/theme pass on `tokens.css` will not visibly affect the audit wizard, the counterfactual card, or (partially) the cockpit's lineage card.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** of the three cited vocabularies only one was a live defect. (1) `workbench.css`'s `.aw` block restated tokens.css's dark hex under short names (`--bg`, `--text2`, `--accent-bd`, ...), referenced 75 times across 17 cockpit files including `LineageSummaryCard.tsx`, so a tokens.css rebrand never reached the workbench: FIXED by aliasing the byte-identical ones to their canonical tokens (`--text2: var(--text-secondary)`, `--accent-bd: var(--accent-border)`, ...) and dropping the same-named `--accent/--warn/--danger/--cyan` redeclarations so they inherit from :root. Zero visual change (values are identical); `--text3` (`#87919d`, brighter than `--text-tertiary` `#7d8590` for contrast) and the `.12` `*-dim` washes genuinely differ and stay local. (2) `ui/primitives.css` was mis-cited: `--text-primary`, `--accent`, `--border-strong` ARE the canonical tokens.css names, so it needed no change. (3) `CounterfactualCard.tsx` was already rebuilt on tokens by BUG-150 (#491). New `workbenchTokens.test.ts` guards the alias contract (10 of 11 assertions fail against the old CSS, all pass now); full frontend suite 363/363, build and eslint clean, built CSS confirmed to emit the `var()` aliases. PR #494.

## BUG-152: Error-state presentation inconsistency
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode completeness/UX audit (added 2026-09-21 to the standing loop rotation). Adversarial-verify: 3/3 skeptics did not refute.
- **Severity:** medium — Error states are surfaced through at least three different patterns across pages — a mandated `EmptyState intent="error"` component, a raw unstyled `<p>` of the error string, and inline error-typed toasts — with no shared convention for retry affordances.
- **Root cause:** `frontend.md` mandates composing every error state from `ui-kit`'s `EmptyState intent="error"` (with a Retry action), citing `CostPanel.tsx` as the reference. `AuditWizard.tsx`, however, renders its run-error as a bare paragraph — `{runError && <p className="aud-wizard__err">{runError}</p>}` (AuditWizard.tsx:100) — with no retry button, no icon, no `role="alert"`. `pages/PipelinesPanel.tsx` instead surfaces load/save/delete failures exclusively as its bespoke bottom-right toast (`showToast(e.message, 'error')`, PipelinesPanel.tsx:258,270,482,513) rather than an inline `EmptyState`. A user hitting the same class of failure (a failed backend call) sees a dismissible toast on one page and a plain red-less paragraph with no dismissal or retry on another.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** partly real, partly mis-diagnosed. REAL: the audit wizard's two failure lines (`AuditWizard.tsx` run error, `UploadStep.tsx` upload error) were bare `<p>`s with no `role="alert"`, so assistive tech was never told an upload or run had failed. Both now render through one shared `audit/wizard/WizardError.tsx` (`role="alert"`, token `danger` border/fill/text, mono, sharp corners) with stable test ids (`wizard-upload-error`, `wizard-run-error`), and the dead `.aud-wizard__err` CSS rule was removed. NOT A DEFECT: (a) the report's 'red-less paragraph' was wrong — `.aud-wizard__err` did set `color: var(--danger)`; (b) 'no retry' is inherent — after a failed run `running` resets so **Run audit** is enabled again, and a failed upload is retried by re-picking the file, so an `EmptyState` Retry button would duplicate the step's own control (EmptyState is a region-level surface, not a form's inline error); (c) `PipelinesPanel`'s load/delete/save failures are transient action feedback and, since BUG-148, already run through the shared `ToastContext` (`role="alert"`), which is the right pattern for an action error — not an inline empty state. Two new AuditWizard tests assert an upload failure and a run failure each surface as `role=alert` with the message (and that Run stays enabled to retry) — both confirmed failing pre-fix via stash; full frontend suite 365/365, build and eslint clean. PR #495.

## BUG-153: counterfactual_service backend -> operator UI (dependency-missing fallback disclosure)
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode completeness/UX audit (added 2026-09-21 to the standing loop rotation). Adversarial-verify: 3/3 skeptics did not refute.
- **Severity:** high — The 'degraded' flag that marks a silent EconML->DoWhy fallback for double_ml is computed correctly in the engine but is dropped by the operator-tier renderer and never reaches any frontend component.
- **Root cause:** econml is not installed in the deployment (or fails to import). A user asks a causal question via the default UI (Counterfactual.tsx defaults audience to 'operator'). engine.py's dispatch (line 927) routes double_ml to the DoWhy backdoor.linear_regression stub instead of the real DR-Learner, and correctly sets estimate.degraded=True (engine.py:1036/1046, schemas.py:139). But renderers.py's _operator() (lines 142-184) never copies `degraded` into the dict it returns, and CounterfactualOperatorView (CounterfactualCard.tsx:43-73) has no such field. The user sees a normal-looking ATE + CI card (Counterfactual.tsx:91, CounterfactualCard.tsx render) with zero indication that a materially weaker estimator ran in place of the one requested. Grep confirms no frontend file reads estimates_full[].degraded for the counterfactual flow (the only 'degraded' fields wired into any UI belong to an unrelated financial-audit Certificate schema in frontend/src/audit/types.ts).
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** `renderers._operator()` now copies the engine's per-estimate `degraded` flag into the view as `degraded_methods` (methods that actually contributed to the headline and ran as a fallback; omitted when empty, like the other optional fields, so existing fixtures still match) — auditor/analyst views inherit it via `_operator`. `CounterfactualOperatorView` gains `degraded_methods?: string[]`, and `CounterfactualCard` renders a warn-toned `role="note"` disclosure naming the method and the weaker linear-regression fallback. `degraded` stays excluded from the signed hash (provenance only), so no artifact signature or replay changes. An errored estimate is deliberately not listed (it never fed the number). Backend: 3 new renderer tests (discloses in all three audiences; omitted when nothing degraded; ignores an errored degraded estimate) — the disclosure test fails pre-fix with `KeyError: 'degraded_methods'`. Frontend: 2 new card tests, the disclosure one failing pre-fix; card suite 25/25, `tsc -b` and eslint clean. PR #496.
- **Citations:** Citations: aurabackend/counterfactual_service/engine.py:927-931,1036,1046; aurabackend/counterfactual_service/schemas.py:56,139,166; aurabackend/counterfactual_service/renderers.py:142-184; frontend/src/components/CounterfactualCard.tsx:43-73; frontend/src/pages/Counterfactual.tsx:37,41,91

## BUG-154: counterfactual_service engine.score_confidence + operator UI
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode completeness/UX audit (added 2026-09-21 to the standing loop rotation). Adversarial-verify: 3/3 skeptics did not refute.
- **Severity:** high — The confidence badge can report 'high' when only a single estimator out of four actually produced a usable result, and neither the backend response nor the frontend card discloses how many estimators/refuters succeeded vs failed.
- **Root cause:** 3 of 4 estimators fail (e.g. a missing optional dependency, degenerate treatment variance, or a per-method timeout) and only one succeeds. pairwise_ci_overlap_rate (engine.py:88-100) explicitly returns 1.0 for a single valid estimate ('no disagreement to penalise'), so score_confidence (engine.py:103-119) can compute raw=0.9 and return 'high' -- this exact case is asserted as intended behavior in test_counterfactual_confidence.py:90-94. renderers.py's _operator() (lines 142-172) exposes only the aggregate confidence string, with no count of valid/failed estimators or refuters anywhere in the payload, and CounterfactualCard.tsx:494-512 renders only that single badge. An operator sees 'high confidence' with no way to know the number is based on one method, not the four the UI's own copy ('Each artifact runs four estimators...') implies.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** DISCLOSURE fix; the scoring rule is deliberately unchanged. `renderers._operator()` now always emits `estimator_coverage: {valid, total}` (estimates without an `error` vs. all estimates; auditor/analyst inherit it), the TS view type gains `estimator_coverage?`, and `CounterfactualCard` shows a warn-toned `role="note"` ('rests on N of M estimators; the rest failed and were excluded') whenever `valid < total`, silent on a full run. Backend: 2 new renderer tests, including one that runs the REAL `score_confidence` on 1-valid-of-4 + two passing refuters and asserts it still returns `high` (so the test proves the premise, not a hardcoded label); the coverage test fails pre-fix with `KeyError: 'estimator_coverage'`. Frontend: 2 new card tests (notice on 1/4 next to a `high` badge; absent on 4/4 and when the field is absent), the disclosure one failing pre-fix; card suite 27/27, `tsc -b`/eslint clean. NOT CHANGED, needs a product/statistics decision: whether a lone surviving estimator should be able to score `high` at all (`pairwise_ci_overlap_rate` returns 1.0 for one valid estimate and `test_counterfactual_confidence.py:90-94` pins it as intended). Changing the formula alters `confidence`, which is part of the signed artifact hash, so it also touches replay/verification — deliberately not done unasked. Refuter counts are not yet reported (the report also mentioned them). PR #497.
- **Citations:** Citations: aurabackend/counterfactual_service/engine.py:88-119; aurabackend/tests/test_counterfactual_confidence.py:90-94; aurabackend/counterfactual_service/renderers.py:142-172; frontend/src/components/CounterfactualCard.tsx:494-512; frontend/src/pages/Counterfactual.tsx:130-131

## BUG-155: counterfactual_service engine DoWhy CI fallback
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode completeness/UX audit (added 2026-09-21 to the standing loop rotation). Adversarial-verify: 3/3 skeptics did not refute.
- **Severity:** medium — When a DoWhy-routed estimator's confidence-interval machinery fails or is unsupported, the engine falls back to point +/- 2*stderr with stderr silently defaulted to 0.0, which can produce a zero-width 'CI' rendered identically to a genuine tight interval, and can flip a fairness/compliance 'detected' verdict.
- **Root cause:** For methods like psm/ipw/linear_regression, if est.get_confidence_intervals() throws or the estimate object lacks a usable stderr attribute, engine.py:1003-1023 computes `stderr = float(getattr(est, 'stderr', 0.0) or 0.0)` then `lo, hi = point - 2*stderr, point + 2*stderr`, giving ci_lower == ci_upper == point with no distinguishing flag (ci_method defaults to 'asymptotic' regardless, schemas.py:166). significance_verdict() (verdict.py:40-41,72-75) treats any CI excluding zero as sufficient for a 'detected' effect -- used by the fairness/compliance demo scenarios (fair_lending.py, hiring_fairness.py) -- so a fabricated zero-width interval could produce a false-certain 'disparate impact detected' (or mask real uncertainty) with no warning in CounterfactualCard.tsx:513-563. This is a plausible code-path risk based on static reading; I did not execute DoWhy live to force this exact branch, so treat as PLAUSIBLE not confirmed [UPDATE: CONFIRMED live during the fix, see Fix]. No test in the repo asserts ci_lower < ci_upper strictly (only <=, test_counterfactual_engine.py:81), so this path isn't guarded.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** CONFIRMED live, and worse than reported: it fired on an ordinary dataset, not a rare failure. A probe of the four DoWhy-routed methods showed `linear_regression` returning `[1.354, 1.354]` (width 0.0000) every time. Actual root cause (different from the report's 'stderr silently defaulted'): DoWhy's `backdoor.linear_regression` returns its interval as a (1, 2) array, `[[lo, hi]]`; the engine's guard `len(ci) >= 2` rejected that length-1 outer list, dropping to the fallback, which read `getattr(est, 'stderr', 0.0)` — an attribute DoWhy estimates do not have (`hasattr` is False) — so stderr was always 0.0 and the interval collapsed to `[point, point]`. `significance_verdict` reads any non-zero-straddling interval as certainty, so this fed 'detected' verdicts. The real interval (`[1.156, 1.553]`) and real standard error (`get_standard_error()`, 0.101) were available the whole time. Fix: new `engine._dowhy_confidence_interval(est, point)` unwraps the nested shape, falls back to DoWhy's real `get_standard_error()` (`point ± 2·se`), and returns None when neither is usable, in which case the estimator now surfaces as an ERRORED estimate (excluded from the point/CI aggregate, verdict and confidence, and counted by BUG-154's coverage note) instead of a fabricated interval. Live result after the fix: linear_regression `[1.156, 1.553]` (matches DoWhy's own), psm/ipw/double_ml unchanged. 11 new tests in `tests/test_counterfactual_ci_extraction.py` (helper: nested/flat shapes, SE fallback, unusable shape, and zero/negative/NaN/inf/None SE all return None; plus a real-DoWhy regression asserting linear_regression width > 0.05 and point inside the CI); pre-fix evidence is the probe (width 0.0000 -> 0.3968) since the helper did not exist before. Wider counterfactual/verdict/demo/audit suites: 314 passed, 1 skipped, no fallout. NOTE: new linear_regression artifacts get different hashes (the CI is in the hash basis) — inherent to correcting the interval; previously signed artifacts verify against their stored payload and are unaffected. PR #498.
- **Citations:** Citations: aurabackend/counterfactual_service/engine.py:1002-1025; aurabackend/counterfactual_service/schemas.py:166; aurabackend/counterfactual_service/verdict.py:40-41,63-75; aurabackend/counterfactual_service/demo_scenarios/fair_lending.py; aurabackend/counterfactual_service/demo_scenarios/hiring_fairness.py; aurabackend/tests/test_counterfactual_engine.py:81; frontend/src/components/CounterfactualCard.tsx:513-563

## BUG-156: frontend UI/UX standards compliance (secondary, non-causal-specific)
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode completeness/UX audit (added 2026-09-21 to the standing loop rotation). Adversarial-verify: 3/3 skeptics did not refute.
- **Severity:** low — CounterfactualCard.tsx is built entirely with inline style objects, violating this repo's own mandatory frontend design system (shadcn/ui + Tailwind tokens, no inline styles), unlike its parent page which was already migrated.
- **Root cause:** frontend/CLAUDE.md states 'do not hand-roll inline style={{...}} objects for new/rebuilt components' and mandates Tailwind token utilities via ui-kit. Counterfactual.tsx (its container) carries a header comment noting it was migrated to 'native shadcn/ui + Tailwind ... no inline styles', but the CounterfactualCard.tsx it renders (all ~640 lines) uses raw style={{...}} throughout for colors, badges, and layout, with hardcoded hex values instead of tokens.css custom properties. This is an inconsistency the user's 'industry-standard UI/UX' ask would flag, though it is a style/maintainability issue rather than a functional defect.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** DUPLICATE of BUG-150, closed by it. `CounterfactualCard.tsx` was rebuilt on the ui-kit `Card`/`Button` and token utilities in PR #491 (BUG-150); the report's own citations (the raw hex maps at :75-141, the inline card at :484-512) no longer exist. The only `style` props left are data-driven percent offsets for the propensity/sensitivity/CATE glyphs, which no static utility can express, and a guard test asserts the card carries no inline style and no raw color. No separate change was needed.
- **Citations:** Citations: frontend/CLAUDE.md (design system rules); frontend/src/components/CounterfactualCard.tsx:1-644 (inline styles throughout, e.g. lines 75-141, 484-512); frontend/src/pages/Counterfactual.tsx:1-5 (header comment noting the page itself was migrated)

## BUG-157: Connectors panel — "Add PostgreSQL, MySQL, or BigQuery" empty-state promise with zero UI path to add a connection
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode completeness/UX audit (added 2026-09-21 to the standing loop rotation). Adversarial-verify: 3/3 skeptics did not refute.
- **Severity:** high — The Connectors panel's empty state tells the user to "Add PostgreSQL, MySQL, or BigQuery to query live warehouses alongside your files," but there is no button, form, dialog, or any other UI element anywhere in the frontend that lets a user actually create a database connection — the backend's rich connector-creation surface (POST /connections, /connectors/{type}/test, /connectors/{type}/tables, /connectors/registry, /databases/test/{db_type}) is fully implemented but has zero frontend callers.
- **Root cause:** A new user (or any tenant with 0 configured DB connections — the default state for a fresh deployment) opens Workbench → Connectors. The table is empty and shows only the text 'Add PostgreSQL, MySQL, or BigQuery to query live warehouses alongside your files.' There is no 'Add connection' button, no settings page, no wizard — nothing to click. The user has no way to act on the instruction the product just gave them. `connectorService.registerSource()` (services/api.ts) exists as a typed wrapper around the real POST /connections endpoint but is never called from any component; `connectorService.getSupportedDatabases()` and `connectorService.registry()` are likewise defined and unused. Grepping the whole frontend for 'Add connection'/'New connection'/'registerSource' confirms no consumer exists.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** the Connectors panel now has an **Add connection** button that expands an inline form (matching the panel's own inline-expand convention, no dialog) generated off `GET /connectors/registry`, so a fresh tenant can finally act on the empty state. On Save it calls `connectorService.registerSource` then immediately `testConnection` and reports what happened — 'Connected — N tables' or 'Saved, but the connection test failed: …' (role=alert) — instead of leaving a row that only looks fine; the list refreshes and the password is cleared from component state after the request. SCOPE, honestly: the form offers only connectors whose fields `POST /connections` can persist (`host/port/database/username/password/ssl`): PostgreSQL, MySQL, DuckDB. BigQuery (and FAISS) are deliberately NOT offered because that endpoint accepts an `extra` dict and silently discards it (filed as BUG-165), and the pipeline engine has no BigQuery branch — so the empty-state copy no longer promises BigQuery. The registry lists `port` twice for postgresql/mysql (BUG-166); the form dedupes by key with the later, defaulted definition winning. `ConnectionCredentials`/`DataSource` types were widened to match the gateway (any registry `type`, optional connection fields). 7 new tests (6 fail against the old panel; the 7th guards that the registry is not fetched until the form opens): entry point exists and BigQuery is no longer promised, only storable connectors offered with the duplicate port collapsed and defaulted to 5432, Save gated on required fields, save->test->report->refresh->password cleared, saved-but-test-failed surfaced as an alert, save failure shown and no test run. Panel suite 16/16, full frontend suite 376/376, `tsc -b`/eslint/build clean. Not exercised against a live database in this session (no reachable Postgres/MySQL here) — the backend create/test endpoints are the existing, already-tested ones. PR #499.
- **Citations:** Citations: UI trigger (misleading text, no affordance): frontend/src/workbench/panels/ConnectorsPanel.tsx:272 (emptyDescription passed to DataTable with no `action` prop) and data-table.tsx:153-155 (renders EmptyState with title+description only, no action element, when rows.length===0). Dead/unreached frontend API surface: frontend/src/services/api.ts:669-672 (registerSource, POST /connections — never called), :685-692 (getSupportedDatabases — never called), :694-699 (registry — never called). Real, working backend surface that nothing in the SPA drives: aurabackend/api_gateway/routers/connections.py:220-221 (create_connection), :84-93 (connectors/available, connectors/registry), :131-171 (test/tables/profile). Independently confirmed BigQuery specifically cannot work even via direct API use for pipeline execution: aurabackend/pipeline/models.py:29 (SourceType.BIGQUERY enum) has no branch in aurabackend/pipeline/engine.py:207-216 (_load_source only handles FILE/POSTGRESQL/MYSQL/DUCKDB/KAFKA; BIGQUERY falls through to `raise ValueError(f"Unsupported source type: {source.type}")`).

## BUG-158: Financial-audit journey — frontend entry point is demo-only, real path is a raw JSON textarea
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode completeness/UX audit (added 2026-09-21 to the standing loop rotation). Adversarial-verify: 3/3 skeptics did not refute.
- **Severity:** medium — Signing/chaining are real end-to-end, but the flagship/discoverable UI (Cockpit's Forensic Audit panel and the Terminal Audit deck) only ever runs a hardcoded canned dataset; the one path that actually lets a user submit their own ledger is a bare JSON-paste textarea buried inside the HITL Exception Queue page, not a first-class upload/mapping flow.
- **Root cause:** A user opens the Cockpit (the product's headline forensic-audit demo) and clicks 'Run demo audit' — this always POSTs the same canned SAMPLE_AUDIT_BATCH (frontend/src/audit/sampleAuditBatch.ts), never their own data, and the panel's own text says so ('DEMO SCENARIO — runs a fixed sample ledger'). The Terminal layout's 'Financial Audit' panel and its 'Findings' panel behave identically — their 'Run audit'/'Run sample audit' buttons call financialAuditService.runAudit(SAMPLE_AUDIT_BATCH) unconditionally, with no parameter for real data. The only genuine 'bring your own ledger' capability in the whole frontend is the 'Audit your ledger' box inside ExceptionQueue.tsx, which asks the user to hand-type/paste raw JSON (ledger/invoices/purchase_orders/journal_entries arrays) into a textarea — no CSV upload, no column mapping, no schema validation UI comparable to the counterfactual flow's AuditWizard (upload → map columns → review). A non-technical auditor following the Cockpit's own instructions ('use Exception Queue') lands on a page that expects them to author JSON by hand to run a real PCAOB-style audit. This is real functionality, but it falls well short of 'industry-highest-standard' UX for what the product presents as its flagship, compliance-grade capability.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** PARTIAL by design — the real-data path is no longer 'hand-author JSON', but the whole flagship journey is not rebuilt (remainder filed as BUG-167). DONE: the 'Audit your ledger' box in the Exception Queue now takes a CSV or JSON file per dataset (general ledger, invoices, purchase orders, journal entries, goods receipts, historical reports) through `LedgerFilePicker`, parsed and validated client-side by the new `audit/ledgerFiles.ts` BEFORE anything is sent: a real RFC-4180 CSV parser (quoted commas, doubled quotes, embedded newlines, BOM, CRLF — the wizard's existing preview parser is capped and newline-blind, so it was not reused); accounting-style numbers (`1,234.50`, `$1,234.50`, `(500.00)`) coerced to numbers in the numeric columns (`amount`, `approval_limit`), while identifier columns stay strings so leading zeros survive; blank cells omitted rather than sent as `""`; duplicate/empty headers, empty files, header-only files, non-numeric amounts (with row number and value) and files over 50,000 rows are BLOCKING errors shown as role=alert under that file. A file with errors blocks the run with an explicit message instead of being silently left out of a signed audit. Missing recommended columns are advisory (role=note) only: the auditor reads every key with `.get()`, so a missing column does not crash but reads as a missing value and can change or add findings (a missing `po_number` on invoices reads as 'no purchase order') — the warning says exactly that rather than 'will skip'. The JSON textarea stays as an optional advanced input; an uploaded file wins over the same key pasted as JSON, other pasted keys are kept. 34 unit tests for the parser/validator and 5 UI tests (file parsed and audited with numeric amounts; bad file explained and run blocked; missing column warns without blocking; file overrides pasted JSON key; removing the only file disables Run) — the 5 UI tests fail against the old component; full frontend suite 415/415, `tsc -b`/eslint/build clean. NOT DONE (BUG-167): no column MAPPING (files must already use the backend's column names — the AuditWizard's upload->map->review pattern has no equivalent here), and the Cockpit 'Run demo audit' / Terminal Audit and Findings panels still only run the canned sample batch (they are labelled as demos and point to the Exception Queue). PR #500.
- **Citations:** Citations: frontend/src/workbench/cockpit/ForensicAuditPanel.tsx:36-48 (demo-only notice); frontend/src/terminal/panels/AuditPanel.tsx:98 and frontend/src/terminal/audit/useAuditDeck.ts:44-49 (hardcoded SAMPLE_AUDIT_BATCH, no way to pass real data); frontend/src/terminal/panels/FindingsPanel.tsx:26-27 (same); frontend/src/audit/sampleAuditBatch.ts:1-16 (the canned dataset); frontend/src/components/HITL/ExceptionQueue.tsx:37-44 and :138-177 (the only real-data path — raw JSON textarea, no upload/mapping UI); contrast with frontend/src/audit/AuditWizard.tsx:1-112 (the counterfactual flow's proper upload→map→review wizard, which the financial-audit journey has no equivalent of).

## BUG-159: Merkle inclusion proof and per-subject ledger history are implemented server-side but never surfaced in the UI
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode completeness/UX audit (added 2026-09-21 to the standing loop rotation). Adversarial-verify: 3/3 skeptics did not refute.
- **Severity:** medium — The backend fully implements RFC-6962 Merkle inclusion proofs and per-subject audit history (the mechanism that would let an auditor independently prove a specific certificate's inclusion in the tamper-evident chain, or pull a subject's full audit trail), but no frontend component calls any of the relevant endpoints — the UI only ever shows a global, tenant-wide 'ledger intact / N records / merkle root snippet' chip.
- **Root cause:** An auditor has a record_hash from a signed certificate and wants to prove that specific record is included in the chain (the actual value proposition of a Merkle-chained ledger, per the task's premise 'can view ledger proof/history in the frontend'). The backend supports this precisely: shared/audit_ledger.py's inclusion_proof() and subject_history(), exposed as GET /audit/ledger/proof/{cert_hash}, GET /audit/ledger/subject/{subject_id}, and GET /audit/inclusion/{record_hash} in api_gateway/routers/counterfactual.py. Searching the entire frontend (src/**) for any call to these paths, or for 'inclusion_proof'/'subject_history'/'proof_hex', returns nothing. The only ledger-related UI is Workbench.tsx's header chip, which fetches only /counterfactual/audit/ledger/verify — a single aggregate boolean + count + a truncated root hash, not a per-certificate proof or per-subject history. A user cannot, anywhere in the product, view the Merkle proof for a specific certificate or the audit history of a specific subject (model/applicant/cohort) even though the ledger and its cryptographic proof machinery genuinely support it.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** both server-side capabilities now have a UI. (1) **Merkle inclusion proof**: the owner's Certificate page gets a 'Ledger inclusion proof' section with a 'Check ledger inclusion' button (on demand, no request on mount; hidden on the public read-only verify page because proofs are scoped to the signed-in tenant's chain). It fetches `GET /counterfactual/audit/ledger/proof/{cert_hash}` and RECOMPUTES the root in the browser with a new RFC 6962 verifier (`audit/merkle.ts`, a port of `shared/merkle.py::verify_inclusion` on WebCrypto) instead of trusting a server-side flag. It refuses a proof whose `cert_hash` is not the certificate on the page, shows the record hash, leaf position, path length and root, and reports a proof that does not recompute as an alert. 404 (unsigned/unchained result), 401/403 (signed out), other errors and a browser without WebCrypto (non-secure context) each get their own message rather than a crash. Two details mirrored exactly from the backend because they are easy to port wrongly: the leaf is the UTF-8 bytes of the hex record_hash STRING (not the raw digest), and leaf/node hashes carry the 0x00/0x01 prefixes. (2) **Subject history**: a 'Subject audit history' panel in the Exception Queue looks up `GET /counterfactual/audit/ledger/subject/{id}` (`financialAuditService.subjectHistory`) and lists the audits oldest-first with kind, time, preparer, reviewer and a link to each certificate; its field is labelled 'Subject to look up' because the page already has a 'Subject ID (optional)' input in the audit form. WHAT THE PROOF DOES NOT SHOW, said on screen and pinned by a test: it establishes that the displayed root commits to that record at that position; it does not show the root is the one to trust (compare with a root you pinned/published earlier), and it does not prove the tree size — RFC 6962 inclusion verification only uses the size to fix the path SHAPE, and the backend's own `verify_inclusion` agrees (for leaf 4, sizes 12..16 all verify, 17 does not), so the UI labels the size 'reported by the server'. VERIFICATION: 101 reference proofs (every leaf of trees of sizes 1..17) were generated by the backend's real `shared.merkle` into `audit/__tests__/merkleVectors.json`, and the TypeScript verifier accepts all of them and rejects wrong record hash / index / root / corrupted sibling / short or long path / non-empty proof for a single leaf / malformed hex — 33 tests. 10 component tests (verified with a real proof; tampered proof -> alert; wrong-cert refused; 404/401/403/500; no-WebCrypto; disabled while checking; no request on mount), 3 subject-history tests, 1 Certificate wiring test, 1 API URL test; the 5 wiring/API tests fail against the old code. Full frontend suite 463/463, `tsc -b`/eslint/build clean. NOT exercised against a live ledger in this session: the endpoints are the existing ones, and for counterfactual audits the ledger's `cert_hash` is the same `audit_record_hash` the Certificate shows (counterfactual_service/main.py:453), so the button should find the record; unsigned results are legitimately not chained and show the 'no ledger record' note. PR #502.
- **Citations:** Citations: aurabackend/shared/audit_ledger.py:399-427 (inclusion_proof), :345-353 (subject_history); aurabackend/api_gateway/routers/counterfactual.py:209 (/audit/ledger/proof/{cert_hash}), :221 (/audit/ledger/subject/{subject_id}), :308 (/audit/inclusion/{record_hash}); frontend/src/workbench/Workbench.tsx:100-116 (the only ledger UI — a global count/root/intact chip, no per-record or per-subject drill-down); frontend/src/audit/Certificate.tsx:68-99 (the certificate view shows signature status, hash, and a verify link, but no Merkle proof).

## BUG-160: ED25519 signing key bootstrap has a known, unresolved concurrency race that can permanently orphan a certificate's signature
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode completeness/UX audit (added 2026-09-21 to the standing loop rotation). Adversarial-verify: 3/3 skeptics did not refute.
- **Severity:** low — The self-documented BUG-100 in signing.py: on a cold instance with no persisted key yet, the check-exists → generate → write sequence is unlocked at the OS/process level for the case that actually matters (two processes/replicas), so two concurrent first-use callers can each generate and persist a different Ed25519 key, silently orphaning certificates signed with the losing key.
- **Root cause:** A fresh deployment (or a fresh replica in a multi-replica rollout) receives two near-simultaneous first calls to /audit/financial (or the counterfactual engine) before data/keys/signing_ed25519.pem exists. Per signing.py's own comment, the in-process threading.Lock only serializes callers within one process — it does not protect against two different processes/replicas racing the same check-then-generate-then-persist sequence against the shared key file. The last writer's key ends up on disk and in the second process's memory; any certificate already signed and returned to a client using the first (now-discarded) key can never be verified again, silently breaking the tamper-evident chain-of-custody guarantee for that specific certificate, without the ledger itself flagging it (verify_chain() only checks hash-chain integrity, not signature validity against the current key).
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** REPRODUCED first, then fixed. The report and the code comment both described the race but no test could see it: the only existing test (`test_resolve_key_pair_concurrent_first_use_all_get_the_same_key`) uses threads in one process, which the lock already handled. New `tests/test_signing_key_race.py` launches 6 REAL processes at the same instant against one empty key directory (generation slowed to widen the window) — against the old code **all 6 processes ended up with 6 different signing keys**, i.e. 5 of 6 would sign certificates that can never verify against the persisted key, and none of it is flagged by `verify_chain()`. Fix: `_create_persisted_key_once` writes the whole PEM to a temp file in the same directory (fsync, chmod 600) and publishes it with `os.link`, which is atomic and fails with FileExistsError if the name is taken — so the file is never visible half-written and a second creator can never overwrite the first; the loser discards its own key and ADOPTS the winner's. Filesystems without hard links fall back to `O_CREAT|O_EXCL` (still a single creator; a failed write unlinks its partial file so it cannot block later starts). `_load_persisted_key` retries briefly to ride out a file still being written on that fallback path, and a wrong-type key raises instead of being replaced. Also fixed a misleading error: in production the failure said the key directory was 'not writable' even when the directory was fine and the key FILE was the problem; it now names the underlying exception. CORRECTION to the report's theory, found while writing the tests: a half-written file was NOT overwritten by the old code — the parse failure fell through to an ephemeral key (or a RuntimeError in production), so the real hazards were the duplicate-generation race plus that degraded state; the 'never overwrite an unparseable key file' test is kept as a guard for the new path and passes on both. 3 new tests (cross-process race; deterministic 'loser adopts the winner's key'; unparseable file left untouched), the first two failing on the old code; the existing signing suites (29 tests including test_signing_persistent_key and test_counterfactual_sprint9) pass, ruff clean on CI's selection. The cross-process test waits for a shared wall-clock start (6s) so children enter the resolver together; if a loaded machine makes them start late they simply find the file already present and agree, so it can only get weaker under load, never flaky-red. Not exercised on a real multi-replica deployment or a network filesystem. PR #503.
- **Citations:** Citations: aurabackend/counterfactual_service/signing.py:66-75 (BUG-100 comment describing the exact race), :93-154 (_resolve_key_pair_locked, the unlocked-across-processes check→generate→persist sequence).

## BUG-161: test_p3_fifo_fairness_within_severity flakes under full-suite load (order-dependent test, not a product defect)
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** pre-push full backend suite run for BUG-145's push, incidentally (2604 passed, 1 failed: `AssertionError: ['M1', 'M3', 'M2'] == ['M1', 'M2', 'M3']`). Passed 15/15 in isolation, confirming order/load-dependence per testing.md.
- **Severity:** low — test-only; no product code path is affected. Blocks every push while the suite is loaded enough to trigger it, though, so it isn't cosmetic.
- **Root cause:** `DistributedRepairCoordinator._enqueue` derives FIFO order from a real Redis `INCR` (`uasr/distributed_repair.py:224`), executed via `asyncio.to_thread`. The test asserted that order using 2-4ms real-time `asyncio.sleep` gaps between task creations as a proxy for enqueue order. Under this suite's full ~2600-test, 40+ minute run, default-executor thread-pool contention from every other concurrent `to_thread` call in flight can delay a task's `to_thread(incr)` dispatch past that 2-4ms window, letting a later-created task's `incr` actually execute first — reordering the FIFO result though the coordinator's own admission fairness never broke. The sibling test `test_p2_cross_node_priority_ordering` uses 50ms gaps and has never been reported flaky.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing, timing-sensitive by construction; surfaced by this session's slow full-suite pre-push run, not by any code change.
- **Fix:** widened `test_p3_fifo_fairness_within_severity`'s inter-submission gaps from 2-4ms to 50ms, matching P2's proven-robust margin — test intent (fleet-wide FIFO fairness within a severity) is unchanged. Verified 15/15 passes in isolation plus a clean full-file run. PR #480.

## BUG-162: test_scalability_fixes.py's "ticks >= N" assertions flake under full-suite load (order-dependent, not a product defect)
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** two separate pre-push full backend suite runs (once for BUG-161, once for a docs-only push), both times `test_sql_generator_llm_call_does_not_block_event_loop` alone: `AssertionError: only 19 ticker iterations ran ... assert 19 >= 20`. Passed in isolation both times.
- **Severity:** low — test-only; no product code path is affected. Repeatedly blocks pushes while the suite is under load, though.
- **Root cause:** each of the file's 11 tests spawns a 10ms ticker alongside a mocked slow sync LLM call (offloaded via `asyncio.to_thread`) and asserts the ticker ran at least 40% of the ideal tick count for that call's duration, to prove the event loop wasn't blocked. Under this suite's full ~2600-test, 44-minute run, real thread-pool contention from every other concurrent `to_thread` call in flight measurably slows the ticker's own scheduling (not just this one test's — all 11 share the same fragile margin, this one simply sat closest to its threshold), so the 40%-of-ideal bound was tight enough to flake though the event loop was never actually blocked.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing, timing-sensitive by construction; surfaced by this session's slow full-suite pre-push runs, not by any code change.
- **Fix:** lowered every "ticks >= N" threshold in `test_scalability_fixes.py` from 40% to 20% of each test's ideal tick count — still fails hard on a real regression (a genuinely blocked loop services next to none), with real headroom against full-suite load. Verified full-file pass (12/12) plus a clean ruff check. PR #481.

## BUG-163: test_dar_research_agent_does_not_block_event_loop's elapsed-time sanity check flakes under full-suite load (order-dependent, not a product defect)
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** pre-push full backend suite run for BUG-149's push, incidentally: `AssertionError: assert 0.7035060999915004 < 0.69`.
- **Severity:** low — test-only; no product code path is affected. Blocks pushes while the suite is under load.
- **Root cause:** the test's real proof that `DARResearchAgent`'s LLM call is offloaded (not blocking the event loop) is `tick_count == 20` — a concurrently-scheduled ticker completing all 20 ticks while the blocking call is in flight. A secondary `elapsed < 0.69` "sanity" check tried to additionally confirm concurrent (not serial) execution using a wall-clock margin against a stated 0.7s serial floor (0.3s LLM + 0.4s ticker). Under this suite's full ~2600-test, 38+ minute run, scheduling contention alone pushed even this genuinely-concurrent run's elapsed time to 0.7035s — past even the theoretical serial floor, showing elapsed time can't reliably discriminate concurrent from serial once the whole process is under load. The test's own comment already stated tick_count is "the real proof" and the elapsed check was just slack around it.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing, timing-sensitive by construction; surfaced by this session's slow full-suite pre-push runs, not by any code change.
- **Fix:** widened the elapsed-time bound from 0.69s to 5.0s, turning it from a (load-unreliable) concurrency discriminator into a loose hang-detector — the load-independent `tick_count == 20` assertion remains the test's real proof. Verified the file passes in isolation. PR #487.

## BUG-164: pytest intermittently hangs at exit after "N passed" (leaked aiosqlite worker thread) — the pre-push gate freezes
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** three pre-push runs in one session (BUG-149 docs branch, BUG-150, and a retry) printed `2605 passed` and then never returned; the pytest worker sat at 0 CPU for 80+ minutes until killed. Each kill lost the whole push and forced a ~40 min rerun.
- **Severity:** medium — test infrastructure only, but it blocks every push through the pre-push hook and wastes the full-suite runtime each time. The hang is intermittent (the same commit pushed clean on retry), which made it look like machine memory pressure rather than a defect.
- **Root cause:** aiosqlite runs each connection on a NON-DAEMON worker thread (`_connection_worker_thread`, parked in `queue.get()`), so one connection still open at interpreter shutdown blocks `threading._shutdown` forever. `conftest.pytest_sessionfinish` already closed joblib/loky's pool and disposed three named module-level engines, but a thread-instrumented full-suite run (probe plugin recording each connection's origin + a post-cleanup non-daemon thread census) showed a live connection to `.../test_audit_wiring_creates_job_0/data/audit_ledger.db` — the audit ledger's engine, which uses a relative sqlite URL and is re-pointed at per-test `tmp_path` databases — surviving that cleanup. Which connections survive depends on test order and GC timing, hence the intermittence (one probe run left a live thread, another left a live connection with the thread already gone).
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing; the third distinct source of the exit-hang class already patched twice in `conftest.py` (loky pool, three named engines).
- **Fix:** `conftest.pytest_sessionfinish` now finishes with a generic safety net: it scans for every live `aiosqlite.Connection` and calls its public, synchronous `stop()` (close the sqlite handle + end the worker thread), so no per-engine allow-list can miss a leaker. New `tests/test_conftest_exit_hang.py` runs a child pytest with a copy of this conftest and a test that deliberately leaks an open connection: without the fix the child prints `1 passed` and then hangs until the 90s timeout (test fails); with it the child exits in ~2s. PR #492.

## BUG-165: POST /connections silently discards the `extra` config dict, so BigQuery/FAISS connections cannot be created
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** BUG-157 fix — reading `create_connection` while deciding which connectors the new Add-connection form could offer.
- **Severity:** medium — accepted input is dropped with a 200, so any connector whose config is not host/port/database/username/password/ssl (BigQuery: project_id/dataset/credentials_json; FAISS: dimension/index_type) appears to save but stores nothing usable.
- **Root cause:** `ConnectionCreateRequest` declares `extra: Dict[str, Any]` (aurabackend/api_gateway/routers/connections.py:67), but `create_connection` builds its `record` from only `name/type/host/port/database/username/ssl` (+ the password passed separately) and never reads `req.extra`, so nothing reaches `persistence.insert_connection`. The registry meanwhile advertises those connectors as `available` with fields the endpoint cannot store.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** PARTIAL by design: the SILENT DATA LOSS is removed; persisting the settings is not done (filed as BUG-170). Reproduced first: before the change `POST /connections` returned 200 for a FAISS connection carrying `extra: {dimension, index_type}` and for a BigQuery connection, storing none of the connector-specific settings. `create_connection` now answers 400 instead: (1) a non-empty `extra` -> 400 'Connector-specific settings (`extra`) are not stored yet, so they would be silently lost', with `unsupported_fields` listing the keys; (2) a connector whose REQUIRED registry fields include settings outside `host/port/database/username/password/ssl` (today only BigQuery: `project_id`, `credentials_json`) -> 400 naming them. Neither creates a row (asserted). Deliberately NOT a validator: this endpoint has never enforced the registry's `required` flags for storable fields (existing tests create a `postgresql` connection with no password), an empty `extra` is still fine, and that policy is unchanged (pinned by a test). The Connectors form from BUG-157 already only offers storable connectors, so the UI is consistent with the new behaviour. 3 new tests in `tests/test_connector_registry.py` (2 fail on the old code with `200 == 400`); 54 connection-related tests pass, ruff clean. Note the two pre-fix runs of those tests wrote rows into the shared test gateway DB; the guard tests compare counts before/after so they are unaffected. PR #506.
- **Citations:** Needs a decision on where connector-specific config lives (encrypted alongside the password for secrets like `credentials_json`), plus a pipeline `_load_source` branch for BigQuery (aurabackend/pipeline/engine.py:207-216, noted in BUG-157) before the UI should offer it. Citations: aurabackend/api_gateway/routers/connections.py:58-67,221-260; aurabackend/connectors/registry.py (bigquery/faiss specs).

## BUG-166: connector registry lists the `port` field twice for postgresql and mysql
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** BUG-157 fix — a registry-driven form rendered two Port inputs for the same key.
- **Severity:** low — cosmetic/duplicate-key hazard for any generic consumer of `GET /connectors/registry`; the Connectors form now works around it.
- **Root cause:** `_DB_FIELDS` already contains `ConnectorField("port", ..., required=True)` (aurabackend/connectors/registry.py:126) and the postgresql/mysql specs then do `list(_DB_FIELDS) + [ConnectorField("port", ..., default=5432|3306)]` (:157, :169), so the same key appears twice — the first without a default, the second with one.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** `connectors/registry.py` now builds the postgresql and mysql field lists with a new `_override_field`, which swaps the shared `port` field for the connector's own (default 5432 / 3306) IN PLACE — same position, right after host — instead of appending a second one. `register_connector` now also REJECTS any spec that lists a field key more than once (ValueError naming the key, spec not registered), so the class of bug cannot return through a built-in or third-party registration. New `tests/test_connector_registry_fields.py` (10 tests): every registered connector lists each key once; postgresql/mysql have exactly one required `port` with the right default, positioned after host; the wire form (`to_dict`, incl. `config_required`) has no repeats; a duplicate-key spec is rejected and not registered — 6 of them FAIL on the old registry. The 47 existing connector tests still pass; ruff clean. The Connectors form's client-side dedupe (BUG-157) is kept as a defensive measure and its comment updated to say the registry no longer serves the duplicate. PR #505.
- **Citations:** Give the shared `port` field no entry in `_DB_FIELDS`, or build the specs by replacing it (override by key) rather than appending. Citations: aurabackend/connectors/registry.py:126,157,169.

## BUG-167: financial-audit bring-your-own-data flow has no column mapping, and the flagship panels stay sample-only
- **Status:** fixed (partial)
- **Found by:** BUG-158 fix — the remainder of that report that was deliberately not done in one change.
- **Severity:** medium — a non-technical auditor can now upload files, but only if their headers already match the backend's field names (`internal_id`, `account_code`, `amount`, `invoice_number`, `po_number`, ...); anything else uploads 'successfully' with only an advisory warning and can yield misleading findings (a missing/misnamed `po_number` reads as 'no purchase order'). The Cockpit's 'Run demo audit' and the Terminal Audit/Findings panels still only run `SAMPLE_AUDIT_BATCH`.
- **Root cause:** `audit/ledgerFiles.ts` validates types and reports absent recommended columns but has no header-to-field mapping step; `ForensicAuditPanel.tsx`, `terminal/panels/AuditPanel.tsx` (+ `useAuditDeck.ts`) and `FindingsPanel.tsx` call `financialAuditService.runAudit(SAMPLE_AUDIT_BATCH)` unconditionally with no way to supply real data.
- **Caused by:** none — the gap predates BUG-158 and is the part of it left open.
- **Fix:** the upload half is done. `parseLedgerFile` takes an optional `ColumnMapping` (backend field -> file column) that renames columns before numeric coercion and the missing-column check; `suggestMapping` auto-applies exact header matches ignoring case/punctuation ("PO Number" -> po_number), never near-misses. `LedgerFilePicker` shows a select per missing recommended field (auto-filled, user-changeable) and re-parses on change; nothing is persisted. Tests: 6 in `ledgerFiles.test.ts`, 1 component test in `ExceptionQueue.test.tsx`; frontend suites, eslint and `npm run build` pass. NOT done: the Cockpit `ForensicAuditPanel` and Terminal `AuditPanel`/`FindingsPanel` still only run `SAMPLE_AUDIT_BATCH` (that needs a design decision on where they get real data); no browser check of the new selects. PR #510.


## BUG-168: CI 'Storage S3 (MinIO integration)' lane broken for every PR — MinIO's community image is no longer pullable from any official channel
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** PR #500 (BUG-158, a frontend-only change): the lane failed in ~5s on two consecutive runs while passing on PRs merged an hour earlier the same day.
- **Severity:** high — the failure is independent of any code change, hits every PR and `main`, and blocks merging (CLAUDE.md: all CI jobs green before merge).
- **Root cause:** the `storage-s3` job did `docker run ... quay.io/minio/minio server /data`. The log shows `docker: Error response from daemon: unauthorized: access to the requested resource is not authorized` (exit 125) at the pull. Verified independently rather than assumed to be transient: anonymous `GET https://quay.io/v2/minio/minio/manifests/latest` and the quay repo API both return 401; Docker Hub `minio/minio` (any tag) returns 401; and `dl.min.io/server/minio/release/linux-amd64/archive/minio.RELEASE.*` returns **410 Gone** for the pinned 2025 releases — MinIO has withdrawn its community distribution from every official channel. This is the second break of this lane (Docker Hub in Sept 2026, then quay.io) and both came from depending on a floating `latest` tag on someone else's registry.
- **Caused by:** none — external; not triggered by any repo change.
- **Fix:** PR #501, VERIFIED in CI (see the last sentence). `tests/test_storage_s3_duckdb.py` needs only an S3-compatible endpoint on :9000 with static keys and path-style access (it creates its own bucket via boto3), so nothing about it is MinIO-specific. The lane now runs SeaweedFS's S3 gateway, image PINNED to `chrislusf/seaweedfs:4.47` (anonymously pullable, released 2026-09-14): `server -s3 -s3.port=9000 -dir=/data`. The readiness wait no longer polls MinIO's `/minio/health/live`; it waits for `/` to answer 200/403 (5xx = filer not ready yet), and a new `if: failure()` step prints the server logs so a failure is diagnosable. The job name is deliberately unchanged so a branch-protection required-check name still matches. No local validation was possible (Docker Desktop not running on the dev machine); the PR's own CI run was the test. RESULT: the `Storage S3 (MinIO integration)` job passed on both of that PR's runs (1m11s and 1m20s), and the job log shows the readiness wait reporting `s3 up (200)` and all three Tier-B tests PASSED — not skipped: `test_duckdb_reads_csv_from_s3`, `test_etl_preview_and_execute_read_uploaded_file_from_s3` and `test_pipeline_execute_reads_uploaded_file_from_s3` — so DuckDB path-style S3 reads, the ETL path and pipeline execution all work against SeaweedFS with the test's static keys. Read from the job log rather than assumed from the green check, because a Tier-B lane that silently skips also goes green. Citations: .github/workflows/ci.yml (job `storage-s3`); aurabackend/tests/test_storage_s3_duckdb.py.

## BUG-169: StreamingEngine.stop() can wait forever when a loop swallows its cancellation — froze the Python 3.11 CI lane for 20 minutes
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** PR #503 (BUG-160): both CI runs (push and pull_request) of that PR hung in the `Backend Tests (Python 3.11)` job at ~72% and were still 'in progress' 27 minutes later, versus ~4 minutes normally; the Python 3.12 job passed. The cancelled run's log ends with the suite's own faulthandler watchdog (`faulthandler_timeout = 1200`) dumping every thread.
- **Severity:** high — a shutdown that can hang forever is a service-lifecycle bug for the streaming pipelines (a stop request that never completes), and it blocks CI whenever it fires.
- **Root cause:** the dump names the stuck test: `tests/test_streaming.py::test_engine_pause_resume`, main thread parked in the asyncio event loop at `loop.run_until_complete(engine.stop())`, every worker thread idle — so `stop()` was awaiting something that would never resolve. `stop()` did `self._task.cancel(); await self._task` (and the same for the ingest task), which only returns if the task actually ends. The engine's loops call `asyncio.wait_for(self._buffer.get(), timeout=0.5)` on every tick (`backpressure.py` `get_batch`), and on Python 3.11 `wait_for` can swallow a cancellation that races its inner future completing (fixed in 3.12) — the loop then keeps running, so `await task` never returns. A second defect was found while reproducing: the `except asyncio.CancelledError: pass` wrapped around that await could not tell 'the task I awaited was cancelled' from 'I was cancelled', so it also swallowed a cancellation aimed at `stop()` itself — which is why a `wait_for`-guarded test appeared to pass after burning its whole timeout.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing; exposed by timing.
- **Fix:** `stop()` now sets a `_stopping` flag that both loops check (`while not self._stopping`), and awaits each loop task through a new `_cancel_loop_task`: it cancels, waits with `asyncio.wait` (which neither raises the task's cancellation nor hides ours), and RE-cancels every 0.5s up to a 5s bound, after which it logs an error and continues shutting down rather than hanging. `start()` resets the flag. New `tests/test_streaming_engine_stop.py`: each loop is parked inside an await that swallows the first cancellation (simulating the 3.11 behaviour deterministically instead of relying on winning the race) and `stop()` must finish within 5s — the run-loop and ingest-loop cases FAIL on the old code ('engine.stop() had not finished after 5s') and pass now, plus a pause/resume/stop guard. The helper deliberately uses `asyncio.wait`, not `wait_for`, because the second defect made a `wait_for`-guarded version pass on the old code. The 87 tests in `test_streaming.py` + the new file pass; ruff clean. WHAT IS NOT PROVEN: the real Python 3.11 `wait_for` race was not reproduced here (only 3.13 is available locally), so that it is the exact trigger of the CI hang is inferred from the stack, the idle threads and the code, not observed; and it is unexplained why ~38 earlier CI runs never hit it while both runs of one PR did (a timing shift from that PR's new subprocess-spawning test is possible but unconfirmed). CONFIRMATION (after merge): PR #503, which hung twice, was updated from main with this fix and both of its Python 3.11 backend jobs then passed in ~4 minutes (versus 27+ min stuck) — consistent with the diagnosis, though the exact trigger remains inferred. PR #504.

## BUG-170: connector-specific settings (BigQuery project/credentials, FAISS dimension/index_type) cannot be persisted, so those connectors cannot be created
- **Status:** fixed (partial)
- **Found by:** BUG-165 fix — what was left after making `POST /connections` stop silently discarding them.
- **Severity:** medium — BigQuery is advertised by the connector registry and the product copy but cannot be added through the API or the UI; FAISS can be created only without its dimension/index type.
- **Root cause:** the connection store (`persistence.insert_connection` and its table) has columns only for `name/type/host/port/database/username/ssl` plus the separately-encrypted password; there is no place for arbitrary connector config, and a secret such as BigQuery's `credentials_json` must be stored ENCRYPTED like the password. Separately, the pipeline engine has no BigQuery branch (`aurabackend/pipeline/engine.py` `_load_source` falls through to `Unsupported source type`), so persisting the settings alone would still not make a BigQuery source runnable.
- **Caused by:** none — a feature gap, previously hidden by the silent drop.
- **Fix:** (approved by the user for this migration.) New Alembic revision `a7b8c9d0e1f2` adds ONE nullable `Text` column `gateway_connections.config_encrypted` (additive; existing rows read as 'no extra settings'; downgrade drops it; the gateway applies migrations at startup, so this reaches production on the next deploy). `insert_connection(record, password, extra)` stores `extra` as a Fernet token over JSON via the same `encrypt_secret` as the password; `get_connection_extra` decrypts it (workspace-scoped) only where a connector is opened, and it is never in the wire dict. `POST /connections` now validates `extra` against the connector's declared fields (unknown keys, missing required settings, `credentials_json` not a JSON object, >64KB all 400 without echoing values) instead of rejecting everything; the test/introspect/sync endpoints build the connector config from stored settings via `_stored_connector_config` (project_id->database, credentials_json parsed, the rest->extra_params). Tests (7 new/rewritten in test_connector_registry.py): rejects unknown/missing/bad credentials, response never carries extra, raw column holds no plaintext and round-trips, another workspace reads {}, BigQuery mapping. tests/conftest.py now deletes the stale throwaway gateway test DB at session start (create_all never ALTERs, so an old file raised 'no such column'). Migration exercised in isolation (upgrade adds column, data intact; downgrade removes it); the full alembic chain could not be run locally (an older revision, c7d8e9f0a1b2, fails on a clean SQLite -- pre-existing, CI runs it on Postgres), so CI's DB Migrations lane is the real check. Frontend follow-up (this second PR): `AddConnectionForm` now offers every available connector, renders `textarea` fields (BigQuery credentials) and sends non-column fields under `extra`; ConnectorsPanel tests updated plus a BigQuery payload test; workbench suite (71), eslint and build pass, not checked in a browser. NOT done: (2) the pipeline engine has no BigQuery branch, so a saved BigQuery connection can be tested/synced through the connector API but not run as a pipeline source; (3) not exercised against a real BigQuery project. PR #517 (backend); frontend PR #518.


## BUG-171: cleanup_verify_resources.py silently inspects nothing when run on the host, then reports 'Nothing to do.' with exit 0
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** the 2026-09-24 live verification. `verify_live_deployment.py` passed 14/14 against https://dataaura.duckdns.org, then the documented cleanup step (`python3 scripts/cleanup_verify_resources.py` on the box) printed `[skip] database not found` for BOTH databases followed by `Nothing to do.` and exit 0. Re-running with the real paths found 15 leftover rows: the persistent `verify-uasr@example.com` login and 14 rows across 7 accumulated `verify_<hex>_uasr` sources in `uasr_drift_events` / `uasr_recovery_records`.
- **Severity:** low-medium — tooling, not product, but it defeats the point of the step: every verification run leaves rows on production, and a cleanup that reads as 'clean' while having examined nothing hides that they are accumulating (7 runs' worth had built up unnoticed).
- **Root cause:** `DEFAULT_METADATA_DB` / `DEFAULT_UASR_DB` are `/data/state/*.db`, the CONTAINER's paths. Run on the host (as the procedure says) they do not exist — the same files are in the `aura_aura-data` Docker volume at `/var/lib/docker/volumes/aura_aura-data/_data/state/`, which needs sudo — so `open_db_readonly` returned `None` for both, `total_rows` stayed 0, and `main()` fell through to `Nothing to do.` / exit 0. `[skip]` for one half is legitimate (different mounts, a container not running); skipping BOTH and then claiming there is nothing to do is not.
- **Caused by:** none — the script was written against the container layout and never exercised on the host.
- **Fix:** (1) a default path that is missing now falls back to the Docker-volume state directory (`[note]` printed; overridable with `AURA_CLEANUP_FALLBACK_STATE_DIR`); a path the operator passed explicitly is NEVER substituted — that is how a half is deliberately skipped, e.g. to leave the persistent test login alone, which is what was done for the manual cleanup. (2) When neither database could be opened the script now prints `NOTHING WAS CHECKED: neither database could be opened, so leftover test rows may still be there.` with the flags to use, and exits 2 instead of 0. One missing half is still skipped with exit 0 (existing tests unchanged). 3 new subprocess tests, driving the script as an operator would: neither DB openable -> exit 2 + the warning and not 'Nothing to do.' (fails on the old script, which returned 0); default paths missing + fallback present -> found and reported with the exact dry-run count (fails on the old script); an explicit path is never replaced by the fallback (guard). 9/9 in the file pass, ruff clean. MANUAL CLEANUP DONE on production for this run: the 14 per-run `verify_*_uasr` rows were deleted by running the fixed-path command with `--metadata-db` pointed at a non-existent file so the persistent test login (whose credentials the verifier uses) was deliberately left; the dry run listed exactly those rows first. Citations: scripts/cleanup_verify_resources.py (DEFAULT_*_DB, main); aurabackend/tests/test_cleanup_verify_resources.py. PR #508.

## BUG-172: SQLAlchemy 2.1.0 release broke every CI lane that imports the async layer (greenlet no longer installed) — `main` red, and a Docker rebuild would have shipped a broken image
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** the push CI run for `main` at 20:22 UTC on 2026-09-24 (right after PR #506 merged) failed in 9 of 17 jobs — Backend Tests 3.11 and 3.12, Causal, Streaming, Scheduler (Postgres), DB Migrations, Storage S3, Contract Tests and SDK Codegen Sync — while PR #506's own run of the identical code had passed ~30 minutes earlier. Reported by the user ("ci's are failing").
- **Severity:** critical — it took CI down for the whole repo, and the same unpinned range is what `docker build` resolves, so the next image rebuild/deploy would have shipped a gateway that cannot import.
- **Root cause:** not a code change. `aurabackend/requirements.txt` and `pyproject.toml` declared `sqlalchemy>=2.0,<3.0`. SQLAlchemy **2.1.0 was released between the two runs** (PyPI lists 2.1.0 above 2.0.54) and no longer installs `greenlet` by default; its asyncio layer needs it. The passing run installed `sqlalchemy-2.0.54` with `greenlet-3.5.6`; the failing run installed `sqlalchemy-2.1.0` and no greenlet. Every `import sqlalchemy.ext.asyncio` then raised `ImportError: The SQLAlchemy asyncio module requires that the Python 'greenlet' library is installed` — pytest collection of `tests/test_api_gateway_persistence.py`, `alembic upgrade` (env.py), the gateway itself — which is why nine unrelated-looking lanes failed together. REPRODUCED locally rather than inferred: in an interpreter with site-packages excluded, `sqlalchemy==2.1.0` alone reproduces that exact ImportError, while `sqlalchemy>=2.0,<2.1` pulls `2.0.54` + `greenlet 3.5.6` and imports cleanly.
- **Caused by:** none in this repo — an upstream release landing under an open-ended (`<3.0`) range; latent since the range was written, triggered by release day.
- **Fix:** both dependency files now declare `sqlalchemy>=2.0,<2.1` and an explicit `greenlet>=3.0,<4.0` (the async layer is used directly, so it should not depend on SQLAlchemy pulling it in). The cap keeps CI and Docker on the 2.0.x line the code was built and tested on; adopting 2.1 is a deliberate upgrade to test separately, not something a `<3.0` range should do on release day. New `tests/test_dependency_contract.py` (6 tests) reads the two declaration files and asserts the cap, the explicit greenlet and that the files agree, plus that the running environment can import `sqlalchemy.ext.asyncio` — 4 of the 6 fail against the old declarations. Full local suite and lint pass; CONFIRMED in CI: on PR #507 every lane that failed on main (Backend 3.11/3.12, Causal, Streaming, Scheduler, DB Migrations, Storage S3, Contract Tests, SDK Codegen Sync) passed, `main`'s post-merge run went 17/17 green, and the CD image rebuild (a cache miss, because requirements.txt changed) resolved `sqlalchemy-2.0.54` + `greenlet-3.5.6`. Production was never affected: the images published from the red push reused a cached `pip wheel` layer from the earlier good build. PR #507.

## BUG-173: dependency resolution is unpinned, so CI and Docker builds change underneath us whenever any upstream releases
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** BUG-172 — the second time in one day an unpinned external dependency broke the pipeline (MinIO's image in BUG-168, SQLAlchemy 2.1.0 in BUG-172).
- **Severity:** medium — recurring, and each occurrence blocks all merges until someone diagnoses it.
- **Root cause:** `requirements.txt` uses open ranges (`<3.0`, `<2.0`, `>=x`) with no lock file or constraints file, so every CI run and every image build resolves the newest matching release of every direct AND transitive dependency. Nothing distinguishes 'the code changed' from 'the world changed' when a run goes red, and a scheduled or deploy-time rebuild can pick up a breaking release with no code change at all.
- **Caused by:** none — a build-reproducibility gap.
- **Fix:** new `aurabackend/constraints.txt`, generated with `uv pip compile requirements.txt requirements-causal.txt requirements-streaming.txt --universal --python-version 3.11` (237 pins, covers 3.11-3.13), applied as `-c constraints.txt` at every `pip install -r requirements*.txt` in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` and every `pip wheel`/`pip install` in `aurabackend/Dockerfile` (which now COPYs it). `tests/test_constraints_file.py` fails if a new install site omits `-c` or SQLAlchemy drifts off 2.0.x. Checked: `uv pip compile -c constraints.txt` succeeds for base, +causal and +streaming on 3.11, 3.12 and 3.13. NOT proven: the PR's own CI is the first real install from the file across all lanes. NOT done: the scheduled 'try latest versions' job, and requirements-multimodal.txt is not covered. Upgrades are now a deliberate regenerate-and-commit. PR #509.


## BUG-174: a deeply nested `credentials_json` string made POST /connections return 500 instead of 400
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit (3 lenses + adversarial verify) of the BUG-170 connector-settings path, 2026-09-26. Secret-handling and tenant-isolation lenses found nothing; the validation lens found this, and the verifier confirmed it from the code.
- **Severity:** low -- a wrong status code (500), no crash and no data exposure.
- **Root cause:** `_validated_extra` parsed `credentials_json` with `json.loads` inside `except ValueError`. CPython raises `RecursionError` (a `RuntimeError`, not a `ValueError`) for a deeply nested document, and the 64KB size check ran only after the parse, so a few-KB string of `[` characters escaped as an unhandled exception and the catch-all handler turned it into a 500.
- **Caused by:** BUG-170 (PR #517) -- introduced by that change.
- **Fix:** catch `(ValueError, RecursionError)` around the parse (-> 400 'must be a JSON object'), refuse an oversized string before parsing (-> 413), and wrap the final `json.dumps` size check so a non-plain-JSON structure gets a 400 rather than a 500. Two new tests in `tests/test_connector_registry.py`; both fail on the old code (RecursionError / 400 instead of 413) and pass now. Not checked: nesting inside a non-string `credentials_json` object sent as JSON (the request parser bounds that first). PR #520.

## BUG-175: schema_columns index is not tenant-scoped: one tenant's upload overwrites another's, and column names + sample values are readable across tenants
- **Status:** fixed (partial)
- **Found by:** ultracode audit (3 lenses + adversarial verify) of the upload / xlsx / schema-context path, 2026-09-26. Verifier confirmed from the code; not run end to end unless stated.
- **Severity:** high
- **Root cause:** `shared/schema_indexer.py:50` derives `source_id` from `Path(file_path).stem`, dropping the per-tenant storage directory, so two tenants uploading `sales.csv` share source_id `sales`. `SchemaColumn` (`metadata_store/models.py:89-101`) has no tenant/workspace column and its unique key is (source_id, table_name, column_name); `_upsert_columns` (`schema_indexer.py:149-164`) deletes by source_id+table_name only, so B's upload wipes A's rows. The MCP tools `metadata_search_columns` / `metadata_describe_table` (`mcp_servers/aura_mcp_server.py` ~289-351) query it with no tenant filter and return sample_values (first rows of the file). Not checked: whether the MCP server is reachable by an authenticated tenant; the overwrite/delete half does not depend on that.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** the overwrite/delete half is fixed without a schema change: new `schema_source_id(tenant, filename)` makes `source_id` `<tenant_slug>::<stem>`, and `upload_universal` passes it to `index_uploaded_file`, so two tenants' `sales.csv` no longer share a key (test: both tenants' rows survive; fails on the old code with ImportError/assert; plus a check that the route uses the helper -- a source-level assertion, weaker than driving the upload). NOT fixed: (1) the READ half -- `metadata_search_columns` / `metadata_describe_table` in `mcp_servers/aura_mcp_server.py` still query `schema_columns` with no tenant filter, so column names and sample values remain readable across tenants if that server is reachable by a tenant (not verified either way); a real fix needs a tenant column (migration) or caller-scoped queries and is a decision for the owner; (2) rows indexed before this change keep the old bare ids and can still be overwritten/collide; (3) the MCP tools now surface `<tenant>::<stem>` ids, so callers passing a bare `source_id` to `describe_table` see no rows for new uploads. PR #522.


## BUG-176: LocalBackend.list only returned .csv/.parquet/.json, so uploaded .xlsx workbooks never reached the schema context on local storage (BUG-146's fix was incomplete)
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit (3 lenses + adversarial verify) of the upload / xlsx / schema-context path, 2026-09-26. Verifier confirmed from the code; not run end to end unless stated.
- **Severity:** medium
- **Root cause:** `shared/storage/local.py` `_READ_EXTS` omitted `.xlsx`; `build_schema_context` and the cache-recipe path iterate `backend.list(tenant)` and only then check `EXCEL_EXTENSIONS`, so the Excel branch added for BUG-146 was unreachable locally (S3 lists everything, so S3 was unaffected -- but S3 Excel is rejected by design).
- **Caused by:** BUG-146 (PR #515) -- its fix left this gap.
- **Fix:** `.xlsx` added to `_READ_EXTS`. New test `test_local_storage_lists_xlsx_and_schema_context_includes_it` writes a real workbook into a `LocalBackend`, asserts it is listed and that `build_schema_context` yields the table with its columns; it fails without the change (`[] == ['sales.xlsx']`). PR #521.

## BUG-177: build_schema_context_cached lists storage synchronously on the event loop (S3 = blocking network round trips on a single-worker gateway)
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit (3 lenses + adversarial verify) of the upload / xlsx / schema-context path, 2026-09-26. Verifier confirmed from the code; not run end to end unless stated.
- **Severity:** medium
- **Root cause:** `shared/data_utils.py:728` calls `_signature_for_tenant` (which calls `backend.list(tenant)`, a blocking boto3 paginator on S3 / iterdir+stat locally) directly inside an async function awaited by every chat, query and dashboard request; every other blocking step in that function is offloaded with `asyncio.to_thread`. Violates `.claude/rules/backend.md` (async safety).
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** `await asyncio.to_thread(_signature_for_tenant, effective_tenant)` (its only call site). New test replaces `_signature_for_tenant` with a stub recording its thread and asserts it is not the event-loop thread; it fails on the old code and passes now (it lives in `tests/test_excel_loading.py` alongside the other data_utils tests). Not measured: the actual latency win against a real S3 endpoint. PR #523.


## BUG-178: filename -> table-name mapping collides and silently overwrites tables (q1-sales.csv vs q1_sales.csv, sales.csv vs sales.parquet)
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit (3 lenses + adversarial verify) of the upload / xlsx / schema-context path, 2026-09-26. Verifier confirmed from the code; not run end to end unless stated.
- **Severity:** medium
- **Root cause:** `shared/data_utils.py` (~440 and ~675) builds `table_name = re.sub(r"[^A-Za-z0-9_]", "_", stem)` with no duplicate check, so distinct files can map to one table and the later load `CREATE OR REPLACE`s the earlier, silently.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** new `_unique_table_name(filename, taken)` used at both sites (`build_schema_context` and the cache-recipe builder): the first file keeps the plain sanitised name, a colliding one gets its extension appended, then a counter. Listing is sorted so names are stable across runs. Tests: a real `LocalBackend` with `q1-sales.csv`/`q1_sales.csv` and `sales.csv`/`sales.parquet` yields 4 distinct tables with the right row counts (fails on the old code: a file silently replaced), plus a unit test of the naming rule. Caveat: when a collision exists the second file's table name is new, so an LLM-generated query written against the old (shadowed) name would now hit the first file's table; that ambiguity is what the bug was. PR #524.


## BUG-179: upload size limit is enforced only after the whole multipart body has been received and spooled
- **Status:** fixed (partial)
- **Found by:** ultracode audit (3 lenses + adversarial verify) of the upload / xlsx / schema-context path, 2026-09-26. Verifier confirmed from the code; not run end to end unless stated.
- **Severity:** medium
