- **Root cause:** `uasr/conformal_martingale.py` ~302: service.py:834-836 runs `_mapek_worker._martingale.register_baseline` in a worker thread via asyncio.to_thread. The MAPE-K loop runs `_analyze_detect_drift` in another thread (mapek_worker.py:429), which calls `self._martingale.update` (694), `diagnostics` (698) and `baseline_stats` (720). register_baseline is not atomic: it resets `_baselines[source_id]`, `_detectors[source_id]` and `_last_distance[source_id]` to empty dicts (conformal_martingale.py:302-304) and refills them column by column (305-313). update() reads `_detectors` (328) and then indexes `self._baselines[source_id][column]` (331). The class has no lock, unlike DriftDetector's per-source threading.Lock (drift_detector.py:116-123). Failure scenario: With UASR_S18 martingale detection on, an operator POSTs /uasr/baseline for the Kafka source while a batch is being analysed. update() gets the old detector at line 328, then hits KeyError at line 331 because the baselines dict was just emptied (swallowed as a warning at mapek_worker.py:695-697), or it feeds the batch into a detector object that is being discarded and sees no detector for columns not yet refilled. The drifted batch's martingale evidence is dropped for those columns, and the alarm for that batch is missed or delayed.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** `ConformalMartingaleRegistry` takes an `RLock` in `register_baseline`, `reset_source`, `update`, `diagnostics` and `baseline_stats`. Not reproduced: a concurrent re-baseline/read stress test also passes on the old code, so this is a hardening that is not proven against the old behaviour; the regression test only proves the methods now take the lock (`tests/test_uasr_martingale_registry_threadsafe.py`).

## BUG-276: SQL expression guard misses DuckDB replacement scans via double-quoted identifiers and comma-joined string literals, so CUSTOM_SQL / ADD_COLUMN can read any local file
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/pipeline` (3 lenses + adversarial verify), 2026-10-02. The verifier confirmed it from the code; nothing was run end to end unless stated.
- **Severity:** high
- **Root cause:** `shared/sql_expression_guard.py` ~77: `_REPLACEMENT_SCAN_PATTERN = r"\b(from|join)\s*\(*\s*'"` only rejects a single-quoted literal placed immediately after FROM/JOIN. Two forms pass: (a) `FROM "path/file.csv"` -- `_strip_quoted` (line 47-53) blanks double-quoted text before the keyword checks, and DuckDB runs a replacement scan on any table name it cannot find in the catalog, quoted or not; (b) `FROM {{prev}}, 'path/file.csv'` -- the literal follows a comma, not FROM/JOIN. pipeline/engine.py:757-759 (CUSTOM_SQL) and :571-572 (ADD_COLUMN) splice the expression into SQL that runs at engine.py:153 on a `new_connection()` (engine.py:107) that never gets `lock_down_connection`. BUG-114 deliberately left the double-quoted form unblocked as 'a normal identifier'; BUG-189 covers only `source.query`. Read from code, not executed. Failure scenario: A tenant POSTs /pipeline/execute with a CUSTOM_SQL step `SELECT * FROM "../uploads/<other-tenant-slug>/payroll.csv"` (or `SELECT b.* FROM {{prev}} a, 'data/uploads/<other>/payroll.csv' b`). `validate_sql_expression` raises nothing, DuckDB opens the file, and the rows come back in `run.preview_data` -- a cross-tenant read of any CSV/Parquet/JSON the server process can reach.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** PR #617. `PipelineEngine.execute` calls `lock_down_connection(conn)` once the sources are loaded and before any step SQL runs, so step SQL has no filesystem or network access whatever the expression guard misses; the file sink exports through a separate connection. Regression: `tests/test_pipeline_step_sql_locked_down.py`.

## BUG-277: Streaming FileWatcherSource reads any server directory named in caller-supplied `watch_dir`/`pattern`
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/pipeline` (3 lenses + adversarial verify), 2026-10-02. The verifier confirmed it from the code; nothing was run end to end unless stated.
- **Severity:** high
- **Root cause:** `pipeline/streaming/sources/file_watcher.py` ~35: `self.watch_dir = config.get("watch_dir", "data/uploads")` and `self.pattern = config.get("pattern", "*.csv")` come straight from `StreamSource.config` in the POST /streaming/pipelines body (streaming_api.py:157-179, streaming_engine.py:65-66). `_scan_dir` (line 76-79) does `Path(self.watch_dir).glob(self.pattern)` with no tenant scoping, no `safe_object_name`, no containment check; `_parse_csv/_parse_json/_parse_parquet` (line 138-189) then turn every row into events. The default `data/uploads` is the parent of every tenant's upload directory. BUG-080 scoped pipeline ownership only, not the paths inside the config. Failure scenario: A tenant creates a streaming pipeline with source `{type: file_watcher, config: {watch_dir: "data/uploads", pattern: "**/*.csv"}}` (or an absolute path) and starts it. `start()` marks existing files as seen, so every file another tenant uploads afterwards is parsed and its rows flow through the window aggregations to the attacker's SSE stream at /streaming/pipelines/{id}/stream, or to a webhook sink they control.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** PR #618. The streaming engine builds the file watcher from a rewritten config (`pipeline/streaming/path_confinement.py`): `watch_dir` is always the pipeline tenant's own upload directory, whatever the request says, and the pattern must match file names only (no separators, `..` or `**`). Regression: `tests/test_streaming_path_confinement.py`.

## BUG-278: Streaming FileSink creates and writes into any directory named in caller-supplied `output_dir`
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/pipeline` (3 lenses + adversarial verify), 2026-10-02. The verifier confirmed it from the code; nothing was run end to end unless stated.
- **Severity:** high
- **Root cause:** `pipeline/streaming/sinks/file_sink.py` ~26: `self._output_dir = config.get("output_dir", "data/streaming_output")` is taken from the request's sink config. `start()` runs `os.makedirs(self._output_dir, exist_ok=True)` (line 36) and `_flush` opens `os.path.join(self._output_dir, f"batch_{stamp}.csv|json")` for writing (line 78, 87). No tenant namespace and no containment check, unlike the batch engine's `_write_file_sink`, which writes under `OUTPUT_DIR/tenant_slug(tenant)` (engine.py:805). The default directory is also shared by all tenants. Failure scenario: A tenant creates a streaming pipeline with a sink `{type: file, config: {output_dir: "/app/aurabackend/data/uploads/<victim-slug>", format: "csv", flush_every: 1}}`. Each window writes `batch_<ts>.csv` into the victim's upload directory, where it is listed as one of the victim's datasets and loaded into their schema context. Pointing `output_dir` at any other writable path (checkpoint dir, a mounted volume) creates directories and fills the disk there.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** PR #618. The file sink's `output_dir` is always `data/streaming_output/<tenant>/<pipeline>/` (`confine_file_sink` in `pipeline/streaming/path_confinement.py`); the request's value is ignored. Regression: `tests/test_streaming_path_confinement.py`.

## BUG-279: DuckDB sink writes into the per-run in-memory connection that is closed immediately, yet the run reports SUCCESS with output_table set
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/pipeline` (3 lenses + adversarial verify), 2026-10-02. The verifier confirmed it from the code; nothing was run end to end unless stated.
- **Severity:** high
- **Root cause:** `pipeline/engine.py` ~893: _write_duckdb_sink (engine.py:893-901) runs `CREATE TABLE {table} AS SELECT * FROM {final_table}` on `conn`, the same connection execute() opened with new_connection() at engine.py:107 (described as a 'fresh empty :memory: connection' in the docstring at engine.py:386-390). execute() then sets run.status = SUCCESS (engine.py:180) and the finally block calls conn.close() (engine.py:190). sink.connection is never read for a DuckDB sink, so there is no persistent target at all. Not found in BUG_REGISTRY.md. Failure scenario: A user runs a pipeline with sink {type: 'duckdb', table: 'clean_orders'}. The response is status=success, rows_written=N, output_table='clean_orders'. The table existed only in the in-memory database that was destroyed when the connection closed, so nothing was persisted and no later query can find it. A lost write is reported as a completed one.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** The DuckDB sink writes the table into a per-tenant database file, `data/processed/<tenant>/pipeline_tables.duckdb`, through its own connection, and sets `run.output_file` so `GET /pipeline/download/{filename}` serves it. `if_exists` is honoured: `replace` overwrites, `append` inserts by column name, `fail` fails the run and leaves the existing table alone (it used to be ignored). Regression: `tests/test_pipeline_duckdb_sink_persists.py` (4 tests, all fail on the old sink). Residual: no in-app query surface reads these tables yet; the file is retrievable by download only.

## BUG-280: PostgreSQL/MySQL/Kafka sources are loaded as all-VARCHAR, so filter, sort and MIN/MAX steps silently compare lexicographically
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/pipeline` (3 lenses + adversarial verify), 2026-10-02. The verifier confirmed it from the code; nothing was run end to end unless stated.
- **Severity:** high
- **Root cause:** `pipeline/engine.py` ~304: _load_db_source declares every column VARCHAR (engine.py:304) and stringifies every value with str(v) (engine.py:311); _load_kafka_source does the same (engine.py:349, 366). FILTER always emits a quoted string literal, `"col" {op} '{val}'` (engine.py:528-529), SORT emits `ORDER BY "col"` (engine.py:538), and AGGREGATE allows MIN/MAX on the same column (engine.py:675-679). No step restores the source type. Not found in BUG_REGISTRY.md (BUG-192 covers only truncation and swallowed query errors in this loader). Failure scenario: A Postgres source has integer column amount. Step filter {column: amount, operator: '>', value: '100'} evaluates VARCHAR > VARCHAR: '95' > '100' is true and '1000' > '200' is false. The run returns SUCCESS with the wrong rows. Sort orders 10 before 9, MAX(amount) returns '99' instead of '1000', and SUM/AVG fail outright on VARCHAR. The same pipeline on a CSV/Parquet source gives correct results.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** `_load_db_source` and `_load_kafka_source` load rows through `_typed_arrow_table`, which keeps each column's type (integers, decimals, floats, booleans, dates, timestamps); a column Arrow cannot give one type to (mixed types, UUIDs, nested JSON) is loaded as text, as every column was before. Regression: `tests/test_pipeline_external_source_types.py` (5 tests, all fail on the old loader; connector and Kafka consumer are fakes, not run against a real Postgres/Kafka).

## BUG-281: Invalid or misconfigured steps are silently rewritten or skipped and the run still reports SUCCESS
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/pipeline` (3 lenses + adversarial verify), 2026-10-02. The verifier confirmed it from the code; nothing was run end to end unless stated.
- **Severity:** high
- **Root cause:** `pipeline/engine.py` ~524: FILTER: an operator outside allowed_ops is replaced with '=' (engine.py:524-525). CAST_TYPE: a type outside the 9-name allowlist returns None (engine.py:580-581). JOIN: missing join_source or keys returns None (engine.py:695-697). AGGREGATE: a disallowed function is dropped with `continue` (engine.py:676-677). Every None is only counted as skipped (engine.py:492-494) and execute() sets SUCCESS (engine.py:180). BUG-010 item 1 fixed only the unimplemented-step-type fall-through (engine.py:766), not these branches. Failure scenario: (a) A filter with operator '<>' or 'BETWEEN' (LLM- or API-supplied) is executed as `col = 'value'`, the opposite of '<>', and the run returns SUCCESS with the wrong rows. (b) A JOIN step with config.right_table but no join_source is dropped; the output is the unjoined left table, reported SUCCESS. (c) cast_type to DECIMAL or INT is skipped, so later steps run on the uncast column. (d) An aggregate with function COUNT_DISTINCT loses that measure while the others succeed. The only signal is steps_skipped, which the async SSE path does not turn into an error.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** `_step_to_sql` raises `ValueError("Pipeline step '<type>' is misconfigured: <reason>")` for a step missing a required setting or naming an operator, direction, type, function or join type outside its allowlist, so the run fails with the reason instead of skipping the step (or running a filter as `=`). `<>` is accepted as a filter operator. The only remaining skip is a fill-all with no NULLs to fill. Regression: `tests/test_pipeline_invalid_steps_fail.py` (11 tests; 10 fail on the old engine).

## BUG-282: No bounds on streaming pipeline config: `num_keys` allocates an arbitrary list on the event loop, and pipeline count and buffers are uncapped
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/pipeline` (3 lenses + adversarial verify), 2026-10-02. The verifier confirmed it from the code; nothing was run end to end unless stated.
- **Severity:** high
- **Root cause:** `pipeline/streaming/sources/simulated.py` ~46: simulated.py:44-46 runs `self.num_keys = config.get('num_keys', 5)` then `self._keys = [f"key_{i}" for i in range(self.num_keys)]` with no upper bound. This executes synchronously inside `engine.start()` (streaming_engine.py:263) on the single uvicorn worker's event loop. `StreamSource.config` is an unvalidated `Dict[str, Any]` (streaming/models.py:182). `RuntimeConfig.backpressure_buffer` (models.py:144) and the websocket `max_buffer` (websocket_source.py:70-72) are likewise unbounded ints. The process-global `_pipelines`/`_engines` dicts (streaming_api.py:49-50, 179, 242) have no per-tenant cap on created or running pipelines, and each running engine holds two permanent tasks (streaming_engine.py:328-329). Failure scenario: An authenticated user POSTs a simulated-source pipeline with `num_keys: 2000000000` and calls /start. The list comprehension builds billions of strings on the event loop, so every tenant's requests freeze and the gateway is then OOM-killed. Separately, a script that creates and starts thousands of pipelines accumulates engines, tasks, buffers of up to `backpressure_buffer` events each, and a checkpoint directory per pipeline, with no limit.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** Simulated source `num_keys` is clamped to 1..10,000 and `events_per_second` to 0.1..10,000; websocket `max_buffer` to 1..100,000; `RuntimeConfig.backpressure_buffer` is validated to 1..1,000,000. A tenant may hold at most 50 streaming pipelines (`MAX_PIPELINES_PER_TENANT`) and run at most 10 at once (`MAX_RUNNING_PER_TENANT`); create and start return 409 past the limit, counting pipelines mid-start. Regression: `tests/test_streaming_config_bounds.py` (9 tests; 7 fail on the old code, the 2-billion-key case was not run against it). Residual: no global cap across tenants.

## BUG-283: Streaming DatabaseSink opens any DuckDB file path from caller config (`path`) and writes a table into it
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/pipeline` (3 lenses + adversarial verify), 2026-10-02. The verifier confirmed it from the code; nothing was run end to end unless stated.
- **Severity:** medium
- **Root cause:** `pipeline/streaming/sinks/database_sink.py` ~64: `db_path = self.config.get("path", ":memory:")` then `duckdb.connect(db_path)` (line 67) and `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS ...` (line 68-79), with later upserts at line 131-147. The path is request input from the POST /streaming/pipelines sink config. It is not passed through `safe_object_name` or the storage backend, although the batch engine's DuckDB source does exactly that for the same kind of input (engine.py:411-415). BUG-079 fixed only the `table` identifier; BUG-232 covers the connector routes, not this sink. Failure scenario: A tenant sets the sink to `{type: database, config: {connector: "duckdb", path: "data/uploads/<victim-slug>/warehouse.duckdb", table: "orders"}}` and starts the pipeline. The server opens the victim's uploaded database read-write and creates or inserts into a table in it (taking the file lock, so the victim's own reads fail while the pipeline runs). With a new path it creates a .duckdb file anywhere the process can write.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** PR #618. A DuckDB sink path keeps only its file name and is placed under `data/streaming_output/<tenant>/<pipeline>/` (`confine_database_sink`); `:memory:` is untouched. Regression: `tests/test_streaming_path_confinement.py`.

## BUG-284: Raw exception text is returned to the client through run.error on both the sync and SSE paths, bypassing sanitize_error
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/pipeline` (3 lenses + adversarial verify), 2026-10-02. The verifier confirmed it from the code; nothing was run end to end unless stated.
- **Severity:** medium
- **Root cause:** `pipeline/engine.py` ~185: execute() catches every exception and stores `run.error = str(exc)` (engine.py:185) instead of raising. pipelines.py:158 then returns `{"status": "success", "run": run.model_dump()}`, so the sanitize_error branch at pipelines.py:159-160 is unreachable for engine failures. The async path publishes `run.error or "Pipeline failed"` over SSE (pipelines.py:254-257), contradicting the 'don't echo raw exception text' comment at pipelines.py:265-267. The registry has no entry for this path (BUG-240 covers Commander chat, BUG-221 JWT decoding). Failure scenario: A pipeline with a Postgres source/sink pointing at an unreachable or misconfigured host, or a bad column name, fails. The client receives the raw asyncpg/DuckDB message in run.error: internal hostnames and ports, DuckDB binder errors listing candidate table and column names, and absolute server paths from the COPY at engine.py:810. On the sync endpoint the envelope also says status 'success', so a client that checks only the top-level status treats the failed run as successful.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** `run.error` is built by `_client_error` in `pipeline/engine.py`: the engine's own messages (plain `ValueError`, `FileNotFoundError`, `ConnectionError`) pass through; a DuckDB error is kept, since the caller needs it to fix their own SQL, with server paths replaced by `<path>` and capped at 1000 characters; anything else (driver errors, `ValueError` subclasses from libraries) is logged in full and reported as a generic message. Regression: `tests/test_pipeline_run_error_sanitized.py` (6 tests; 4 fail on the old behaviour). Residual: the sync endpoint's envelope still says `status: "success"` for a failed run -- the run's own `status` carries the failure and the frontend reads that.

## BUG-285: PostgreSQL sink with if_exists='fail' silently appends into an existing table
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/pipeline` (3 lenses + adversarial verify), 2026-10-02. The verifier confirmed it from the code; nothing was run end to end unless stated.
- **Severity:** medium
- **Root cause:** `pipeline/engine.py` ~876: For if_exists in ('replace', 'fail') the code runs `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` (engine.py:876-879) and then unconditionally `INSERT INTO ... VALUES` (engine.py:882-885). There is no existence check for 'fail' (models.py:129 documents the options as 'replace, append, fail'). _write_duckdb_sink gets 'fail' right only because its plain CREATE TABLE raises (engine.py:899). Not in BUG_REGISTRY.md (BUG-191 covers only the missing transaction). Failure scenario: A user sets if_exists='fail' to protect an existing table 'orders'. The table exists, so CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS is a no-op, the rows are inserted and the transaction commits. The run reports SUCCESS and the protected table now holds duplicated rows. If the column counts differ the insert fails, but when they match the corruption is silent.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** `_write_pg_sink` uses a plain `CREATE TABLE` for `replace` (after its DROP) and `fail`, so `fail` raises "already exists" inside the transaction before any row is written; `append` uses `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` (it used to fail outright on a missing table); any other `if_exists` value is rejected. Regression: three Tier B tests in `tests/test_pipeline_pg_sink_transaction.py`, run by CI's Postgres lane (no local Postgres on the dev machine).

## BUG-286: Step column references go through _sanitize_id, but file sources keep their original column names, so any column with a space or punctuation fails or binds to a different column
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/pipeline` (3 lenses + adversarial verify), 2026-10-02. The verifier confirmed it from the code; nothing was run end to end unless stated.
- **Severity:** medium
- **Root cause:** `pipeline/engine.py` ~527: _sanitize_id replaces every non [A-Za-z0-9_] character with '_' (engine.py:41-46) and is applied to column references in FILTER (527-529), SORT (538), DROP_COLUMNS (544), RENAME (554, 559), CAST (582), FILL_MISSING (650-655), DEDUPLICATE (660), AGGREGATE (669, 678), JOIN keys (704), WINDOW (719, 722) and PIVOT/UNPIVOT (736-737, 744). File sources are loaded by smart_load_file, whose Parquet/JSON branch is a plain `CREATE TABLE AS SELECT * FROM read_parquet(...)` that keeps the original names (shared/data_utils.py:433-436). The FILL_MISSING '*' branch uses _q() on the real names (engine.py:610-643), which shows the inconsistency. Not in BUG_REGISTRY.md. Failure scenario: A Parquet file has a column "Order Date" or "unit-price". The schema endpoint shows that name, and a sort/filter/aggregate step on it generates `"Order_Date"`, which fails with a binder error, so the column cannot be used in any step. If the table has both "unit-price" and "unit_price", the step silently operates on the wrong column and reports SUCCESS.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** `_step_to_sql` quotes a referenced column exactly as named, with the shared quoter (`_q`), in every step type; `_sanitize_id` is now used only for names the engine invents (aliases, `_cast` / `_filled` columns). PostgreSQL/MySQL/Kafka sources keep their original column names, like file sources. Regression: `tests/test_pipeline_real_column_names.py` (5 tests; 4 fail on the old engine, including the case where `unit-price` silently bound to `unit_price`). Behaviour change: a step that named a column by its sanitized form (`Order_Date` for `Order Date`) no longer matches.

## BUG-287: Streaming webhook sink and websocket source connect to any caller-supplied URL with no SSRF filter
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/pipeline` (3 lenses + adversarial verify), 2026-10-02. The verifier confirmed it from the code; nothing was run end to end unless stated.
- **Severity:** medium
- **Root cause:** `pipeline/streaming/sinks/webhook_sink.py` ~100: webhook_sink.py:37 takes `self._url = config["url"]` and line 100 calls `self._client.post(self._url, content=body, headers=headers)` with caller-controlled `headers` (line 39) and an uncapped caller-controlled `retries` (line 41). websocket_source.py:63 and 133 do `websockets.connect(self._url, ...)` and turn inbound messages into events that flow to the caller's SSE stream. The only SSRF guard in the backend, `_is_ssrf_safe_url`, is defined and used solely in api_gateway/routers/webhooks.py:76/124/170 (the BUG-054 fix); nothing under pipeline/streaming calls it. Failure scenario: A tenant creates a pipeline with a webhook sink at `http://169.254.169.254/...` or `http://localhost:8009/uasr/...` plus custom headers, and the gateway issues POSTs to internal-only services on every closed window. With a websocket source pointed at an internal `ws://` endpoint, the internal service's messages are aggregated and returned to the tenant over SSE, giving a read channel into the internal network. This is the BUG-054 exposure reopened through the streaming path.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** The SSRF check moved to `shared/ssrf.py` (`is_public_url`, same rules as BUG-054; `webhooks.py` delegates to it). `WebhookSink.start()` and `WebSocketSource.start()` raise if the URL is not a public http(s) / ws(s) address, and both re-check before every delivery / connection so a name re-pointed after start is not followed. Webhook sink `retries` is capped at 5. Regression: `tests/test_streaming_ssrf_guard.py` (18 tests; the 11 refusal cases fail on the old code). Residual: the check and the connection resolve the name separately, so a resolver that answers differently within that window is not covered (same limit as BUG-054).

## BUG-288: Batch pipeline run has no memory, row, step or concurrency bound; the Postgres sink fetches the whole result into Python
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/pipeline` (3 lenses + adversarial verify), 2026-10-02. The verifier confirmed it from the code; nothing was run end to end unless stated.
- **Severity:** medium
- **Root cause:** `pipeline/engine.py` ~847: `_MAX_SOURCE_ROWS` (engine.py:59) is enforced only in `_load_db_source` (295). FILE sources (256) and DUCKDB sources (437-445) load with no row cap. The transform chain is materialised as `CREATE TABLE pipeline_output AS ...` (507, executed at 153) on a connection from `new_connection()`, which sets no `memory_limit`, thread limit or timeout (shared/duckdb_factory.py:16-19). The LIMIT step takes any `int(n)` (749), `Pipeline.steps` has no max length (models.py:142), and each JOIN step loads another full source (132-138). `_write_pg_sink` then runs `SELECT *` and `result.fetchall()` on the whole output (847-849) and passes all rows to one `executemany` (885). /pipeline/execute/async starts each run with `fire_and_forget` and no per-tenant or global concurrency limit (pipelines.py:274-275), and `execute()` has no overall timeout. Failure scenario: A tenant uploads a multi-GB parquet file and submits a pipeline with a JOIN step back onto the same file on a low-cardinality key, or a `custom_sql` step that self-joins `{{prev}}`, with a postgresql sink. DuckDB materialises the blown-up output in-process, then `fetchall()` copies every row into Python tuples and the single gateway worker is OOM-killed for all tenants. Posting the same pipeline to /pipeline/execute/async 50 times runs 50 such jobs at once, because nothing queues or rejects them.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** `PipelineEngine.execute` now bounds a run: at most 50 steps; the transform is stopped with `conn.interrupt()` after 300 s; the run connection has `memory_limit=512MB` (DuckDB spills to disk past it instead of taking 80% of RAM); at most 3 concurrent runs per tenant across the sync, async and scheduled paths; and the PostgreSQL sink reads and inserts in 10,000-row batches instead of `fetchall()`. Regression: `tests/test_pipeline_run_bounds.py` (5 tests; they depend on the new constants, so they were not run against the old engine). Residual: FILE and DUCKDB sources still have no row cap, and source loading and the sink write are not covered by the timeout.

## BUG-289: Pipeline output files and streaming checkpoint directories are never deleted
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/pipeline` (3 lenses + adversarial verify), 2026-10-02. The verifier confirmed it from the code; nothing was run end to end unless stated.
- **Severity:** low
- **Root cause:** `pipeline/engine.py` ~794: `_write_file_sink` (engine.py:793-816) writes `data/processed/<tenant_slug>/<stem><ext>` on every non-preview run, defaulting to a fresh `pipeline_output_{run.run_id}` name (794). pipelines.py has only a download route (340-368); there is no delete route, retention job or per-tenant quota, and `DELETE /pipeline/{id}` (320-326) removes only the DB row. On the streaming side, `StateManager.__init__` creates `data/checkpoints/<pipeline_id>/` (state_manager.py:56-61) and writes checkpoints every interval. `clear_checkpoints()` (147) has no caller anywhere in aurabackend, and `delete_pipeline` (streaming_api.py:196-218) only pops the in-memory dicts. Failure scenario: A tenant, or a schedule or inbound hook, runs a file-sink pipeline repeatedly with no `file_name`. Each run leaves a new full-size CSV or Parquet file, and the shared volume fills until uploads, SQLite writes and checkpoints fail for every tenant. Likewise, each streaming pipeline that is created, started and deleted leaves a directory of up to 5 checkpoint files behind permanently, since pipeline ids are random and never reused.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** `_write_file_sink` keeps only the newest 50 auto-named outputs (`pipeline_output_run_*`) per tenant (`MAX_AUTO_NAMED_OUTPUTS`); files the caller named are never pruned. `DELETE /streaming/pipelines/{id}` removes the pipeline's checkpoint directory (`remove_pipeline_checkpoints`). Regression: `tests/test_pipeline_output_retention.py` (4 tests; the two retention cases fail on the old code). Residual: no size quota per tenant, no delete route for a named output, and a streaming pipeline's own output directory is kept on delete.

## BUG-290: Audit-your-own-data resolves uploads from flat, tenant-less directories, while uploads are stored per tenant
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/counterfactual_service` (3 lenses + adversarial verify), 2026-10-02. The verifier confirmed it from the code; nothing was run end to end unless stated.
- **Severity:** high
- **Root cause:** `counterfactual_service/main.py` ~415: `_find_upload` (main.py:412-425) and `_resolve_dataset` (main.py:172-204) look for the bare filename directly under `data/uploads`, `api_gateway/uploads` and `uploads`. Neither takes a tenant. The upload route (`api_gateway/routers/files.py:178`) writes through `get_storage_backend().write(tenant, safe_name, data)`, and `shared/storage/local.py:27-28` puts that at `<root>/<tenant_slug>/<name>` (or in S3). `_SAFE_UPLOAD_NAME` forbids `/`, so the tenant sub-directory can never be addressed. `run_audit` derives the tenant from the token (main.py:527) but only uses it for the job and ledger, never for the file lookup. The same applies to `POST /counterfactual/jobs` with `dataset.source_id = "uploaded_file:<name>"`. Failure scenario: Tenant A uploads `loans.csv` via `POST /upload`; it is stored at `data/uploads/<A>/loans.csv`. The audit wizard then posts `/api/v1/counterfactual/audit` with `uploaded_file: "loans.csv"`, and `_find_upload` checks `data/uploads/loans.csv`, finds nothing and returns 404 `uploaded file not found`. This happens for every tenant's own upload. In the other direction, any file that does sit in one of the flat roots (a legacy pre-tenant upload, or anything in `api_gateway/uploads` or `uploads`) has no owner check: any authenticated tenant can name it and get a signed audit of it, and the 400 `columns not in file ...: [missing]` response (main.py:522) lets them probe its column names. In this checkout `aurabackend/data/uploads` contains only the `default` tenant directory, so the 404 path is the live one. Read from the code; not run. Verifier's note: The claimed root cause is accurate. One addition: `shared/upload_migration.py` deliberately moves flat files in `data/uploads/` into `data/uploads/default/`, so the flat roots that `_find_upload` and `_resolve_dataset` search are empty by design. The live defect is therefore the 404 on every tenant-uploaded file (including the `default` tenant); the unowned cross-tenant read only applies if a file is placed in a flat root some other way.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** The audit reads uploads through the storage backend for the caller's tenant (`_upload_exists` / `_read_upload` in `counterfactual_service/main.py`); the flat `data/uploads`, `api_gateway/uploads`, `uploads` lookups relative to the working directory are gone. `run_audit` takes the tenant from the verified token before the lookup, the worker reads `payload["tenant_id"]`'s file, and `/counterfactual/jobs` resolves `uploaded_file:` sources for the job's tenant. Works for the S3 backend too (reads bytes, no local path). Regression: 4 new tests in `tests/test_audit_endpoint.py` (3 fail on the old code); the existing fixtures there and in `test_audit_encode.py` wrote a flat layout the upload route never produces and now write per-tenant.

## BUG-291: Signed AS-1215 preparer identity is taken from the request body, so a caller can attribute an audit to anyone
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/counterfactual_service` (3 lenses + adversarial verify), 2026-10-02. The verifier confirmed it from the code; nothing was run end to end unless stated.
- **Severity:** medium
- **Root cause:** `counterfactual_service/main.py` ~751: `financial_audit` passes `preparer_id=req.preparer_id` (also `subject_id`, `subject_type`) straight from `FinancialAuditRequest` into `build_completion_document` (main.py:749-751) and into the ledger append (main.py:766). `financial_report.py:73` places it at `assignment.preparer_id` inside the signed bytes, documented as the AS-1215 accountable preparer. `/counterfactual/audit` does the same: `AuditRequest.preparer_id` (main.py:399) reaches the ledger at main.py:460. The tenant is correctly overwritten from the verified token (main.py:738, 529), and the reviewer on the decision path is bound to `user["sub"]` (main.py:900, with the comment at 851-853 explaining why), but the preparer is never bound to the token. Failure scenario: User `alice` (role `user`, org X) posts `/api/v1/counterfactual/audit/financial` with `preparer_id: "bob.cpa@firm.com"`. The service returns an ED25519-signed EngagementCompletionDocument whose `assignment.preparer_id` is Bob, and writes a ledger row with `preparer_id` Bob. `/audit/ledger/subject/{id}` and the later `human_review` row (which inherits `original.preparer_id`, main.py:485) then show a cryptographically attested preparer-to-reviewer trail for a person who never ran the audit. If the field is omitted, the record says `system` rather than the real caller. Verifier's note: `preparer_id` is a free client string on both `FinancialAuditRequest` (main.py:719) and `AuditRequest` (main.py:399). It flows unvalidated into the signed completion document's `assignment.preparer_id` (main.py:751, financial_report.py:73) and into the ledger rows (main.py:766, 460), and is inherited by `human_review` rows (main.py:485). It is never bound to the verified token's `sub`, unlike the tenant (`_ledger_tenant`) and the reviewer (main.py:900). The Subsystem C spec specified it as "threaded from the request", so the flaw originates in the design. `/audit/financial` also accepts anonymous callers through the optional `get_current_user` (main.py:724), so on that path there is no verified identity to bind at all.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** `_preparer(user, requested)` in `counterfactual_service/main.py`: an authenticated caller is the preparer (the token's `sub`, the same binding the reviewer has), used for the signed completion document and the ledger row on both `/audit/financial` and `/counterfactual/audit`. Only a request with no identity (dev/open mode) keeps the body's value. Regression: `tests/test_audit_preparer_identity.py` (4 tests; the two endpoint tests fail on the old code). `subject_id` / `subject_type` remain caller-supplied -- they describe what was audited, not who did it.

## BUG-292: Raw estimator and refuter exception text is returned to callers and sealed into the signed artifact
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/counterfactual_service` (3 lenses + adversarial verify), 2026-10-02. The verifier confirmed it from the code; nothing was run end to end unless stated.
- **Severity:** low
- **Root cause:** `counterfactual_service/engine.py` ~562: The per-estimator and per-refuter `except Exception` blocks store `error=f"{type(exc).__name__}: {exc}"` on the result model (engine.py:562, 760, 933, 1105, 1336, 1404; line 1053 uses `f"IV (2SLS) failed: {exc}"`). Those fields are part of `artifact.model_dump()`, which `GET /counterfactual/jobs/{id}` and `/artifacts/{hash}` return and `persistence.write_artifact` stores. This bypasses `sanitize_error`, which the job-level handlers in main.py:226-231 use, and contradicts the bulk-replay path's own rule of surfacing only the exception class name (main.py:977-979). Failure scenario: A job is submitted on data that makes econml, dowhy or numpy raise inside an estimator or refuter (a singular design matrix, a non-numeric column, a library import or file error). The job still reports `succeeded`, and `artifact.estimates[i].error` carries the unsanitised library message. That message can include server-side module or file paths and internal detail. It is shown to the client and stored permanently in the content-addressed artifact, which is served to any caller holding the hash because engine artifacts carry no tenant stamp.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** Every estimator and refuter `except` block in `counterfactual_service/engine.py` builds its `error` text through `_estimator_error`: the exception class plus the first line of its message, with server paths replaced by `<path>` and a 200-character cap. A numeric failure ("Singular matrix") stays readable; multi-line library output and paths do not reach the caller or the signed artifact. Regression: `tests/test_counterfactual_estimator_error_text.py` (6 tests; they call the new helper, so they were not run against the old code). Residual: the first line of a library message is still shown, so a message that names a column or value on its first line is passed through.

## BUG-293: double_ml/tmle/forest_dr estimate the opposite contrast from the DoWhy estimators when treatment.actual is the 0-coded value; the headline averages them
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/counterfactual_service` (3 lenses + adversarial verify), 2026-10-02. The verifier confirmed it from the code; nothing was run end to end unless stated.
- **Severity:** high
- **Root cause:** `counterfactual_service/engine.py` ~418: The DR-class estimators binarise the treatment as `T_bin = (df[treatment.column] == treatment.actual)` (engine.py:418, 620, 878), so their effect is 'rows equal to actual' minus 'all other rows'. The DoWhy estimators (linear_regression, ipw, psm; engine.py:1062-1072) and IV use the raw column with DoWhy's default control=0 / treatment=1, and `treatment.counterfactual` is never read anywhere in the engine. The two families only agree when actual == 1 on a 0/1 column. Nothing validates this; `renderers._operator` (renderers.py:144) and `verdict.significance_verdict` (verdict.py:61) average all points together. Failure scenario: POST /counterfactual/jobs (default methods linear_regression, ipw, psm, double_ml; econml installed) with a 0/1 treatment column and InterventionSpec(actual=0, counterfactual=1), true ATE +0.30. The three DoWhy estimators return about +0.30 and double_ml returns about -0.30 with no error. The headline average is +0.15 (half the true effect), and with methods=[linear_regression, double_ml] it is about 0, so the verdict reads 'No material disparate impact detected'. If every CI excludes zero the verdict is 'detected' with the wrong magnitude. For a non-binary treatment (e.g. price, actual=10) double_ml silently estimates 'price==10 vs everything else' and is averaged with a per-unit slope.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** PR #630. Every estimator measures the outcome at `treatment.actual` minus the outcome at `treatment.counterfactual`: the DR-class estimators run on the rows at those two values only (`_contrast_rows`), and DoWhy/IV per-unit effects are scaled by `actual - counterfactual` (`_per_unit_to_contrast`). Unchanged for `actual=1, counterfactual=0` on a 0/1 column. Regression: `tests/test_counterfactual_contrast_direction.py` (7 tests, 3 fail on the old engine). Residual: `actual == counterfactual` is not rejected.

## BUG-294: A failed adversarial critic is cached as an empty challenge list and reported as 'the critic had no objections'
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/counterfactual_service` (3 lenses + adversarial verify), 2026-10-02. The verifier confirmed it from the code; nothing was run end to end unless stated.
- **Severity:** high
- **Root cause:** `counterfactual_service/engine.py` ~1691: `raw = res.output.get('challenges', []) if res.succeeded else []` turns a FAILED critic run into `[]`, then `critic_cache.put(cache_k, raw)` (line 1697) persists it. AdversarialCriticAgent returns status FAILED without raising on an LLM error, non-JSON output, agent timeout or exhausted budget, so the `critic_timeout` except branch in run_job (lines 1830-1837) never fires and no warning is added. The empty result is indistinguishable from a critic that ran and found nothing, and every later run with the same request_hash hits the poisoned cache (line 1674-1676). Failure scenario: The LLM provider returns a 429 or a non-JSON body during an audit. The artifact is sealed and signed with challenges=[] and warnings=[]. score_confidence applies no high-severity penalty, so refuters passing plus overlapping CIs gives raw 0.9 = 'high'. The PDF prints 'No challenges raised — the critic had no objections.' (pdf_renderer.py:244-246). Re-running the same query after the provider recovers still returns no challenges, because the empty list is served from the cache. Verifier's note: In `_run_critic` (aurabackend/counterfactual_service/engine.py:1690-1701) a FAILED AgentResult is collapsed to `[]` and written unconditionally to the critic cache, then returned as `([], True)`. AdversarialCriticAgent and BaseAgent.execute report LLM errors, non-JSON output, agent timeout and budget exhaustion as status FAILED without raising. As a result run_job's "critic skipped" warning branch (1830-1837) never fires, and the path with `critic_timeout=None` has no failure handling at all. The cached `{"challenges": []}` is a valid hit at 1674-1676, so the failure is replayed permanently for that request_hash and model. The fix is to raise, or return a failure signal, when `not res.succeeded`, to skip the cache write in that case, and to surface a warning in the artifact.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** PR #631. `_run_critic` raises `CriticUnavailable` on a FAILED agent result and writes nothing to the cache; `run_job` records a warning starting "Adversarial LLM critic skipped" on both the timeout and no-timeout paths; the PDF and the operator card (`critic_skipped`) say the critic did not run. Regression: 4 tests in `tests/test_critic_timeout.py` (3 fail on the old engine), 1 in `CounterfactualCard.test.tsx`. Residual: `score_confidence` does not lower the badge when the critic was skipped.

## BUG-295: sensitivity_headline reports the largest point E-value across estimators and ignores a CI that crosses zero
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/counterfactual_service` (3 lenses + adversarial verify), 2026-10-02. The verifier confirmed it from the code; nothing was run end to end unless stated.
- **Severity:** medium
- **Root cause:** `counterfactual_service/audit_mapping.py` ~256: `e = max(evals) # most conservative (largest) E-value across methods` is backwards: a larger E-value means a more robust claim, so max() picks the least conservative estimator. The list is built from `e_value_point` only (line 252); `e_value_ci` and `null_crossed`, which sensitivity.py sets to 1.0 / True when the interval includes zero, are never consulted. Failure scenario: /counterfactual/audit with an instrument, outcome SD 0.5: double_ml point 0.03 (E-value about 1.30), iv point 0.40 with CI [-0.1, 0.9] (point E-value about 3.56, e_value_ci 1.0, null_crossed True). The result's headline reads 'an unmeasured confounder would need an E-value of about 3.56 ... to fully explain away this effect', although the more precise estimator needs only 1.30 and the 3.56 estimate is not distinguishable from zero.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** `sensitivity_headline` quotes the smallest point E-value across the estimators (the weakest claim) and, when any estimator's interval includes zero (`null_crossed`, or `e_value_ci <= 1`), says so and names it instead of quoting a robustness figure. Regression: 3 new tests in `tests/test_audit_mapping.py`; the existing `test_sensitivity_headline_reads_evalue` asserted the larger value and was corrected.

## BUG-296: Refuter pass threshold has an absolute floor of 0.1, so refuters cannot fail on small-scale (binary/rate) outcomes
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/counterfactual_service` (3 lenses + adversarial verify), 2026-10-02. The verifier confirmed it from the code; nothing was run end to end unless stated.
- **Severity:** medium
- **Root cause:** `counterfactual_service/engine.py` ~1265: `threshold = max(abs(baseline) * 0.2, 0.1)` is scale-blind. For any baseline with |effect| < 0.5 the tolerance is a fixed 0.1 in outcome units, while the verdict's own materiality cut-off is 0.02 (verdict.py:15). On a 0/1 outcome, a placebo passes whenever |placebo effect| < 0.1 and the other refuters pass whenever the refuted estimate moves by less than 0.1, even if that is several times the effect or flips its sign. refute_pass carries weight 0.5 in score_confidence. Failure scenario: Fair-lending audit on a binary approval outcome with baseline effect 0.05. The placebo-treatment refuter returns 0.09 (larger than the real effect) and passes because 0.09 < 0.1. The data-subset refuter returns -0.04 (sign reversed) and passes because |-0.04 - 0.05| = 0.09 < 0.1. With all four refuters passing, raw = 0.5*1.0 + 0.4*overlap = 0.9, so the signed artifact says confidence 'high' and the PDF shows 'Passed: Yes' for refutations that contradict the estimate. Verifier's note: `_refuter_passed` (aurabackend/counterfactual_service/engine.py:1255-1268) uses `max(abs(baseline) * 0.2, 0.1)` as its tolerance. The 0.1 absolute floor is in raw outcome units and ignores outcome scale, effect sign and the refuter's own p_value, so for any baseline with |effect| < 0.5 (typical for binary/rate outcomes) a refuter passes even when the placebo effect exceeds the real effect or the refuted estimate flips sign. The floor is a documented intentional heuristic, but it makes refuters nearly unfalsifiable on small-scale outcomes, and `passed` feeds `score_confidence` at weight 0.5 and the PDF "Passed" column directly.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** `_refuter_passed` keeps its tolerance (`max(0.2*|baseline|, 0.1)`) and adds two rules that hold whatever the outcome's scale: a placebo effect at least as large as the real effect fails, and a refuted estimate with the opposite sign to the baseline fails. Regression: `tests/test_counterfactual_refuter_pass_rule.py` (6 tests; the placebo and sign-flip cases fail on the old rule); the full counterfactual/audit/demo set (438 tests) is unchanged. Residual: the 0.1 floor is still in raw outcome units, so on a rate or binary outcome a same-sign drift of up to 0.1 still passes. Replacing the floor with one scaled to the outcome would change pass/fail on existing demo scenarios and needs a decision on the rule, not a patch.

## BUG-297: Critic cache key rests on a head/tail-only dataset fingerprint, so a changed dataset is served the previous run's challenges
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/counterfactual_service` (3 lenses + adversarial verify), 2026-10-02. The verifier confirmed it from the code; nothing was run end to end unless stated.
- **Severity:** medium
- **Root cause:** `counterfactual_service/engine.py` ~1640: `_dataset_fingerprint` hashes only column names, dtypes, the first 3 rows, the last 3 rows and the row count (lines 1634-1642). `_request_hash` (line 1776) is built from that fingerprint, and the critic cache key is (request_hash, model_id, model_version) only (line 1671) — it does not include the estimates or refutations the critique was written about. BUG-096 added the tenant to the key but left this same-tenant case untouched. The same weak value is signed into the artifact as `dataset_fingerprint` and written to the ledger as `input_fingerprint` (main.py:453-461). Failure scenario: A tenant audits loans.csv (10,000 rows) and the critic raises a high-severity challenge citing the estimates. They correct rows 4..9,997 (same columns, dtypes, length, first/last 3 rows) and re-run the same query. The estimators produce different numbers, but `_run_critic` returns the cached challenges from the first run with regenerated_critic=False. The new signed artifact carries challenge text and a confidence penalty that describe the old data, and both artifacts and both ledger entries show an identical dataset/input fingerprint for two different populations.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** `_dataset_fingerprint` hashes every value (`pd.util.hash_pandas_object` over the sorted columns, falling back to canonical JSON for columns holding unhashable values) instead of only the first and last three rows, so the request hash and the critic cache key change whenever the data does, and the fingerprint signed into the artifact and written to the ledger identifies the audited data. Regression: `tests/test_counterfactual_dataset_fingerprint.py` (5 tests; the two mid-frame edits fail on the old code). Consequence: every dataset's fingerprint changes once, so the critic cache misses once and a fingerprint in an already-sealed artifact will not match a recomputation.

## BUG-298: Audit ProcessPoolExecutor is cached forever, so one dead child permanently breaks POST /counterfactual/audit until restart
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/counterfactual_service` (3 lenses + adversarial verify), 2026-10-02. The verifier confirmed it from the code; nothing was run end to end unless stated.
- **Severity:** high
- **Root cause:** `counterfactual_service/audit_worker.py` ~21: get_audit_pool() lazily creates a module-global ProcessPoolExecutor (_POOL) and returns the same object forever; nothing ever checks for or replaces a broken pool. When a worker process dies abruptly (OOM kill, segfault in a native dowhy/econml/numpy routine), concurrent.futures marks the executor broken and every later submit() raises BrokenProcessPool. main.py:497 (`loop.run_in_executor(get_audit_pool(), run_audit_subprocess, payload)`) just catches that as a generic Exception and marks the job failed. Failure scenario: Tenant A uploads a large CSV and POSTs /counterfactual/audit; the child running the dowhy/econml fan-out is OOM-killed on the small box. That job fails with BrokenProcessPool (also failing the other in-flight audit sharing the pool). From then on, every audit from every tenant fails immediately with the same sanitized error, because _POOL is still the broken executor; only a gateway restart recovers. Expected: the pool is recreated and only the offending job fails. Verifier's note: The claimed root cause is accurate as stated: `get_audit_pool()` (`audit_worker.py:16-22`) caches a module-global `ProcessPoolExecutor` and never detects or replaces a broken one, and `_run_audit_job_async` (`main.py:493-505`) treats `BrokenProcessPool` as a generic per-job failure without resetting `_POOL`.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** `_run_audit_job_async` catches `BrokenExecutor` and calls `audit_worker.discard_audit_pool(pool)`, which drops that pool (only if it is still the cached one) so the next audit gets a fresh one. The job whose child died still fails. Regression: `tests/test_audit_pool_recovery.py` kills a real child with `os._exit` and runs a second audit (fails on the old code).

## BUG-299: Audit job is reported 'succeeded' before the ledger append; a poller can read a success that is later flipped to 'failed'
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/counterfactual_service` (3 lenses + adversarial verify), 2026-10-02. The verifier confirmed it from the code; nothing was run end to end unless stated.
- **Severity:** medium
- **Root cause:** `counterfactual_service/main.py` ~498: _run_audit_job_async sets `_jobs[job_id].update(state="succeeded", artifact=result)` at line 498 and only then awaits `_append_fairness_audit_to_ledger` (line 499), which is a DB append with retries. The docstring at 440-449 states a ledger-append failure must mark the job failed 'instead of reporting a false success', but the success state is already observable during the whole awaited append (including its retry backoff). Failure scenario: Client POSTs /counterfactual/audit and polls GET /counterfactual/jobs/{id}. The subprocess finishes; state becomes 'succeeded' with the signed artifact. The ledger DB is locked/unavailable, so append_audit_with_retry spends its retries and then raises. A poll landing in that window returns state='succeeded', the UI stops polling and shows a signed certificate; afterwards the job flips to 'failed'. The user holds a certificate with no ledger entry (the orphan this code says it prevents) and never sees the failure. Verifier's note: In `_run_audit_job_async` (`aurabackend/counterfactual_service/main.py:497-499`), the job is published as `state="succeeded"` with the signed artifact before `_append_fairness_audit_to_ledger` is awaited. The append retries 3 times with 0.2s/0.4s backoff and re-raises, so the terminal success state is visible to `get_job` (290-306) for the whole append window and is only afterwards overwritten to `failed`. The frontend poller treats `succeeded` as terminal (`useJobPolling.ts:5`) and never sees the flip. The fix is to await the ledger append first and set `succeeded` only after it returns.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** Fixed with BUG-298 (same five lines): the ledger append now runs before the job is marked `succeeded`, so the job stays `running` until the audit is on the ledger and goes straight to `failed` if the append fails. Regression: `test_a_job_is_not_reported_succeeded_before_its_ledger_append` in `tests/test_audit_pool_recovery.py` (fails on the old code).

## BUG-300: Unauthenticated /audit/sth and /audit/inclusion parse whole audit-log files and build Merkle trees synchronously on the event loop
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/counterfactual_service` (3 lenses + adversarial verify), 2026-10-02. The verifier confirmed it from the code; nothing was run end to end unless stated.
- **Severity:** high
- **Root cause:** `counterfactual_service/main.py` ~1142: `get_sth` calls `daily_merkle_root(target_day)` (line 1142) and `get_inclusion_proof` calls `inclusion_proof_for_record(record_hash, day=day)` (line 1202) directly inside `async def` handlers with no asyncio.to_thread. shared/audit_log.py:287-314 reads the day's JSONL line by line with json.loads per line, then hashes every leaf and builds the tree; inclusion_proof_for_record with no `day` repeats the file read for up to 30 daily files (audit_log.py:373-379) and does a linear `record_hash not in hashes` list scan per day. BUG-043 offloaded 8 other sites in this file but not these two. Both routes are on the gateway's public (no-JWT) path list (shared/middleware.py:131,155). Failure scenario: The daily audit JSONL holds a record per audited request/finding (tens of thousands of lines on a busy day). An anonymous client calls GET /api/v1/counterfactual/audit/inclusion/<any 64-hex not in the log> with no `day`: the handler parses 30 days of JSONL on the single uvicorn worker's loop and returns 404. For the duration (seconds), every other tenant's request, health check and SSE stream is frozen; a handful of such requests in a loop keeps the gateway unresponsive without any credentials.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** `GET /counterfactual/audit/sth` and `/audit/inclusion/{record_hash}` run `daily_merkle_root` / `inclusion_proof_for_record` through `asyncio.to_thread`; a `record_hash` that is not 64 hex characters or a `day` that is not `YYYYMMDD` gets a 400 before any log file is read. Regression: `tests/test_audit_event_loop_bounds.py`. Residual: the endpoints stay unauthenticated by design (public verification) and have no rate limit of their own; a well-formed unknown hash still costs a 30-day scan, now off the event loop.

## BUG-301: POST /audit/financial runs the whole PCAOB audit, per-finding audit-log writes and fingerprinting on the event loop over unbounded input lists
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/counterfactual_service` (3 lenses + adversarial verify), 2026-10-02. The verifier confirmed it from the code; nothing was run end to end unless stated.
- **Severity:** high
- **Root cause:** `counterfactual_service/main.py` ~740: FinancialAuditRequest (main.py:702-712) declares ledger/purchase_orders/invoices/journal_entries/historical_reports/goods_receipts as List[Dict] with no max_length. `agent.run_full_audit` (line 740) is `async` but contains no real await: agents/specialists/financial_auditor.py:374-385 runs four pure-Python passes over every row and calls the synchronous `audit_event(...)` file append once per finding. `dataset_fingerprint` (line 745, canonical_dumps + sha256 of all inputs) and `build_completion_document` (line 749, one canonical_dumps + sha256 per finding) also run inline. Only `sign_and_persist` (line 752) is offloaded. The only body cap in the gateway, UploadBodyLimitMiddleware, applies to paths ending in /upload. Failure scenario: A tenant POSTs /api/v1/counterfactual/audit/financial with 300k journal_entries whose amounts are multiples of 1000. The round-dollar test emits 300k findings, each doing a synchronous audit-log append on the loop, followed by canonical JSON serialization of the whole payload and 300k per-finding hashes. The single worker is blocked for many seconds to minutes: all other tenants' requests time out, and asyncio.wait_for timers elsewhere cannot fire. Expected: the audit runs off-loop and the lists are capped.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** Each `FinancialAuditRequest` input list is capped at 50,000 rows (`MAX_AUDIT_ROWS`, 422 past it), and `dataset_fingerprint` and `build_completion_document` run through `asyncio.to_thread`. Regression: `test_financial_audit_input_lists_are_bounded` in `tests/test_audit_event_loop_bounds.py`. Residual: `FinancialAuditorAgent.run_full_audit` itself still runs on the event loop, including its per-finding audit-log writes; it is bounded by the row cap but not offloaded.

## BUG-302: _jobs registry is never evicted: every job, including each cached demo click, stays in memory with its full artifact and Task for the process lifetime
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/counterfactual_service` (3 lenses + adversarial verify), 2026-10-02. The verifier confirmed it from the code; nothing was run end to end unless stated.
- **Severity:** medium
- **Root cause:** `counterfactual_service/main.py` ~63: `_jobs` is a module-level dict written by `_new_job` (line 272) and by lines 217/244/498, which store the full artifact dict (estimates, refutations, challenges, rendered views, plus identification/sensitivity/data_quality for audits) and the asyncio Task (`_task`, lines 286/379/530). No code path deletes an entry, applies a TTL or caps the size. The demo fast path (lines 358-359) adds a new entry on every POST /counterfactual/demo/{id}, even though it only serves a pre-warmed artifact. Failure scenario: On the long-lived single-worker gateway, the frontend's one-click demo and audit flows each create a job per click. After weeks of uptime (or a script looping POST /counterfactual/demo/fair_lending, or repeated /counterfactual/audit runs), `_jobs` holds every job ever submitted, each audit entry pinning a multi-KB to multi-hundred-KB artifact and a finished Task. RSS grows monotonically until the small box's OOM killer takes down the gateway, which also wipes all job state for in-flight audits. Expected: completed jobs are evicted by age or count. Verifier's note: `_jobs` in counterfactual_service/main.py is an insert-only module-level dict: `_new_job` (line 272) adds an entry per submitted job and nothing ever removes one, so on the single long-lived gateway worker it grows for the process lifetime. Live jobs (lines 217, 244, 498) each retain their own full artifact dict plus the finished asyncio Task (lines 286, 379, 530). The cached demo fast path (lines 358-359) also adds an entry per click, but it only references the shared `_demo_last_good` artifact, so its per-click cost is a small record rather than a full artifact copy.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** `_new_job` calls `_evict_finished_jobs`, which keeps at most 500 jobs (`MAX_RETAINED_JOBS`) by dropping the oldest finished ones; queued and running jobs are never dropped. Regression: `tests/test_counterfactual_jobs_eviction.py` (3 tests; they use the new constant, so they were not run against the old code). A dropped job's poller gets a 404; the signed audit stays retrievable from the ledger.

## BUG-303: Six test files gated on the causal dependencies run in no CI lane, so their tests are skipped everywhere and report green
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** CI failure on PR #630 (2026-10-02): a new tmle test failed in the base lane with `No module named 'sklearn'`, which led to checking where the dowhy/econml-gated tests actually run.
- **Severity:** medium
- **Root cause:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` ~153: the Causal Tests lane, the only one that installs `requirements-causal.txt`, runs `tests/test_counterfactual_*.py tests/test_uasr_canary_causal_estimator.py`. `test_audit_encode.py`, `test_audit_endpoint.py`, `test_critic_timeout.py`, `test_demo_endpoints.py`, `test_demo_scenarios.py` and `test_iv_estimator.py` call `pytest.importorskip("dowhy")` / `("econml")` and match neither pattern, so the base lane skips those tests and no other lane runs them -- contrary to `.claude/rules/testing.md` (Tier B needs a lane that installs the dependency and runs the file). Failure scenario: a change breaks the audit worker's end-to-end path, the critic timeout handling or a demo scenario; every CI lane stays green because the tests that would catch it are skipped. They only ever ran on a developer machine that has the causal stack installed.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** the Causal Tests lane now also runs those six files.

## BUG-304: Production auth_mode guard only rejects the literal "open", but the token endpoint treats every value other than "password" as open mode
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/shared` (3 lenses + adversarial verify), 2026-10-02. The verifier confirmed it from the code; nothing was run end to end unless stated.
- **Severity:** high
- **Root cause:** `shared/config.py` ~254: _reject_open_auth_in_production raises only when `v == "open"` (exact, case-sensitive, no allowlist), while the sole consumer, api_gateway/routers/auth.py:99-101, does `if settings.auth_mode == "password": ... else: return await _issue_token_open(body)`. Any other string ("Password", "PASSWORD", "password ", "oidc", "sso", "") passes the production validator yet selects the no-credential open branch. The validator's own error text invites this ("Set AURA_AUTH_MODE=password (or another credential-validating mode)"), but no other mode exists. Failure scenario: Production deploy with ENVIRONMENT=production, AURA_JWT_ENABLED=true and AURA_AUTH_MODE=Password (or =oidc on an SSO-only deployment). Startup succeeds. An unauthenticated caller sends POST /api/v1/auth/token (a public path) with {"user_id":"<victim org_id>","role":"admin"}. _issue_token_open mints a signed JWT with sub and org_id set to the caller-chosen value and role=admin, with no password check. That token passes JWTAuthMiddleware, require_role("admin") and require_tenant, giving full admin access to any tenant. Verifier's note: The stated root cause is accurate. auth_mode is a free-form str whose production validator (aurabackend/shared/config.py:254) denylists only the exact literal "open", while the sole consumer (aurabackend/api_gateway/routers/auth.py:99-101) allowlists only the exact literal "password" and falls through to _issue_token_open for everything else. The two checks are not complements, so any unrecognised, mis-cased or whitespace-padded value fails open in production. The fix is to restrict auth_mode to the allowlist {"open","password"} (normalised), or have the router fail closed on unknown modes.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** PR #642. `AURA_AUTH_MODE` is normalised (strip, lower-case) and must be `open` or `password`; anything else fails at startup in every environment. `POST /auth/token` mints open-mode tokens only for the explicit `open` value and returns 503 for any other mode. Regression: `tests/test_auth_mode_fail_closed.py` (13 tests, all fail on the old code).

## BUG-305: Audit trail can be silently off in production: audit_log parses AURA_AUDIT_ENABLED differently from the production validator that requires it
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/shared` (3 lenses + adversarial verify), 2026-10-02. The verifier confirmed it from the code; nothing was run end to end unless stated.
- **Severity:** medium
- **Root cause:** `shared/audit_log.py` ~48: `AUDIT_ENABLED = os.getenv("AURA_AUDIT_ENABLED", "false").lower() == "true"` is the flag that actually gates `_AuditWriter.append` and `AuditLogMiddleware`. The fail-loud production check (`_require_audit_trail_in_production`, config.py:225-242) validates a separate pydantic bool, `settings.audit_enabled`, which accepts 1/yes/on/y/t and also reads the value from aurabackend/.env or the root .env. audit_log accepts only the exact string "true" (no whitespace strip) and only from the process environment. Failure scenario: Operator sets AURA_AUDIT_ENABLED=1 (or yes/on), or puts AURA_AUDIT_ENABLED=true only in the .env file that pydantic loads, with ENVIRONMENT=production. settings.audit_enabled is True, so the production validator passes and the service boots. audit_log.AUDIT_ENABLED is False, so AuditLogMiddleware skips every request and audit_prompt/audit_event/audit_human_override return without writing. No hash-chained audit record is produced, and the deployment appears to have passed the guard written to prevent exactly this. Verifier's note: The audit on/off flag has two independent sources of truth. `shared/audit_log.py:48` computes `AUDIT_ENABLED` at import from `os.getenv(...).lower() == "true"`, process environment only. The production fail-loud validator in `shared/config.py:223-242` checks pydantic's `settings.audit_enabled`, which accepts 1/yes/on/y/t and also reads `aurabackend/.env` and the root `.env`. Nothing exports the pydantic value back to `os.environ`, and `audit_log` never consults `settings`, so the guard can pass while the runtime gate is False. The fix is for `audit_log` to derive `AUDIT_ENABLED` from `settings.audit_enabled`, or for both to share one parser.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** PR #648. `shared/audit_log.AUDIT_ENABLED` now comes from `settings.audit_enabled`, the same value the production guard checks, so there is one parser. Regression: `tests/test_audit_flag_single_source.py` runs a fresh interpreter per value (`1`, `yes`, `on` fail on the old code).

## BUG-306: OIDC tenant mapping puts unrelated users who share a public email domain into the same tenant
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/shared` (3 lenses + adversarial verify), 2026-10-02. The verifier confirmed it from the code; nothing was run end to end unless stated.
- **Severity:** medium
- **Root cause:** `shared/oidc.py` ~190: When the id_token has no configured org claim and no tid/hd, map_org falls back to `email.split("@", 1)[1].lower()` for any `email_verified: true` token and returns it as org_id. email_verified proves mailbox ownership, not organisation membership. There is no exclusion for public mail domains and no way to disable the fallback (setting AURA_OIDC_ORG_CLAIM does not stop it when the claim is absent). oidc_callback (api_gateway/routers/auth.py:284) writes the result straight into the AURA JWT's org_id, which require_tenant and current_workspace_id use as the isolation key. Failure scenario: AURA_OIDC_ISSUER=https://accounts.google.com (or any Auth0/Keycloak realm that allows social or self-signup). alice@gmail.com and mallory@gmail.com each sign in; consumer Google accounts carry no hd claim and email_verified is true. Both receive JWTs with org_id="gmail.com", so mallory reads and writes alice's workspace data (uploads, saved queries, hooks, ledger rows), all scoped only by org_id. Likewise, anyone who can get a verified address at a company's domain from the IdP lands in that company's tenant. Verifier's note: map_org (aurabackend/shared/oidc.py:187-190) deliberately falls back to the verified email's domain as org_id when the configured org claim, tid and hd are all absent. There is no public/free-mail domain denylist, no allowed-domain list and no switch to make an org claim mandatory; setting AURA_OIDC_ORG_CLAIM only changes which claim is tried first. On an IdP that admits consumer or self-signup accounts, unrelated users sharing a mail domain are therefore mapped to the same tenant. Exposure is conditional on that IdP configuration; single-tenant enterprise IdPs that emit tid/hd/org claims are not affected.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** With no org claim, a verified email at a consumer mailbox domain (`_PUBLIC_EMAIL_DOMAINS` in `shared/oidc.py`) maps to a personal tenant derived from issuer + subject (`personal-<hash>`) instead of the domain; a company domain is still the tenant and an org claim (`AURA_OIDC_ORG_CLAIM`, `tid`, `hd`) still takes precedence. Regression: `tests/test_oidc_public_domain_tenant.py` (6 tests, 3 fail on the old code). Residual: the domain list cannot be complete -- an IdP that serves the public should send an org claim. Consequence: a user who already signed in under a public-domain tenant gets a new, empty personal tenant.

## BUG-307: Redis URL, including any embedded password, is written to the application log
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/shared` (3 lenses + adversarial verify), 2026-10-02. The verifier confirmed it from the code; nothing was run end to end unless stated.
- **Severity:** low
- **Root cause:** `shared/rate_limit.py` ~139: `logger.info("Rate limiter: using Redis backend (%s)", settings.redis_url)` and the warning at lines 142-145 log the full AURA_REDIS_URL verbatim. Redis credentials are conventionally carried in the URL userinfo, and logging_config has no redaction filter. create_service calls get_rate_limit_backend() at startup in every service. Failure scenario: AURA_REDIS_URL=rediss://default:S3cretPw@redis.internal:6379/0. On every start of every microservice, the line "Rate limiter: using Redis backend (rediss://default:S3cretPw@redis.internal:6379/0)" goes to stdout, the rotating LOG_FILE and any log shipper (Loki/CloudWatch). Anyone with log read access gets the Redis password and can read or flush the rate-limit keys (resetting auth brute-force buckets) and any other data on that Redis. Verifier's note: `get_rate_limit_backend()` in `aurabackend/shared/rate_limit.py` logs the raw `settings.redis_url` (a plain `str` from `AURA_REDIS_URL`, `shared/config.py:433`) at INFO on line 139 and at WARNING on lines 142-145, with no masking of the URL userinfo. `shared/logging_config.py` has no redaction filter (only `_RequestIdFilter`), so the message reaches stdout and the rotating log file unchanged. `shared/service_factory.py:130-131` calls it at app construction when `settings.rate_limit_enabled` is true. Because `aioredis.from_url` is lazy, the INFO line at 139 is the path that fires in practice; the WARNING branch is rarely reached. The leak only exposes a secret when the URL carries a password in its userinfo.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** PR #643. The rate limiter logs the Redis scheme, host and port only (`_redact_url`). Regression: `test_the_redis_password_never_reaches_the_log` in `tests/test_rate_limit_resilience.py`.

## BUG-308: APIKeyMiddleware raises on a non-ASCII X-API-Key, returning 500 instead of 401
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/shared` (3 lenses + adversarial verify), 2026-10-02. The verifier confirmed it from the code; nothing was run end to end unless stated.
- **Severity:** low
- **Root cause:** `shared/middleware.py` ~217: `hmac.compare_digest(supplied, self._api_key)` is called with two `str` values. compare_digest raises `TypeError: comparing strings with non-ASCII characters is not supported` when either str contains a non-ASCII character. Starlette decodes header bytes as latin-1, so any header byte >= 0x80 produces such a string. The values are not encoded to bytes first and the call is not guarded. Failure scenario: AURA_API_KEY is set. An unauthenticated client sends any non-public request with header `X-API-Key: \xe9`. `supplied` is 'é', compare_digest raises TypeError inside dispatch, the "API key rejected" warning (with client IP) is never logged, and the caller gets 500 INTERNAL_ERROR with a full stack trace logged per request via logger.exception, instead of a 401. The same crash hits every legitimate request if the operator's configured key contains a non-ASCII character, since the header value then also decodes to a non-ASCII str.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** PR #646. `APIKeyMiddleware` compares bytes (header re-encoded as latin-1, configured key as UTF-8), so a non-ASCII key is a logged 401. Regression: 4 tests in `tests/test_middleware.py` (all fail on the old code).

## BUG-309: Outbound webhook dispatcher delivers every tenant's events to every tenant's subscriptions
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/shared` (3 lenses + adversarial verify), 2026-10-02. The verifier confirmed it from the code; nothing was run end to end unless stated.
- **Severity:** high
- **Root cause:** `shared/webhook_dispatcher.py` ~291: WebhookDispatcher.start() subscribes to the bus with streaming_manager.subscribe("*") and no workspace_id (line 252). StreamingManager._workspace_allowed treats a subscriber with workspace_id None as allowed to see every tenant's events, so the dispatcher receives all of them. _run() then selects targets with `matched = [s for s in self._subs.values() if s.active and s.matches(event_type)]` (line 291), which never compares sub.workspace_id with event.workspace_id. BUG-016 scoped only the CRUD/deliveries methods, and BUG-018 scoped only the SSE bus and the delivery-log event; the fan-out itself was left unscoped and is not in the registry. Failure scenario: Tenant B registers POST /webhooks {url: "https://b.example/hook", events: ["*"]} (or ["pipeline.complete", "agent.complete"]). Tenant A runs an async pipeline; pipelines.py:261 publishes publish_complete(TOPIC_PIPELINE, run_id, run.model_dump(), workspace_id=A). _classify maps it to "pipeline.complete", B's subscription matches, and _deliver POSTs {"data": <A's full run dump>} to B's URL. The same applies to agent.complete (report.to_dict()), upload.complete, etl.complete, query.complete and hook.fired. Expected: only subscriptions whose workspace_id equals event.workspace_id (or untenanted system events) receive the event.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** `WebhookDispatcher._targets` selects the subscriptions for an event and requires the subscription's workspace to equal the event's; `_run` uses it. An event with no workspace goes only to subscriptions that have none. Regression: `TestFanOutTenantScope` in `tests/test_webhook_dispatcher.py` (4 tests; they call the new method, so they were not run against the old code, whose inline filter never compared workspaces). Residual: an event published without a workspace (e.g. a UASR drift event, if the publisher does not set one) now reaches no tenant's webhook instead of all of them.

## BUG-310: is_public_url accepts CGNAT 100.64.0.0/10 and IPv6 site-local fec0::/10 addresses as public
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/shared` (3 lenses + adversarial verify), 2026-10-02. The verifier confirmed it from the code; nothing was run end to end unless stated.
- **Severity:** medium
- **Root cause:** `shared/ssrf.py` ~40: The check is a deny-list of ipaddress flags: is_private, is_loopback, is_link_local, is_reserved, is_multicast, is_unspecified (lines 40-44). Python sets none of these for the shared address space 100.64.0.0/10 (is_private is False and is_global is also False) or for deprecated site-local fec0::/10. Evaluated locally: ip_address('100.100.100.200') and ip_address('100.64.0.1') return False for all six flags, so is_public_url returns True. Rejecting on `not ip.is_global` would close both. Not in the registry (BUG-054/BUG-287 cover only the flags listed). Failure scenario: A tenant calls POST /webhooks with url "http://100.100.100.200/latest/meta-data/" (Alibaba Cloud instance metadata), or "http://100.64.x.y:port/" on a deployment whose pods or VPN peers sit in CGNAT space (EKS custom networking, Tailscale). webhooks.py's _is_ssrf_safe_url -> is_public_url returns True, the subscription is stored, and POST /webhooks/{id}/test makes the backend send the request and return http_status/error as a scan oracle. The streaming webhook sink and websocket source (webhook_sink.py:56/95, websocket_source.py:92/135) accept the same hosts. Expected: False for any non-globally-routable address. Verifier's note: The root cause as stated is right, but the suggested fix is incomplete. On the local Python 3.14.5, ip_address('fec0::1').is_global is True (and is_site_local is True), so rejecting on `not ip.is_global` closes CGNAT 100.64.0.0/10 (is_global False) but would NOT close fec0::/10. Closing both needs `not ip.is_global or ip.is_site_local` (or an explicit fec0::/10 network check) in addition to the existing flags.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** PR #645. `is_public_url` is an allow-rule: every resolved address must be globally routable unicast (`ip.is_global`, not multicast, not IPv6 site-local, IPv4-mapped addresses judged by the IPv4 address). Regression: `tests/test_ssrf_non_global_addresses.py` (22 cases; CGNAT and site-local fail on the old check).

## BUG-311: SQL expression guard is bypassed by wrapping a quote in a block comment
- **Status:** fixed
