- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/shared` (3 lenses + adversarial verify), 2026-10-02. The verifier confirmed it from the code; nothing was run end to end unless stated.
- **Severity:** low
- **Root cause:** `shared/sql_expression_guard.py` ~47: _strip_quoted (lines 47-53) blanks everything between two apostrophes before the ';', keyword and call-position checks run, but it does not understand SQL comments. An apostrophe inside /* ... */ is a comment to DuckDB yet opens a 'string' for the regex, so all text up to the next apostrophe (the real SQL) is blanked out of `bare`. _REPLACEMENT_SCAN_PATTERN on the raw text also misses it because the comment sits between FROM and the quote. This defeats the BUG-220 statement-stacking fix and the BUG-207/114 function blocks; it is distinct from BUG-276 (double-quoted identifiers, comma-joined literals). Failure scenario: Evaluated against the origin/main module: validate_sql_expression("amount > 0 /*'*/ ; DROP TABLE t; -- /*'*/") and validate_sql_expression("id IN (SELECT column0 FROM /*'*/ read_csv_auto('/etc/passwd') /*'*/)") both return without raising, while the same payloads without the comment wrappers are rejected. DuckDB parses the first as two statements and the second as a real read_csv_auto call. Current impact is limited to defence in depth: the three callers (etl.py:446, pipeline/engine.py:259, causal_service/main.py:82-83) all disable external access on the connection before the spliced SQL runs, so file reads fail today; any new caller relying on the guard alone, or a path that skips lock_down_connection, would be fully exposed. Verifier's note: `_STRING_OR_IDENTIFIER` (sql_expression_guard.py:47), applied by `_strip_quoted` (lines 50-53), pairs quote characters with no awareness of SQL comments. An apostrophe (or double quote) inside `/* ... */` is inert to DuckDB but opens a "string" for the regex, so the real SQL up to the next quote is blanked out of `bare` before the `;`, `_BLOCKED_PATTERN` and `_BLOCKED_CALL_PATTERN` checks at lines 84-98. `_REPLACEMENT_SCAN_PATTERN` on the raw text also misses it because the comment sits between FROM and the quote. This bypasses the BUG-220 statement-stacking check and the BUG-053/207 function blocks, and is distinct from BUG-276. External-access lockdown at the callers mitigates file and network reads only; stacked statements against the connection's own tables still run.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** PR #649. `_strip_quoted` is a left-to-right scanner that blanks quoted spans and refuses a comment outside one, or an unterminated quote. Regression: `tests/test_sql_guard_comment_bypass.py` (16 cases; 8 fail on the old guard). The guard is defence in depth: the connections that run these expressions are locked down (BUG-196, BUG-276).

## BUG-312: Kafka consume_batch idle timeout can never fire at default settings: an idle or drained topic loops forever
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/shared` (3 lenses + adversarial verify), 2026-10-02. The verifier confirmed it from the code; nothing was run end to end unless stated.
- **Severity:** high
- **Root cause:** `shared/kafka_client.py` ~96: The idle stop is implemented as asyncio.wait_for(consumer.getmany(timeout_ms=1000, max_records=500), timeout=idle_timeout_s). getmany() returns an empty dict by itself after 1000 ms, so wait_for only raises TimeoutError when idle_timeout_s < 1s. With the default timeout_ms=5000 (line 68) the call always returns {} first, and lines 104-106 `if not batch: continue` loop again with no elapsed-idle accounting. The only other exit is len(rows) >= max_messages (default 10,000). The caller, pipeline/engine.py:458 `rows = await consume_batch(cfg, progress_cb=progress_cb)`, applies no outer timeout. Failure scenario: A pipeline has a Kafka source with the default timeout_ms=5000 and a topic holding 0 messages (or 300 messages, fewer than max_messages=10000). consume_batch reads what exists, then spins on getmany() once per second forever. The pipeline run never completes or fails, the engine's 'returned no messages within timeout_ms' error at engine.py:459-463 is unreachable, and the consumer and its group membership stay open for the life of the process. Each further run adds another permanent task and consumer.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** PR #644. `consume_batch` measures idle time across polls and stops once no message has arrived for the configured timeout. Regression: `tests/test_kafka_idle_timeout.py` (fake consumer following `getmany()`s contract; 2 of 3 time out on the old code).

## BUG-313: Redis rate-limit backend never falls back and has no timeouts: a Redis outage makes every request fail or hang
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/shared` (3 lenses + adversarial verify), 2026-10-02. The verifier confirmed it from the code; nothing was run end to end unless stated.
- **Severity:** high
- **Root cause:** `shared/rate_limit.py` ~94: RedisBackend.__init__ calls aioredis.from_url(redis_url, decode_responses=True), which is lazy (no connection is made) and sets no socket_timeout or socket_connect_timeout. The try/except in get_rate_limit_backend (lines 137-146) that promises 'Redis unavailable, falling back to in-memory' therefore can never trigger on an unreachable Redis. check_and_record (lines 103-108) awaits pipe.execute() with no try/except, and RateLimitMiddleware.dispatch (middleware.py:392) awaits it unguarded before call_next. service_factory.py:131 wires this backend into every service whenever AURA_REDIS_URL is set. Failure scenario: AURA_REDIS_URL is set and Redis is down or restarting. Startup still logs 'using Redis backend'. Every non-exempt request then raises ConnectionError inside the rate-limit middleware and returns 500 before reaching any handler, so the whole API is down. If Redis is instead unreachable at the network level (packets dropped), the connection attempt has no timeout and each request hangs in the middleware until the OS TCP timeout, piling up on the single worker. (Also reported by the auth lens: the fallback branch at rate_limit.py:138 can never trigger.)
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** PR #643. The Redis client has 1 s connect and socket timeouts, and a failed check falls back to an in-process limiter for that call (warning at most once a minute). Regression: `tests/test_rate_limit_resilience.py` against a real refused and a real unroutable address (all fail on the old code).

## BUG-314: StreamingManager replay buffers are never evicted: one retained list per job topic for the life of the process
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/shared` (3 lenses + adversarial verify), 2026-10-02. The verifier confirmed it from the code; nothing was run end to end unless stated.
- **Severity:** medium
- **Root cause:** `shared/streaming_manager.py` ~145: publish() does self._buffers.setdefault(event.topic, []) and caps each list at _BUFFER_LEN=50 events, but nothing ever removes a topic key. The only other references to _buffers are the constructor (line 110) and the reader get_buffered_events (line 175). Topics are per job (query:<job_id>, agent:<run_id>, upload:<file_id>, etl:<run_id>, pipeline:<id>), so the number of keys grows with every job ever run, and each retains up to 50 events including the 'complete' event whose payload carries the full result dict (publish_complete, lines 212-224). Failure scenario: On a long-running gateway, each query, agent run, upload and ETL run publishes progress and complete events on a fresh topic. After N jobs the bus holds N topic lists with up to 50 payloads each (result sets, agent outputs) that no subscriber will ever read again. Memory climbs steadily with usage until the single worker is OOM-killed on the small deployment box, dropping all in-flight requests and in-memory state. Verifier's note: StreamingManager.publish() (aurabackend/shared/streaming_manager.py:145) creates a replay list per topic via self._buffers.setdefault(event.topic, []) and bounds each list to _BUFFER_LEN=50, but no code path ever removes a topic key (no eviction on complete/error, unsubscribe, TTL or max-topic cap). Since topics are per job (query:/agent:/upload:/etl:/pipeline:<id>), the dict grows by one entry per job for the process lifetime. Retained payloads are mostly small progress/complete dicts (the query complete event holds only a row count and timing, not the result set), so this is slow unbounded growth rather than retention of full result sets.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** PR #650. `StreamingManager` buffers at most 500 topics (`_MAX_TOPICS`), dropping the least recently published to. Regression: `tests/test_streaming_manager_buffer_eviction.py` (uses the new cap; not run against the old code). Consequence: Last-Event-ID replay for a topic idle behind 500 others returns nothing.

## BUG-315: AuditLogMiddleware does a synchronous locked write plus fsync on the event loop for every request
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/shared` (3 lenses + adversarial verify), 2026-10-02. The verifier confirmed it from the code; nothing was run end to end unless stated.
- **Severity:** medium
- **Root cause:** `shared/middleware.py` ~103: AuditLogMiddleware.dispatch (async) calls audit_request() directly. That runs _AuditWriter.append (shared/audit_log.py:113-154), which takes a threading.Lock, opens the day file, writes, flushes and calls os.fsync (line 145), all on the event-loop thread with no asyncio.to_thread. On the first write of a process or of a new UTC day, _rotate_if_needed also calls _tail_hash (lines 92-106), which reads the entire existing day file line by line. The middleware is added unconditionally at service_factory.py:159 and is active whenever AURA_AUDIT_ENABLED=true. The same lock is taken by audit_prompt/audit_event calls made from worker threads, so the loop thread can also block waiting on another thread's fsync. Failure scenario: With AURA_AUDIT_ENABLED=true, every non-health request ends with an fsync on the loop thread. On a slow or network-backed volume each fsync takes tens of milliseconds, during which no other request, SSE stream or background task can run, so throughput is capped at roughly 1/fsync-latency for the whole service. After a restart late in the day the first request also stalls the loop while the full day's audit file is read to recover the tail hash.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** PR #651. `AuditLogMiddleware` awaits `audit_request` through `asyncio.to_thread`. Regression: `test_the_audit_write_does_not_run_on_the_event_loop` (fails on the old code).

## BUG-316: Webhook PATCH bypasses the retries cap: uncapped or negative retries create multi-hour delivery tasks or silently disable delivery
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/shared` (3 lenses + adversarial verify), 2026-10-02. The verifier confirmed it from the code; nothing was run end to end unless stated.
- **Severity:** medium
- **Root cause:** `shared/webhook_dispatcher.py` ~230: register() clamps retries with max(0, min(retries, 10)) (line 215), but update() applies fields with a bare setattr(sub, k, v) (lines 227-231), and the router's WebhookUpdateRequest.retries is an unconstrained Optional[int] (api_gateway/routers/webhooks.py:57, passed through at line 155). _deliver then loops `while attempts <= sub.retries` (line 340) with a sleep of up to 30s per attempt (line 353). _run spawns one fire_and_forget delivery task per matching event (lines 296-302) with no concurrency limit, and fire_test awaits _deliver inline (line 400). Failure scenario: A tenant sends PATCH /webhooks/{id} with {"retries": 1000000} for a subscription whose URL returns 500 or times out. Every matching event now starts a task that retries every 30s for hours (it eventually dies with an OverflowError from 0.5 * 2**(attempts-1) at around attempt 1025, without recording a delivery). These tasks pile up one per event, each making outbound POSTs through the shared httpx client, and POST /webhooks/{id}/test hangs for the same duration. With {"retries": -1} the while loop never executes, so no POST is ever sent and every event is logged as failed with attempts=0.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** PR #647. `WebhookDispatcher.update` clamps retries through the same helper as `register` (0..10). Regression: `tests/test_webhook_retries_cap.py` (the out-of-range cases fail on the old code).

## BUG-317: In-memory rate limiter never deletes client keys: one dict entry per distinct IP forever
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/shared` (3 lenses + adversarial verify), 2026-10-02. The verifier confirmed it from the code; nothing was run end to end unless stated.
- **Severity:** low
- **Root cause:** `shared/rate_limit.py` ~75: InMemoryBackend.check_and_record does self._hits[key] = [t for t in self._hits[key] if t > cutoff] on a defaultdict(list). Old timestamps are pruned but the key is always reassigned, even to an empty list, and no code path removes keys outside the test-only reset_all(). This is the default backend for every service (AURA_REDIS_URL unset), keyed by client IP plus a second 'auth:<ip>' key for credential endpoints. Failure scenario: A public-facing gateway receives requests from many distinct source addresses over weeks (scanners, or a client rotating through an IPv6 /64). Each new address adds a permanent key and list to _hits, plus an 'auth:' twin when it touches a login path. The dict only grows, so memory creeps upward until restart on the single long-lived worker.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** PR #643. The in-memory limiter sweeps keys with no hit in the last hour every 1000 recorded requests. Regression: `test_in_memory_keys_for_departed_clients_are_swept` (fails on the old code).

## BUG-318: SSE hook opens a native EventSource with no bearer token, so every live stream 401s when JWT auth is on
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `frontend/src` (3 lenses, 2 adversarial verifiers per finding -- code, and registry/reachability -- both had to confirm), 2026-10-02.
- **Severity:** high
- **Root cause:** `frontend/src/hooks/useSSE.ts` ~103: openConnection() does `new EventSource(url.toString())`. A native EventSource cannot send an Authorization header, and the URL carries no token. The target, GET /api/v1/stream/{topic} (aurabackend/api_gateway/routers/stream.py:95), is not in _PUBLIC_PATHS or _PUBLIC_PATH_PREFIXES (aurabackend/shared/middleware.py:118-166). JWTAuthMiddleware.dispatch (middleware.py:243-251) only reads the Authorization header and returns 401 without it. BUG-041's own entry states this route is JWT-gated. Every other call in the app goes through ApiClient or authFetch, which add the bearer; this hook is the one path that cannot. Failure scenario: With AURA_JWT_ENABLED=true (required in production), a signed-in user opens Pipelines, generates an AI pipeline and clicks Execute. POST /pipeline/execute/async succeeds and returns a run_id. PipelineMonitor then subscribes via useSSE({topic: `pipeline:${runId}`}) (frontend/src/components/PipelineMonitor.tsx:82); the EventSource gets 401, onerror retries 10 times and gives up. PipelineMonitor passes no onError, so onSettled never fires and no plan, progress, complete or error event ever arrives. The monitor stays at 0% and Execute/Preview stay on "Running…" permanently, although the run finished server-side. The same 401 silences LiveFeedPanel and usePipelineTelemetry (system:health, uasr:metrics). (Also reported by the security-ux lens.)
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** PR #653. useSSE uses a fetch-based event-stream client (`hooks/fetchEventSource.ts`) that sends the bearer token. Confirmed on the live site: the stream route returns 401 without a token and 200 with one. Regression: `hooks/__tests__/useSSE.test.ts`.

## BUG-319: Download, PDF and artifact links are plain navigations to JWT-protected endpoints, so they return 401 instead of the file
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `frontend/src` (3 lenses, 2 adversarial verifiers per finding -- code, and registry/reachability -- both had to confirm), 2026-10-02.
- **Severity:** high
- **Root cause:** `frontend/src/pages/PipelinesPanel.tsx` ~395: handleDownload (line 395) and handleAiDownload (line 523) call `window.open(url, '_blank')` on etlService/pipelineService.getDownloadUrl(), i.e. /api/v1/etl/download/{filename} and /api/v1/pipeline/download/{filename}. The same pattern is used as `<a href target="_blank">` in frontend/src/audit/Certificate.tsx:103 (auditApi.pdfUrl -> /api/v1/counterfactual/artifacts/{hash}/report.pdf) and frontend/src/pages/Counterfactual.tsx:208, 219 and 230 (report.pdf, artifact JSON, verify). A browser navigation cannot attach the bearer token, which lives only in localStorage and is sent only as a header. None of these paths is in _PUBLIC_PATHS/_PUBLIC_PATH_PREFIXES (aurabackend/shared/middleware.py:118-166), so JWTAuthMiddleware answers 401 before the route handler runs. Failure scenario: With JWT auth on, a signed-in user runs an ETL pipeline (or an AI pipeline) to completion and clicks Download. A new tab opens showing `{"error":"AUTHENTICATION_REQUIRED","message":"Bearer token required"}` and no file is downloaded. Likewise, clicking "Download PDF" on an audit certificate, or "Download PDF report" / "Open persisted artifact (JSON)" / "Verify ED25519 signature" on the Counterfactual page, opens the same 401 JSON instead of the report. (Also reported by the security-ux lens.)
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** PR #654. `fetchProtectedFile` (services/api.ts) and `AuthenticatedLink` fetch the file with the bearer token and save it or open it from a blob URL; PipelinesPanel, Certificate and Counterfactual use them. Regression: `components/__tests__/AuthenticatedLink.test.tsx`. Not exercised in a real browser against the live site.

## BUG-320: ApiClient throws a plain object and reads `message` instead of FastAPI's `detail`, so backend errors show as "[object Object]" or a generic fallback
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `frontend/src` (3 lenses, 2 adversarial verifiers per finding -- code, and registry/reachability -- both had to confirm), 2026-10-02.
- **Severity:** medium
- **Root cause:** `frontend/src/services/api.ts` ~382: ApiClient.request builds the error with `errorData.message || response.statusText` (line 382), but routes that raise HTTPException return `{"detail": ...}`: register_exception_handlers (aurabackend/shared/middleware.py:498+) only reshapes AuraError and unhandled Exception into `{message}`. The server's explanation is therefore dropped. In addition, createError (lines 425-437) returns a plain object literal, not an Error, so every caller written as `e instanceof Error ? e.message : String(e)` takes the else branch. Affected callers include frontend/src/components/HITL/ExceptionQueue.tsx:110, 136, 230 and 249, components/HITL/SubjectHistory.tsx:32, and the form handlers at workbench/panels/WebhooksPanel.tsx:201, DashboardsPanel.tsx:116, InboundHooksSection.tsx:218, LibraryPanel.tsx:162 and AddConnectionForm.tsx:140. Failure scenario: (1) In the HITL Exception Queue a user clicks Approve or Reject on a finding and the gateway rejects the decision (for example 403 from _require_auditor for a signed-in non-auditor, or 404 for an unknown record). financialAuditService.decide rejects with the plain ApiError object, and the panel's alert renders the literal text "[object Object]". (2) In Webhooks a user registers a URL that resolves to a private address; the backend answers 400 with detail "url must be a public http(s) address" (aurabackend/api_gateway/routers/webhooks.py:106). The form shows only "Could not register the webhook.", so the user cannot tell the URL was the problem. Verifiers' notes: Two defects in `ApiClient.request` / `createError` (`frontend/src/services/api.ts` ~381 and ~425-437): 1. `createError` returns a plain object literal rather than an `Error`. Every caller written as `e instanceof Error ? e.message : String(e)` renders "[object Object]" for any API failure (HTTP error, timeout or network error), and callers written as `err instanceof Error && err.message ? ... : fallback` always show the generic fallback. 2. The message is taken from `errorData.message` only. FastAPI `HTTPException` responses (`{"detail": ...}`) are not reshaped by `register_exception_handlers`, which handles only `AuraError` and `Exception`, so the server's explanation is lost and replaced by `response.statusText`. The 403 non-auditor example is largely pre-empted by `ensureAuditorToken` minting an auditor token (BUG-112), but any other failure on the Exception Queue calls still shows "[
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** PR #657. ApiClient throws `ApiRequestError` (a real Error carrying status/code/details) and reads the message from `message`, then FastAPI's `detail` (string or a 422 list). Regression: `services/__tests__/apiErrors.test.ts` (the detail and 422 cases fail on the old client).

## BUG-321: Public /verify/:hash, /certificate/:hash and the audit front door call JWT-gated endpoints, so anonymous visitors get HTTP 401
- **Status:** open
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `frontend/src` (3 lenses, 2 adversarial verifiers per finding -- code, and registry/reachability -- both had to confirm), 2026-10-02.
- **Severity:** high
- **Root cause:** `frontend/src/audit/VerifyPage.tsx` ~17: AppRoutes.tsx:19-28 mounts AuditFrontDoor, CertificatePage and VerifyPage under PublicShell with no ProtectedRoute. They call auditApi.verify / getArtifact / listScenarios (frontend/src/audit/auditApi.ts:20, 34, 50-55), and the comment at auditApi.ts:6-10 states that "the still-anonymous endpoints (scenario list, artifact verify) keep working untouched" because authFetch omits the header when logged out. But /api/v1/counterfactual/artifacts/{hash}/verify, /artifacts/{hash}, /audit/financial/verify/{hash} and /demo/scenarios are not in _PUBLIC_PATHS/_PUBLIC_PATH_PREFIXES (aurabackend/shared/middleware.py:118-166; only /counterfactual/jwks, /audit/sth and /audit/inclusion/ are). JWTAuthMiddleware returns 401 for a request with no Authorization header before the handlers in routers/counterfactual.py:127-141 and 166 are reached. Failure scenario: With JWT auth on, an external auditor who is not logged in opens a shared https://<host>/verify/<record_hash> link. auditApi.verify issues three header-less GETs that all 401; the final one throws and the page renders "Verification unavailable: HTTP 401: {\"error\":\"AUTHENTICATION_REQUIRED\",...}" instead of the signed/unsigned verdict. A logged-out visitor on the landing page "/" likewise sees the scenario list fail with HTTP 401 (AuditFrontDoor.tsx:20) rather than the demo scenarios. Verifiers' notes: The claimed root cause is accurate. The fault is on the backend: `aurabackend/shared/middleware.py` `_PUBLIC_PATHS` / `_PUBLIC_PATH_PREFIXES` omit `/api/v1/counterfactual/demo/scenarios`, `/api/v1/counterfactual/artifacts/{hash}` (and its `/verify`), and `/api/v1/counterfactual/audit/financial/verify/{hash}`, although the gateway handlers in `api_gateway/routers/counterfactual.py` are written as unauthenticated or optional-auth. The frontend files (`VerifyPage.tsx`, `CertificatePage.tsx`, `AuditFrontDoor.tsx`, `auditApi.ts`) are only where the symptom shows; the comment at `auditApi.ts:6-10` is wrong about these endpoints being anonymous when JWT is enabled. Same class as BUG-005, BUG-017 and BUG-037.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** Needs a product decision before any change: making the verification endpoints readable without login (by record hash) is what these pages are for, but it exposes that audit's certificate to anyone with the link. Asked the user on 2026-10-02.

## BUG-322: Connectors table reads a `status` field that GET /connections never returns, so every connection shows UNKNOWN
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `frontend/src` (3 lenses, 2 adversarial verifiers per finding -- code, and registry/reachability -- both had to confirm), 2026-10-02.
- **Severity:** medium
- **Root cause:** `frontend/src/workbench/panels/ConnectorsPanel.tsx` ~217: The Status column tests `c.status === 'connected'` and renders `(c.status || 'unknown').toUpperCase()` (lines 217-219, sort value at 224). GET /connections returns rows from persistence._row_to_connection_dict (aurabackend/api_gateway/persistence.py:1654-1673), whose keys are id, workspace_id, name, type, host, port, database, username, ssl, is_active, last_tested, table_count, created_at and updated_at. There is no `status` key; connection health is carried by `is_active`, which test_connection_by_id updates (aurabackend/api_gateway/routers/connections.py:420-431). The panel uses the raw response with no mapping. Failure scenario: A user adds a PostgreSQL connection in Workbench > Connectors. The form reports "Saved … Connected — Found 12 tables." and the backend stores is_active=true. The table reloads and the new row's Status cell shows a warning-coloured "UNKNOWN". Every connection, healthy or broken, always shows UNKNOWN, so the column never reflects the real state and sorting by it does nothing.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** PR #660. The status is derived from `is_active` / `last_tested` (CONNECTED, FAILED, UNTESTED); the test fixtures now use the real response shape. Regression: `ConnectorsPanel.test.tsx` (fails on the old panel).

## BUG-323: useSSE never asks for buffered events on first connect, so a fast pipeline run loses its plan/complete events and Execute stays on 'Running...' forever
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `frontend/src` (3 lenses, 2 adversarial verifiers per finding -- code, and registry/reachability -- both had to confirm), 2026-10-02.
- **Severity:** high
- **Root cause:** `frontend/src/hooks/useSSE.ts` ~97: openConnection() builds `${API_BASE}/stream/<topic>` with no `replay=true` (useSSE.ts:97-103). The gateway only replays buffered events when `replay=true` or a Last-Event-ID header is present (aurabackend/api_gateway/routers/stream.py:62-69: otherwise `missed = []`). But POST /pipeline/execute/async starts the run with fire_and_forget BEFORE returning the run_id (routers/pipelines.py:273-275), and the task publishes the 'plan' data event and progress immediately. PipelinesPanel only mounts <PipelineMonitor runId> (and therefore only opens the EventSource) after the POST response arrives and React re-renders (PipelinesPanel.tsx:453-456, 838-841). Every event published before the SSE subscription exists is dropped. PipelineMonitor is the only thing that resets `aiExecuting` (onSettled, PipelinesPanel.tsx:469), and it has no timeout or status fallback. BUG-244 fixed the server-side replay ordering, and BUG-250 fixed the SSE 'error' event path; neither makes the client request the replay. Failure scenario: User opens Pipelines > AI tab, generates a pipeline over a small CSV and clicks Execute Pipeline (or Preview). The run finishes in the tens of milliseconds it takes the browser to receive the run_id, render PipelineMonitor and open the EventSource. The 'complete' event was published before the subscription, so it never arrives: the monitor sits at 0% with all stages pending, no result card is shown, and both Preview and Execute stay disabled showing 'Loading...' / 'Running...' until the page is reloaded. Even on slower runs the 'plan' event is always missed, so the per-step list never renders. Verifiers' notes: `frontend/src/hooks/useSSE.ts` `openConnection()` (URL built at ~lines 97-101) never sends `replay=true` on first connect; it only sends a `last_event_id` query param on reconnect. That query param is itself ignored by the server, which reads `Last-Event-ID` only as a header (`aurabackend/api_gateway/routers/stream.py`, `stream_topic`). `GET /stream/{topic}` therefore replays nothing. Everything `pipeline_execute_async`'s fire-and-forget `_run` task publishes before the client subscribes is lost: always the plan event and the early progress events, and for fast runs the terminal complete/error event as well. The affected consumer is `frontend/src/pages/PipelinesPanel.tsx` (not `components/`): `setAiRunId` at line 455, `<PipelineMonitor>` at 838-840, and `handleAiRunSettled` at 469 as the only reset of `aiExecuting` once a run has started. `PipelineMonitor` has no timeout or status fallba
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** PR #653. useSSE has a `replay` option that asks for the topic's buffered events on the first connect; PipelineMonitor sets it, and settles if the stream is lost for good. Regression: `hooks/__tests__/useSSE.test.ts`.

## BUG-324: useSSE sends last_event_id as a query parameter but the gateway only reads the Last-Event-ID header, so reconnects never replay missed events
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `frontend/src` (3 lenses, 2 adversarial verifiers per finding -- code, and registry/reachability -- both had to confirm), 2026-10-02.
- **Severity:** medium
- **Root cause:** `frontend/src/hooks/useSSE.ts` ~101: On reconnect the hook does `url.searchParams.set('last_event_id', entry.lastEventId)` (useSSE.ts:101). The route declares `last_event_id: Optional[str] = Header(None, alias="Last-Event-ID")` (aurabackend/api_gateway/routers/stream.py:99), so the query parameter is ignored. The browser's automatic Last-Event-ID header is not sent either, because es.onerror closes the EventSource and openConnection creates a brand new one (useSSE.ts:159-180, 103). The file header promises 'tracks Last-Event-ID for missed-event replay on reconnect', but the replay branch on the server is never reached from this client. Failure scenario: User starts a longer AI pipeline run and watches PipelineMonitor. The SSE connection drops briefly (proxy idle timeout, network blip, laptop wake). The hook reconnects after its backoff with ?last_event_id=..., the gateway ignores it and replays nothing. If the run's 'complete' or 'error' event was published during the gap it is lost for good: the monitor stays on the last stage it saw and Execute stays on 'Running...' permanently. The same gap silently drops system:health and uasr:metrics events in the Terminal pipeline panel timeline.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** PR #653. The resume point goes in the `Last-Event-ID` header, which the gateway reads. Regression: `hooks/__tests__/useSSE.test.ts`.

## BUG-325: AuditWizard.pickFile has no request guard: an earlier upload's response overwrites the filename of the file now selected, so the audit runs on the wrong dataset
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `frontend/src` (3 lenses, 2 adversarial verifiers per finding -- code, and registry/reachability -- both had to confirm), 2026-10-02.
- **Severity:** high
- **Root cause:** `frontend/src/audit/AuditWizard.tsx` ~44: pickFile (AuditWizard.tsx:44-57) sets `file` (which drives the browser-side preview and the column mapping) synchronously, then awaits auditApi.uploadDataset(f) and unconditionally calls setFilename(name) and setUploading(false). Nothing ties a response to the pick that started it. UploadStep keeps the dropzone active during an upload and even invites it ('Click to choose a different file', UploadStep.tsx:38-47, 60). `run` sends `uploaded_file: filename` with the mapping built from `preview.columns` of the current `file` (AuditWizard.tsx:59-71), and canNext only checks `!uploading && filename !== null` (line 79). Failure scenario: User drops big_wrong.csv, immediately realises it is the wrong file and drops loans.csv. The preview and Map step now show loans.csv's columns. (a) big_wrong.csv finishes first: filename='big_wrong.csv' and uploading=false while loans.csv is still uploading, so Next is enabled and Run audit submits uploaded_file=big_wrong.csv with loans.csv's treatment/outcome columns. (b) loans.csv finishes first and big_wrong.csv later: filename is overwritten back to big_wrong.csv. Either way the signed audit is for a dataset other than the one shown on the Upload step, or the job fails on missing columns. A late failure of the abandoned upload also sets uploadError and blocks Next for the good file.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** PR #655. Each pick is tagged and only the latest may set the uploaded name; the previous name is cleared on a new pick. Regression: 2 tests in `audit/__tests__/AuditWizard.test.tsx` (the late-landing case fails on the old component).

## BUG-326: Terminal QueryPanel: Enter is not guarded by busy, so a query is submitted twice and a slower earlier response overwrites the newer result
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `frontend/src` (3 lenses, 2 adversarial verifiers per finding -- code, and registry/reachability -- both had to confirm), 2026-10-02.
- **Severity:** medium
- **Root cause:** `frontend/src/terminal/panels/QueryPanel.tsx` ~48: `run` (QueryPanel.tsx:14-29) checks only the prompt and active dataset, never `busy`. The Run button is disabled while busy (line 50), but the input's onKeyDown calls run() on every Enter (line 48). Each call awaits chatService.sendMessage and then does setResult(res) with no request id, and the first call to finish sets busy=false while the other is still in flight. Failure scenario: In /app/terminal the user types a question and presses Enter, sees nothing yet (LLM SQL generation takes seconds), and presses Enter again, or edits the question and presses Enter. Two POST /chat requests run (double LLM/query cost, counts twice against the rate limit). The header flips from 'running...' back to the dataset name when the first one returns although one is still pending, and whichever response lands last replaces the table/SQL; if the first question was the slower one, the panel ends up showing the old question's SQL and rows under the new question text. Verifiers' notes: QueryPanel.tsx:14-29 `run` has no `if (busy) return` guard and no request sequencing or abort; line 48's onKeyDown invokes it on every Enter while only the button (line 50) is disabled during busy. Concurrent runs each call setResult/setError/setBusy(false) unconditionally, so the last-settling response wins and busy clears when the first one settles.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** PR #659. The panel guards its run with an in-flight ref (Enter and Run alike) and tags each request so only the latest response is shown. Regression: a test in `terminal/__tests__/QueryPanel.test.tsx` (fails on the old panel).

## BUG-327: SchedulerPanel caches a job's run history (and a failed load) forever: 'Show runs' never refetches, and Refresh does not clear it
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `frontend/src` (3 lenses, 2 adversarial verifiers per finding -- code, and registry/reachability -- both had to confirm), 2026-10-02.
- **Severity:** low
- **Root cause:** `frontend/src/workbench/panels/SchedulerPanel.tsx` ~60: toggleOpen only fetches when `!runs[id] && !runsError[id]` (SchedulerPanel.tsx:57-68). `runs` and `runsError` are only ever added to; `load()` (lines 45-53, used by Refresh and after Pause/Resume) reloads the job list but never invalidates them, and nothing else resets either map while the panel is mounted. Failure scenario: User opens Scheduler and clicks Show runs on an hourly job, then leaves the panel open. After the next scheduled run the user clicks Refresh: the row's 'last ...' timestamp updates, but hiding and re-showing runs still lists the old history without the new run (for example a new failure is not shown). Separately, if the first listRuns call fails once (transient 5xx or network blip), 'Could not load run history for this job.' is shown for that job on every later Show runs click and after every Refresh; the only way to retry is to leave the panel and come back.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** PR #661. The run history is fetched every time a job is opened, and Refresh also reloads the open job's runs. Regression: 2 tests in `SchedulerPanel.test.tsx` (both fail on the old panel).

## BUG-328: Terminal Audit panel signs an irreversible AS 1215 override on a single Approve/Reject click, with no confirmation and no rationale guard
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `frontend/src` (3 lenses, 2 adversarial verifiers per finding -- code, and registry/reachability -- both had to confirm), 2026-10-02.
- **Severity:** high
- **Root cause:** `frontend/src/terminal/panels/AuditPanel.tsx` ~187: The Approve (line 187) and Reject (line 194) buttons call decide() directly and are disabled only on a.busy. There is no arm-then-confirm step like the one BUG-143/BUG-253 added to the healing surfaces (BUG-253 fixed HealingQueuePanel, terminal PipelinePanel and SchedulerPanel only; this panel was not covered). Unlike components/HITL/ExceptionQueue.tsx (buttons disabled on !rationale.trim()), the note is not required client-side, although the backend demands it (counterfactual_service/main.py:907 `rationale: str = Field(..., min_length=1)` plus a blank check returning 422). The decision is final: a second decision on the same finding returns 409 AlreadyDecidedError. In addition, decide() at AuditPanel.tsx:53-59 runs setNote('') and setSelected(null) unconditionally, because useAuditDeck.decide (terminal/audit/useAuditDeck.ts:80-108) catches its own errors and never rethrows. Failure scenario: An auditor in /app/terminal runs the audit, selects a Critical finding, types a rationale and mis-clicks Reject instead of the adjacent Approve. A signed HumanOverrideRecord plus a WORM audit_human_override entry is written under their identity immediately. The finding leaves the list and cannot be re-decided (409). Second path: the auditor clicks Approve with the rationale box empty; the backend returns 422, and the panel still clears the selection and note, so the inspector closes as if the decision had been taken and only the deck error line shows the failure.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** PR #656. A decision needs a written rationale and two clicks (arm, then confirm). Regression: 2 tests in `terminal/audit/__tests__/renderContract.test.tsx` (both fail on the old panel).

## BUG-329: GET /dashboard/stats reports every tenant's combined query count and row total
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/api_gateway/routers` (3 lenses, 2 adversarial verifiers per finding, both had to confirm), 2026-10-03.
- **Severity:** medium
- **Root cause:** `api_gateway/routers/queries.py` ~1092: `_dashboard_counters` (queries.py:53) is one dictionary for the whole process with no tenant key. `track_query` (lines 201-204) adds to `queries_run` and `total_rows` for every chat query from any workspace, even though it receives `workspace_id`. `get_dashboard_stats` reads these global numbers (lines 1092-1094) and returns them as the caller's `queries_run`, and adds them into `total_rows` (lines 1097/1101). The response is cached under the caller's workspace key (`dashboard:stats:{workspace}`), and the endpoint's docstring says it is scoped to the caller's workspace. Every other figure in the response (files, connections) is workspace-filtered. Failure scenario: Tenant A runs 500 chat queries returning 2,000,000 rows in total. Tenant B is new: no uploads and no queries. B calls GET /api/v1/dashboard/stats and gets queries_run=500 and total_rows=2000000. That is A's activity reported as B's own, and B can watch A's usage over time by polling. Verifiers' notes: The root cause is as claimed. `_dashboard_counters` (queries.py:53) is one in-memory dict with no tenant key. `track_query` (queries.py:201-204, reached from chat.py:678 through `_track_query`) increments it for every workspace, and `get_dashboard_stats` (queries.py:1092-1101) returns those global numbers as the caller's `queries_run` and as part of `total_rows`, even though files and connections are workspace-filtered and the result is cached per workspace. The root cause in the claim is correct. `_dashboard_counters` (queries.py:53) is one counter for the whole process. `track_query` (queries.py:201-204) adds every tenant's chat queries and rows to it, and GET /api/v1/dashboard/stats (queries.py:1092-1101) returns it as the caller's workspace-scoped `queries_run` and part of `total_rows`. BUG-039 tenanted gateway_query_history but deliberately left these counters global.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** PR #665. `_dashboard_counters` is keyed by workspace. Regression: `tests/test_dashboard_stats_tenant_counters.py`. Residual: the counters are in memory and reset on restart (unchanged).

## BUG-330: Scheduled saved queries in a named workspace load an empty, made-up tenant instead of the owner's data
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/api_gateway/routers` (3 lenses, 2 adversarial verifiers per finding, both had to confirm), 2026-10-03.
- **Severity:** medium
- **Root cause:** `api_gateway/routers/queries.py` ~909: `_fire_due_saved_queries` passes the saved query's `workspace_id` to `_execute_saved_query_sql`, which passes it as the storage tenant to `build_schema_context_cached(con, workspace_id, ...)` (line 834). For an authenticated caller in a non-default folder, `current_workspace_id` returns "<tenant>::<folder>". `tenant_slug("acme::reports")` becomes "acmereports-<hash>", which is not the bucket the files were uploaded to (`tenant_slug("acme")`). The tenant should be recovered with `tenant_from_workspace_id()`, as `inbound_hooks._fire_pipeline` does. The docstring (lines 820-826) says this was fixed by threading the owner through, but it threads the workspace key, not the tenant key. Failure scenario: A user in org `acme` switches to workspace folder `reports` (X-Workspace-Id: reports), saves `SELECT * FROM sales` and sets a daily schedule. When it comes due, the scheduler loads the tenant "acme::reports". Its storage bucket has no files, so no tables are loaded and the query fails with a table-not-found error. GET /saved-queries/{id}/runs shows every run as failed, while the same SQL works through POST /execute. The storage key is also derived from client-chosen text: a tenant whose org_id literally equals that slug would have its data loaded into the other org's scheduled run. Verifiers' notes: _execute_saved_query_sql (aurabackend/api_gateway/routers/queries.py:834) passes the saved query's workspace_id, which can be "<tenant>::<folder>", to build_schema_context_cached as the storage tenant. It should pass tenant_from_workspace_id(workspace_id), as inbound_hooks._fire_pipeline does. Scheduled queries saved in any non-default folder by an authenticated user therefore load the empty slug bucket "<tenant><folder>-<hash>" instead of the tenant's uploads. queries.py:909 passes the saved query's workspace key ("<tenant>::<folder>" for authenticated non-default folders) to _execute_saved_query_sql. Line 834 then uses it as the storage tenant in build_schema_context_cached, but uploads, /execute and dashboards all key storage by the bare org id from _request_tenant. The tenant should be recovered with workspaces.tenant_from_workspace_id(record["workspace_id"]), as inbound_hooks._fire_
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** PR #664. The storage tenant is recovered with `tenant_from_workspace_id`. Regression: `tests/test_saved_query_scheduler_fixes.py`.

## BUG-331: POST /pipeline/save accepts a caller-chosen id, and another tenant's id gives a 500 instead of a 404, so it can be used to check which pipeline ids exist
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/api_gateway/routers` (3 lenses, 2 adversarial verifiers per finding, both had to confirm), 2026-10-03.
- **Severity:** low
- **Root cause:** `api_gateway/routers/pipelines.py` ~288: `pipeline_save` copies `pipeline.id` straight from the request body (the Pipeline model only uses `pipe_<uuid8>` as a default). `persistence.save_pipeline` (persistence.py:1366-1390) looks for an existing row filtered by (id, workspace_id), finds none for another tenant's id, and INSERTs a new `PipelineRow`. `PipelineRow.id` alone is the primary key (persistence.py:366), so the insert hits a duplicate key and raises an unhandled IntegrityError. The id is never checked or rewritten when it belongs to another workspace. Failure scenario: Tenant B POSTs /api/v1/pipeline/save with {"id": "pipe_1a2b3c4d", ...}. If tenant A owns pipe_1a2b3c4d, B gets HTTP 500. If the id is unused, B gets 200 and has now taken that id. So B can learn which pipeline ids exist in other tenants, which the codebase's 404-not-403 rule is meant to prevent. B can also take an id first, so A's later save with that id fails with a 500. Verifiers' notes: pipeline_save passes the request's pipeline.id unchanged to persistence.save_pipeline. save_pipeline only checks for an existing row by (id, workspace_id) and then INSERTs. Because gateway_pipelines.id is a global primary key, a foreign tenant's id causes an IntegrityError at s.flush(), which nothing catches, so the response is a 500. metadata_store/repository.py:220-227 already fixes this pattern with a global session.get(...) and a fresh id; save_pipeline has no such check. The root cause is as described. pipeline_save (aurabackend/api_gateway/routers/pipelines.py:278-300) trusts the caller's pipeline.id. persistence.save_pipeline looks for an existing row by (id, workspace_id), but PipelineRow.id alone is the primary key. When the id belongs to another workspace, the INSERT raises IntegrityError, and nothing catches it, so the caller gets a 500. An unused id gives a 200. (Also reported by the correctness-contract lens as a 500 on an id another workspace owns.)
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** PR #666. When another workspace holds the id, this workspace's copy is stored under a fresh id returned as `pipeline_id`. Regression: a test in `tests/test_durable_tenant_stores.py` (fails on the old code with the IntegrityError).

## BUG-332: POST /execute/query reports every successful query as failed: the success-path ExecuteQueryResponse leaves out the required 'error' field
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/api_gateway/routers` (3 lenses, 2 adversarial verifiers per finding, both had to confirm), 2026-10-03.
- **Severity:** high
- **Root cause:** `api_gateway/routers/queries.py` ~481: ExecuteQueryResponse (queries.py:237-244) declares `error: Optional[str]` with no default. The repo pins pydantic>=2.7, and in Pydantic v2 an Optional field with no default is still required. The success return at lines 481-485 passes success, data, rows, columns, insights and execution_time_ms but never `error`, so building the model raises ValidationError inside the try. The generic `except Exception` at line 488 catches it and returns ExecuteQueryResponse(success=False, data=None, rows=0, error=sanitize_error(...)). (data and insights are also defaultless Optionals, but this call sets them.) The BUG-225 tests only cover the refusal paths, so no test reaches the success branch. Failure scenario: POST /api/v1/execute/query with {query:'SELECT 1', connector_type:'postgresql', connector_config:{host,port,database,username,password}} against a reachable database. The query runs and the AnalysisAgent LLM call runs, then the response is HTTP 200 {success:false, data:null, rows:0, columns:[], error:'<generic sanitized message>'}. The query actually worked, every external-DB query through this route looks like a failure, and the rows are thrown away. Verifiers' notes: As claimed. ExecuteQueryResponse.error is declared Optional[str] with no default, which Pydantic v2 treats as required. The success return in execute_query_with_insights omits `error`, so it raises ValidationError, and the route's `except Exception` turns that into success=False and throws away the results. This happens for every successful query, including ones that return no rows. Fix: give data, insights and error a default of None. Root cause confirmed as claimed. ExecuteQueryResponse declares `error: Optional[str]` with no default, which Pydantic v2 treats as required, and the success return in execute_query_with_insights leaves it out. The resulting ValidationError is caught by `except Exception` and returned as success=false.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** PR #663. `ExecuteQueryResponse`'s optional fields default to None. Regression: a test in `tests/test_execute_query_embedded_refused.py` (fails on the old model).

## BUG-333: PUT /saved-queries/{id}/schedule returns 500 for a weekly schedule without day_of_week, which the frontend type and the model allow
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/api_gateway/routers` (3 lenses, 2 adversarial verifiers per finding, both had to confirm), 2026-10-03.
- **Severity:** medium
- **Root cause:** `api_gateway/routers/queries.py` ~750: SavedQuerySchedule.day_of_week is Optional[int] with default None (line 726), and the frontend type is `day_of_week?: number | null` (frontend/src/services/api.ts:838). The handler builds `schedule = payload.dict()` (line 766), so the key is present with value None. In _compute_next_run's weekly branch, `int(schedule.get("day_of_week", 0))` gets None instead of the default, and int(None) raises TypeError. Nothing catches it, so the request is a 500 before anything is saved. The frontend already treats null as Monday (SchedulerPanel.tsx:31, `s.day_of_week ?? 0`), so the API contract really does allow null. Failure scenario: PUT /api/v1/saved-queries/sq_123/schedule with body {"interval":"weekly","hour":9,"minute":0}, or with day_of_week:null. The model accepts it, then _compute_next_run raises TypeError and the client gets HTTP 500 'Internal server error' instead of a schedule that runs on Monday or a 422 naming the missing field. The schedule is never saved. Verifiers' notes: Same as claimed. payload.dict() keeps day_of_week=None, and in _compute_next_run (queries.py:750) `schedule.get("day_of_week", 0)` returns None, not the default 0. `int(None)` then raises an uncaught TypeError, the request fails with a 500, and the schedule is never saved. A fix such as `int(schedule.get("day_of_week") or 0)` avoids it, as would requiring day_of_week in the model when interval is weekly.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** PR #664. A weekly schedule without `day_of_week` defaults to Monday. Regression: `tests/test_saved_query_scheduler_fixes.py`.

## BUG-334: POST /chat returns status 'Error' when only the chart or narrative step failed after SQL ran, so the frontend shows 'failed' and hides the result
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/api_gateway/routers` (3 lenses, 2 adversarial verifiers per finding, both had to confirm), 2026-10-03.
- **Severity:** medium
- **Root cause:** `api_gateway/routers/chat.py` ~643: In langgraph_orchestrator.py, visualization_node and analysis_node append a NodeError (via _err) when their LLM agent fails, and _route_after_visualization still runs analysis after a viz failure. The run therefore ends with state.execution set (a successful query) but a non-empty state.errors. chat_endpoint takes state.errors[0]. For any node other than sql_gen or execution, it sets error_message = f"{first.node} failed: ..." (chat.py:642-643), and the response becomes status="Error" (line 685) even though execution_result.success is True and holds the data. Both frontends compute `ok = resp.status !== 'Error' && er.success !== false` (AskAuraChat.tsx, AskAuraPanel.tsx:49). With ok false they drop the chart, show error_message, and omit the row count and 'executed' marker. A failure at the execution node is the opposite case: it sets only execution_result.error, so a failed query is labelled status 'Success'. Failure scenario: A user asks 'total sales by region'. SQL generation and DuckDB execution succeed and return 5 rows, then the AnalysisAgent LLM call returns a provider 429. state.errors=[NodeError(node='analysis')]. The response is {status:'Error', error_message:'analysis failed: The AI service is temporarily rate-limited...', execution_result:{success:true, data:[5 rows], chart_spec:{...}}}. The Ask AURA panel shows a failure message and no chart for a query that answered the question, and CHAT_REQUESTS records it as an error. Verifiers' notes: chat_endpoint (chat.py:633-643, 685) treats any NodeError from a node after execution as fatal. If AnalysisAgent's LLM call raises (for example a 429), analysis_node appends NodeError(node='analysis'), and the response becomes status='Error' with error_message='analysis failed: ...'. This happens even though execution_result.success=True and it carries the rows and the chart_spec. CHAT_REQUESTS also counts it as an error. How the frontends react: - AskAuraChat.tsx (gateway fallback, lines 75-92) computes ok=false, drops the chart and data, omits the 'executed · N rows' marker and shows the error as the answer. - AskAuraPanel.tsx still renders the data, chart and row count, but adds a failure error banner. A visualization failure is largely unreachable, because VisualizationAgent falls back to a heuristic spec when its LLM fails. The analysis node is the realistic trigger. chat_endpoint (
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** PR #667. `status` is "Success" whenever the query executed; `error_message` still names the step that failed. Regression: `tests/test_chat_partial_success.py` (fails on the old handler).

## BUG-335: Saved-query scheduler runs each due query inline with no timeout, so one stored query can stop every tenant's schedules
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/api_gateway/routers` (3 lenses, 2 adversarial verifiers per finding, both had to confirm), 2026-10-03.
- **Severity:** high
- **Root cause:** `api_gateway/routers/queries.py` ~908: _scheduler_loop (line 948) awaits _fire_due_saved_queries, which runs every due query across all workspaces one after another: `result = await _execute_saved_query_sql(...)` at line 908, which does `await asyncio.to_thread(_run)` at line 841. Neither call has asyncio.wait_for or a conn.interrupt() deadline. next_run_at is moved forward only after the run returns (lines 933-941). BUG-288 gave PipelineEngine a 300 s interrupt, but this scheduler path got none. BUG-228 notes that lock_down_connection does not block generator table functions such as range(). stop_saved_query_scheduler (line 972) gives up after 5 s and cancels the task, but the worker thread keeps running. Failure scenario: Any tenant saves a query such as `SELECT sum(a.range*b.range) FROM range(100000000) a, range(1000000) b` and sets a schedule on it through PUT /saved-queries/{id}/schedule. On the next 30 s tick the scheduler awaits that DuckDB statement, which runs for hours. It also holds one default-executor thread the whole time. Until the process restarts, no other workspace's scheduled query runs, nothing is written to their run history, and no error is shown anywhere. Verifiers' notes: As claimed. `_fire_due_saved_queries` runs every tenant's due saved queries one after another inside the single scheduler task, via `await _execute_saved_query_sql` and then `asyncio.to_thread(con.execute(sql))`, with no `wait_for` or `interrupt` deadline and no per-run isolation. The locked-down DuckDB connection sets no timeout and still allows generator functions such as `range()`, so one stored long-running query stalls all schedules indefinitely. `next_run_at` only moves forward after the run finishes. Same as the claim, with small line corrections. The await of _execute_saved_query_sql is at queries.py:909, not 908. stop_saved_query_scheduler is defined at line 966, and its 5 s wait_for is at line 972. _fire_due_saved_queries runs each due query in turn, across all workspaces, inside the single scheduler task. _execute_saved_query_sql runs asyncio.to_thread(_run) at line 841 with n
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** PR #664. Each scheduled run is stopped after 120 s with `conn.interrupt()` and recorded as failed. Regression: `tests/test_saved_query_scheduler_fixes.py` (the runaway query hung on the old code).

## BUG-336: Chat audit intent loads a whole column with no row cap, then builds and hashes 2xN dicts on the event loop
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/api_gateway/routers` (3 lenses, 2 adversarial verifiers per finding, both had to confirm), 2026-10-03.
- **Severity:** high
- **Root cause:** `api_gateway/routers/chat.py` ~557: In the intent=="audit" branch, _read_amounts runs `SELECT <amount_col> FROM <table>` and calls cur.fetchall() with no cap (lines 519-523). Then, directly in the coroutine and not in a thread, it builds one dict per row (`entries`, lines 542-545), calls dataset_fingerprint(entries, [], [], entries) at line 557, which runs canonical_dumps over every entry twice and hashes the result with SHA-256, and calls build_completion_document at line 558. The /audit/financial path was fixed for exactly this (MAX_AUDIT_ROWS=50,000, with dataset_fingerprint and build_completion_document moved to asyncio.to_thread), but this call site in chat was left out. BUG-238/237 offloaded only storage.list and sign_and_persist here. Failure scenario: A tenant uploads a parquet file of 5,000,000 transactions and sends POST /chat {"message": "audit the transactions for fraud"}. The fetch copies 5M floats into Python. The handler then creates 5M dicts on the event loop and canonical-JSON-serialises 10M of them (a JSON string of several hundred MB) before hashing it. The single uvicorn worker is blocked for many seconds and uses GBs of RAM, so every other tenant's requests and SSE heartbeats stall, or the process is OOM-killed. Verifiers' notes: In the chat audit intent (chat.py ~517-557), _read_amounts reads the whole amount column with fetchall() and no row cap, unlike MAX_AUDIT_ROWS on /audit/financial and AURA_QUERY_MAX_ROWS on /execute. The handler then builds N `entries` dicts and calls dataset_fingerprint(entries, [], [], entries) synchronously on the event loop. That canonical-JSON-serialises 2N dicts and SHA-256 hashes the result. build_completion_document only processes at most 4 findings and is not a material cost. The 25MB upload cap does not bound row count, because compressed parquet or short-value CSV can hold millions of rows. chat.py audit intent (lines 518-557): the column is fetched with no row cap. Then, on the event loop, the handler builds one dict per row and calls dataset_fingerprint(entries, [], [], entries), which canonical-JSON-serialises the full list twice and hashes it. That blocks the single worker
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** The chat audit intent reads at most `CHAT_AUDIT_MAX_ROWS` (100,000) values with a `LIMIT`, says so in its answer when the table is larger, and runs `dataset_fingerprint` and `build_completion_document` through `asyncio.to_thread`. Regression: 2 tests in `tests/test_chat_audit_ledger.py` (the cap case fails on the old handler).

## BUG-337: /files list, info, profile and delete routes call the storage backend's blocking list()/delete() on the event loop
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/api_gateway/routers` (3 lenses, 2 adversarial verifiers per finding, both had to confirm), 2026-10-03.
- **Severity:** medium
- **Root cause:** `api_gateway/routers/files.py` ~319: list_files (line 319), get_file_info (line 334), get_file_profile (line 364) and delete_file (line 396) call file_service.list_files/get_file_info/delete_file synchronously inside async handlers. Each of these (shared/file_service.py:96, 134, 149-151) calls get_storage_backend().list(tenant), and delete_file also calls backend.delete(...). Under AURA_STORAGE_BACKEND=s3 those are blocking boto3 paginator and DeleteObject network round trips; locally they are iterdir+stat. BUG-238 (chat.py), BUG-177 (data_utils) and pipelines.py:119 already wrap this same backend.list call in asyncio.to_thread, but these four routes were missed. Failure scenario: On an S3-backed deployment, a tenant with a few thousand uploads opens the Files panel, which sends GET /files, or GET /files/{id}/profile, which lists all objects only to check ownership. Each request runs a multi-page ListObjectsV2 loop on the event loop, and S3 latency or a slow endpoint adds seconds per call. While it runs, the single worker serves nothing else: other tenants' requests queue and SSE streams miss heartbeats. DELETE /files/{id} adds a blocking DeleteObject on top. Verifiers' notes: Root cause as claimed. One refinement: get_file_info returns as soon as it finds a match, but the backend.list() paginator is still synchronous and may fetch several pages first, so every one of the four routes can block the event loop.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** GET /files, GET /files/{id}, the ownership check in GET /files/{id}/profile and DELETE /files/{id} call `file_service` through `asyncio.to_thread`. Regression: `tests/test_files_routes_off_event_loop.py` (asserts no event loop is running in the calling thread; fails on the old routes).

## BUG-338: /synthetic/generate runs the long job on the process's shared default executor, which can starve every asyncio.to_thread call
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/api_gateway/routers` (3 lenses, 2 adversarial verifiers per finding, both had to confirm), 2026-10-03.
- **Severity:** medium
- **Root cause:** `api_gateway/routers/synthetic.py` ~242: synthetic_generate starts _run_job with `asyncio.get_event_loop().run_in_executor(None, _run_job, job_id, req)`. That is the loop's default ThreadPoolExecutor (min(32, cpu+4) workers), the same pool that every asyncio.to_thread call in the gateway uses: bcrypt login, DuckDB /execute, storage writes, hook registry saves. A job may write up to AURA_SYNTHETIC_MAX_BYTES (1 GiB) of parquet, holds its thread for the whole run and cannot be cancelled. BUG-227 left per-tenant concurrency unlimited. BUG-061 fixed exactly this pattern in chat.py by giving the commander loop its own executor, but synthetic.py still passes None. Failure scenario: On a 2-vCPU container the default pool has 6 threads. A user (or a script) sends six POST /synthetic/generate requests with target_size="1GB". All six default-pool threads are busy writing parquet for minutes. During that time every other request that uses asyncio.to_thread waits in the executor queue: POST /auth/token (bcrypt verify), POST /execute, /upload storage writes, inbound-hook fires. Logins and queries hang for every tenant until a generate job finishes. Verifiers' notes: synthetic.py:242 starts `_run_job` on the loop's default executor (`run_in_executor(None, ...)`). No `set_default_executor` exists anywhere in aurabackend, so that pool is shared with every `asyncio.to_thread` call in the gateway: auth bcrypt, persistence, connections, ETL, dashboards and chat helpers. Each job holds a thread for its whole run, writes up to `AURA_SYNTHETIC_MAX_BYTES` (1 GiB by default), cannot be cancelled, and nothing limits how many run at once (BUG-227 left concurrency open). Enough concurrent generate calls fill the pool and stall every other `to_thread` caller. This is the same pattern BUG-061 fixed in chat.py with a dedicated executor. synthetic.py:242 hands _run_job to the loop's shared default ThreadPoolExecutor via run_in_executor(None, ...). That pool has min(32, cpu_count+4) threads and is the same one that auth.py's bcrypt calls and every other asyncio.to_thr
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** `/synthetic/generate` submits jobs to the router's own `ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=2)` (`_GENERATION_POOL`) instead of the event loop's default executor; further jobs queue there. Regression: a test in `tests/test_synthetic_generate_bounds.py` (the job ran on `asyncio_0`, the shared pool, on the old code).

## BUG-339: BigQuery connections block the event loop: connection schema/test/query routes call the connector's synchronous client in async handlers
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode audit of `aurabackend/api_gateway/routers` (3 lenses, 2 adversarial verifiers per finding, both had to confirm), 2026-10-03.
- **Severity:** medium
- **Root cause:** `api_gateway/routers/connections.py` ~470: get_connection_schema awaits connector.list_tables() and then, for up to 50 tables, connector.profile_table(t) (lines 465-470). The same pattern is in test_connector/test_connection_by_id (258-261, 417-419) and in POST /execute/query (queries.py:461-462). BigQueryConnector's methods are declared `async`, but their bodies make blocking google-cloud-bigquery calls with no await or thread: self.client.list_tables(...), self.client.get_table(...), and self.client.query(...).result() for both the sample and the COUNT(*) (connectors/bigquery_connector.py:84, 101, 133, 153, 176). Every query job is a blocking HTTP round trip, plus a wait for the job to finish, on the single uvicorn worker. google-cloud-bigquery is in the base requirements.txt, and saved BigQuery connections have worked since BUG-170 and BUG-201. Failure scenario: A tenant with a saved BigQuery connection to a dataset of 50 tables opens its schema (GET /connections/{id}/schema). The handler runs about 1 list call plus 50 x (get_table + a SELECT ... LIMIT 1000 job + a COUNT(*) job) one after another on the event loop. At roughly 1-2 s per BigQuery job, the gateway serves no other request for one to three minutes. In the same way, POST /execute/query with connector_type=bigquery and a slow query freezes the whole gateway until BigQuery returns. Verifiers' notes: The root cause is as claimed. BigQueryConnector's async methods (connectors/bigquery_connector.py: list_tables, get_table_schema, sample_rows, execute_query, profile_table) call the synchronous google-cloud-bigquery client (list_tables, get_table, query().result()) with no asyncio.to_thread. These routes await them directly: GET /connections/{id}/schema (up to 50 profile_table calls in a row), POST /connectors/{type}/test, the test-by-id route, and POST /execute/query for bigquery. Each call blocks the single uvicorn worker's event loop. BUG-042 and the later blocking-call bugs never covered this site.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** Every call to the synchronous google-cloud-bigquery client in `connectors/bigquery_connector.py` (list_tables, get_table, query + result) runs through `asyncio.to_thread`. Regression: `tests/test_bigquery_off_event_loop.py` (a stand-in client records whether each call ran on the event loop; no BigQuery reachable from the test lane).

## BUG-340: Production stopped redeploying: the box stayed on build 982b679 while main moved on
- **Status:** open
- **Found by:** live verification, 2026-10-03. `/health` reported `build: 982b679` (PR #628, merged ~15:00 UTC 2026-10-02) for 15+ hours while the CD workflow kept publishing images (latest successful CD run on `05379a2`) and about 30 PRs merged.
- **Severity:** high -- every fix merged since #628 (the counterfactual, `shared/` and frontend audits, including the live-stream fix BUG-318) is not running in production.
- **Root cause:** unconfirmed. The box redeploys by a 15-minute cron running `deploy/aws-free-tier/redeploy.sh` (git checkout + `docker compose pull` + `up -d`); that has stopped taking effect. Not diagnosed: SSH to the box timed out on port 22 and the local AWS CLI session had expired, so the redeploy log, crontab and disk could not be read. Candidates: cron/crond not running, a failing `git fetch`/`checkout`, a failing image pull (disk or registry auth), or the health wait timing out.
- **Caused by:** unknown.
- **Fix:** pending -- needs access to the box (user to re-authenticate the AWS CLI or check it directly); redeploying is a production action that needs the user's go-ahead.

## BUG-341: redeploy.sh has no time limits, never prunes images, and runs a splice of itself when a deploy changes it
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** investigating BUG-340 (production frozen on 982b679), 2026-10-05.
- **Severity:** high -- each one can stop production from taking new builds while CI keeps publishing them.
- **Root cause:** `deploy/aws-free-tier/redeploy.sh`. (1) `git fetch`, `docker compose pull` and `docker compose up -d` had no timeout, and the cron entry runs the script under `flock -n`: one run that hangs keeps the lock and every later run skips silently. (2) Nothing removed superseded images, so every deploy left another full set (the causal image alone is GBs) until a pull fails for lack of disk and `set -e` stops before `up -d`. (3) `git checkout` rewrites the running script; bash reads a script by byte offset, so a deploy that changes `redeploy.sh` executes a splice of old and new text. Reproduced with shimmed git/docker: the old script printed `ODE-RAN: command not found` after the checkout and exited before pulling.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing. (1) and (2) are the leading candidates for BUG-340; (3) only fires on a deploy that changes the script itself.
- **Fix:** the script re-executes from a private copy, every network step runs under `timeout` (fetch 120 s, pull 1200 s, up 300 s), and images no container uses (older than a day) are pruned before each pull, with free disk logged. Verified with a shim run: the copy survived the checkout rewriting the original, and the timed steps and prune ran in order. Takes effect on the box only once a redeploy runs this version.

## BUG-342: index.html is served with no Cache-Control and un-hashed static files are cached as immutable for 30 days
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** checking whether old frontend code can outlive a deploy, 2026-10-05. Live: `GET /` returns an ETag and `Last-Modified: Fri, 02 Oct 2026 02:44:23 GMT` and no `Cache-Control`.
- **Severity:** medium
- **Root cause:** `frontend/nginx.conf` set no caching policy for `index.html`, so browsers apply heuristic freshness and can keep an old page -- which references the previous build's bundle -- for hours or days after a deploy. Its static-asset rule marked every `.js/.css/.png/.svg/...` `public, immutable` for 30 days, including un-hashed files from `public/` (favicon, logos), which then never update.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** `index.html` is served `Cache-Control: no-cache` (revalidated each load); only Vite's content-hashed `/assets/` are immutable (1 year, `^~` so the regex rule cannot take them); other static files get `max-age=3600`. Not exercised against a running nginx (no Docker on the dev machine); CI's frontend image build and the live probe after deploy confirm it.


## BUG-343: Shim AST gate bypass: dunder string built by concatenation and fed to str.format reads the real logging/os/sys modules, leaking service secrets and other tenants' state
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode UASR audit (run wf_6234d56e-d9c, isolation-sandbox lens; confirmed by 2 adversarial verifiers), 2026-10-07. `aurabackend/uasr/recovery_loop.py:114`.
- **Severity:** high
- **Root cause:** _validate_shim_source (recovery_loop.py:97-118) rejects `__` only inside each individual string Constant (lines 114-117) and dunder Attribute/Name nodes. A format string assembled at runtime from pieces that individually contain no `__` ("{0._" + "_globals_" + "_[logging].os.environ}") passes every check, and `.format` is not a forbidden attribute. str.format then does the dunder attribute walk itself. The stand-in `logging.getLogger` that the sandbox hands every shim (_shim_logging, lines 130-147; injected at line 576) is `get_logger`, a function defined in recovery_loop.py, so its __globals__ is that module's namespace, which holds the real `logging` module (imported at line 18). From there `.os.environ` and `.sys.modules[...]` are reachable as attribute/index lookups inside the format field. The exec at line 583 and every apply_shims_counted call (line 919) run this code. BUG-258 added the AST gate; this bypass of it is not registered.
- **Failure scenario:** An authenticated tenant user posts /uasr/heal with column names that prompt-inject the LLM shim path (actuator_agent._llm_shim only checks for `"def transform" in code`). The LLM returns `def transform(rows): s = ("{0._" + "_globals_" + "_[logging].os.environ}").format(logging.getLogger); rows[0]["note"] = s; return rows`. _validate_shim_source accepts it. With the default risk_tiered=False the shim auto-deploys, or under risk_tiered the same tenant approves its own held shim. The next /uasr/heal response returns rows containing str(os.environ): the JWT signing secret, LLM API keys and DB DSN. The same route with `[logging].sys.modules[uasr.service]._loop._deployed_shims` returns every tenant's deployed shim code as a string, which bypasses the BUG-262..264 tenant scoping.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** the gate now also forbids the attribute names `format` and `format_map`, the only builtin path that walks attributes named in a runtime string, so `"...".format(...)`, `str.format(...)` and `.format_map(...)` are rejected before the shim runs whatever literal pieces they are built from. No shipped shim template uses `.format`. Regression test `test_a_format_string_assembled_at_runtime_cannot_reach_the_environment` (3 cases) read the environment through the stand-in logger's `__globals__` on the old gate and is rejected now.

## BUG-344: Standing shims are applied twice to a batch whenever a new shim deploys (/uasr/heal and the MAPE-K pause path)
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode UASR audit (run wf_6234d56e-d9c, correctness lens; confirmed by 2 adversarial verifiers), 2026-10-07. `aurabackend/uasr/service.py:794`.
- **Severity:** high
- **Root cause:** heal_batch first replaces batch.rows with the output of every standing shim (service.py:718, `batch.rows, standing_applied, _ = _loop.apply_shims_counted(...)`). After a new shim deploys it calls apply_shims_counted again on those same rows (service.py:794), and that call runs the WHOLE chain, standing shims included. The Kafka worker does the same thing: it applies shims at mapek_worker.py:479, then runs `self._loop.apply_shims(batch.source_id, batch.rows)` on the already-shimmed rows at mapek_worker.py:618 (and at 631 on the cross-source path). The new shim was validated against the already-shimmed rows (recovery_loop.py:265/459), so only the new shim should run at that point. The actuator's rescale template is `row[col] = row[col] / factor` (actuator_agent.py:292), which is not idempotent.
- **Failure scenario:** Source S has a deployed rescale shim A (price / 100, cents to dollars). A later batch shows new drift on qty (x1000). Standing A is applied, the drift is detected, and shim B is validated and auto-deployed. Line 794 (or mapek 618) then applies [A, B] to rows that A already transformed, so price is divided by 100 twice. /uasr/heal returns these rows with healed:true. On Kafka, the corrupted batch is written to DuckDB and, because batch_healed=True, re-registered as the detector baseline (mapek_worker.py:622-623). Correct later batches then drift against a baseline that is 100x too small.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** `RecoveryLoop.apply_new_shim` applies only the just-deployed shim (validated against the already-shimmed rows) and is what `/uasr/heal` and both MAPE-K pause-path branches (own recovery and cross-source borrow) now call, instead of re-running the whole chain. Regression tests `test_a_newly_deployed_shim_does_not_re_run_the_standing_ones` (heal) and `test_mapek_new_shim_applied_once.py` (worker) produced 101 instead of 11 on the old code.

## BUG-345: With UASR_USE_SHIM_ROUTER on, the Kafka path ignores every shim restored at startup or approved by a human
- **Status:** open
- **Found by:** ultracode UASR audit (run wf_6234d56e-d9c, correctness lens; confirmed by 2 adversarial verifiers), 2026-10-07. `aurabackend/uasr/mapek_worker.py:460`.
- **Severity:** high
- **Root cause:** When _shim_router is set, the MAPE-K loop transforms batches only through ShimRouter.apply (mapek_worker.py:460-477) and never calls _loop.apply_shims. ShimRouter is a fresh in-memory object per worker (mapek_worker.py:261). Its routes are added only at mapek_worker.py:563/584, from recoveries this worker deployed itself in this process. Startup hydration (service.py:318, hydrate_deployed_shims) and /approve (service.py:1094, deploy_approved_shim) both write only to RecoveryLoop._deployed_shims, which the router branch never reads.
- **Failure scenario:** In router mode a canary shim heals source S, and its RecoveryRecord is DEPLOYED. After the container restarts, the lifespan logs 'UASR restored 1 deployed shim(s)', but ShimRouter is empty. apply() returns '_passthrough' (shim_router.py:237-239), so raw drifted rows flow through, drift re-fires, and a new recovery runs on every drifted batch. In the same way, a PENDING_APPROVAL recovery that an operator approves is marked DEPLOYED in the DB, yet Kafka batches for that source are never transformed by it.
- **Caused by:** none -- pre-existing.
- **Fix:** pending.

## BUG-346: Distributed repair: a waiter that is cancelled, errors, or whose node dies leaves its token in the Redis wait queue forever, so later repairs across the fleet stop being admitted
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** ultracode UASR audit (run wf_6234d56e-d9c, reliability lens; confirmed by 2 adversarial verifiers), 2026-10-07. `aurabackend/uasr/distributed_repair.py:324`.
- **Severity:** high
- **Root cause:** submit() calls _enqueue(), which ZADDs the token to `{ns}:waitq`, then loops `while not await self._try_admit(token): await asyncio.sleep(self._poll)` (lines 324-328). Nothing removes the token if the wait ends without admission: there is no try/finally ZREM, and no task cancellation or Redis error is handled. Wait-queue entries also have no TTL and are never pruned; only active leases expire (_prune_expired_locked only scans `{ns}:active`). _try_admit (lines 274-285) walks the queue in priority order and returns False as soon as the first eligible entry is not its own token. A dead token at the head therefore makes every later _try_admit with equal or lower priority return False, forever.
- **Failure scenario:** docker-compose.yml and docker-compose.prod.yml both run UASR_REPAIR_BACKEND=distributed against a shared Redis. (1) Kafka drift fires while all 8 global slots are busy, so a MEDIUM repair waits in submit(). (2) The service is restarted (a deploy, or uvicorn --reload in dev). The lifespan's MAPEKWorker.stop() runs wait_for(task, 15s), which cancels _run_loop while it is still inside submit's wait loop, so the token stays in waitq. A SIGKILLed node, or one Redis ConnectionError raised out of _try_admit, has the same effect. (3) The other repairs finish and the slots free up. Every later HIGH/MEDIUM/LOW repair on every node still sees the orphan as the head candidate, so `cand != token` returns False on every 25 ms poll.
